- Summary: a new outbound purpose `AmanFeed` (script 0032; registry, step-up, sites, secret protection, SSRF-safe transport and resilience of ARV-045 unchanged): `TotpClientCredentials` with `X-TOTP-Code` on every call, one site, a feed path ending in a slash (checked by `OutboundRules` and by the database). `AmanPoller` (Ariva.Api.Integration, every 15 s, `Integration:Outbound:PollAman`) claims due endpoints (one replica each; a failing endpoint waits up to ten intervals), and for each of the four contracts asks `GET {pullPath}{contract}?after={position}&limit=500`, reads AMAN's envelope strictly (`AmanFeedPages`: items as strictly as the Kafka values, unreadable ones counted and never echoed, a page refused when AMAN reports errors, holds more than 1,000 records or its position does not move forward), up to 20 pages a poll, and applies each page through the immigration intake for its one site (`ImmigrationScope.ForPull`, feed `aman-` and the endpoint's code) with the forward-only position per contract (`aman_pull_cursor`) in one unit of work; the status records counts only. `TokenHandler` now tries at most one TOTP exchange per step (AMAN's replay guard and lockout).
- Decisions: recorded in decisions.md; AMAN's paging and envelope follow the simulator's mock AMAN (To confirm against AMAN's Integration API).
- Gates: unit tests (page reading and refusals without echo, the AmanFeed registration rules, the pull scope, the step guard); integration tests (a stand-in AMAN: one token for four contracts and the next poll, a valid `X-TOTP-Code` on every call, an unreadable record and another site's record refused, the position saved and continued, a wrong secret recorded and backed off, runtime role refused on `aman_pull_cursor` and on the replaced checks); e2e `aman-pull.spec.ts` against the simulator's mock AMAN with its own client (registration refusals, contracts pulled with positions moving, a wrong secret recorded, no secret in any answer or host log). Backend gate passed (unit 1979), integration 295 passed (1 pre-existing skip), e2e 358 passed, scan 0 errors, docs pass.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-287, CWE-918 and the baseline): FAIL on the first pass (no log canary for the new credentials, no test of another site's record, no runtime-role test for the new table and checks), fixed; advisories taken: forward-only position, a catch-all status, per-request TOTP required, back-off for failing endpoints, the feed path checked in the database. Re-review PASS.

## 2026-10-03 Fix: the border integration tests no longer depend on their order
- The CI runs of ARV-049 and the mix fix failed in two arrival-wave integration tests: every test of `ImmigrationIntakeTests` starts its clock at the same instant in one shared database, so when the two-sites test ran first its flight (and its XS2 lane demand) fell in the other tests' windows. Locally the order hid it. Each arrival-wave and desk-feed test now advances its clock to a day of its own; the Border tests pass alone, together and in the subsets that failed.

## 2026-10-03 ARV-051 Web sign-in, first-login and the route groups
- Summary: Ariva.Web now signs in. Ariva.Api.Main is reached on the web origin (the ingress already routes `/api` and `/hubs` on the web host; `vite dev` and `vite preview` proxy them, `ARIVA_WEB_API_PROXY` overrides), so the SameSite=Strict refresh cookie and the X-Ariva-Csrf check need no CORS credentials and `connect-src` loses the main API origin. `auth.svelte.ts` keeps the access token in memory, refreshes 60 s before expiry under the Web Locks API, shares the token and sign-out over a BroadcastChannel (another account's token makes the tab reload the user), keeps a non-secret `ariva-signed-in` flag so a signed-out visit sends no refresh, treats only a 401 refresh as an ended session, and signs out reliably (refreshes and retries once when logout answers 401; tells the user when the server could not end the session). `Api.ts` adds the token only to Main's resolved origin, refuses non-relative paths, opens the step-up dialog on 401 `mfa_required` (RFC 9470) and resends once, refreshes once on other 401s, and returns to `/login?next=` on `session_expired`. New `GET /api/auth/me` (pending allowed, no-store): roles, permissions, sites, first-login needs. Routes: `(public)/login` (code or recovery code on `mfa_required`; `next` limited to paths on this origin), `(public)/setup` (temporary password change, TOTP enrolment from a QR code drawn as an SVG path, recovery codes once with a copy action), `(modules)` guard with the shell, `(modules)/account` (password change, new recovery codes as the critical action). The sidebar filters by permission and has a user card with Account security and Sign out; the error page stands outside the shell. English and Arabic strings.
- Decisions: recorded in decisions.md (same origin, memory token with Web Locks and BroadcastChannel, the hint flag, /me as the web's view of permissions, enrolment optional only where Auth:TotpRequired is off, the account screen).
- Gates: integration test for `CurrentAsync` (full, pending, no caller); e2e `auth-me.spec.ts` (fields, permissions per role, pending, no secrets, 401 for none, tampered or ended); functional `auth.spec.ts` (return to the remembered page and no token in storage, one message for every refusal, TOTP sign-in in Arabic, `next` off-site attempts, XSS on the form, new tab from the cookie and shared sign-out, two tabs at once, sign-out with an expired token ending the server session, a failed sign-out reported, session ended on the server, first sign-in end to end with QR, manual key, recovery codes and clipboard, pending account kept in setup, step-up with a 20-minute-old factor and a wrong code first, cancelled step-up, sidebar per role with the server refusing the hidden screen); shell, layout, accessibility (sign-in and account screens added) and XSS specs now sign in first. Backend gate passed (unit 1979), integration 296 passed (1 pre-existing skip), e2e 386 passed, web gate passed, scan 0 errors, docs pass.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-384, CWE-79, CWE-287 and the baseline): FAIL on the first pass (sign-out with an expired access token left the server session alive; the token was attached by base string rather than resolved origin; a token from another tab's account kept the old user), fixed; second pass FAIL (any refused refresh counted as an ended session, so a 429 or 503 during sign-out looked clean), fixed with tests; advisories taken: control characters refused in API paths, refresh retried after a network error, clipboard advice. Re-review PASS; open advisory: no back-off when refresh keeps answering 403.

## 2026-10-04 Fix: ending the Redis connection at shutdown never throws
- One integration run of ARV-058 failed in `FusionCacheBackplaneTests` with a NullReferenceException from inside StackExchange.Redis 2.10's own close (`ConnectionMultiplexer.AllComplete`, during `DisposeAsync`), apparently a race with a reconnect in flight; the test passed six times alone. `RedisConnection.DisposeAsync` now tolerates exactly that exception and a first connect that failed, and lets anything else through, so a host's shutdown is not turned into an error by the library. Unit tests for the two tolerated cases, a third failure still thrown, and a never-connected connection; the caching integration tests pass.

## 2026-10-04 Fix: no literal invisible characters in source
- ARV-059's tests first carried a literal right-to-left override in a C# string, and nine older test files did too (device, alert and password tests, unit and E2E): the character is invisible in review and can make code read differently from how it runs (Trojan Source). They are now `\uXXXX` escapes, the same strings at run time, and the scanner's new rule SEC-130 (CWE-94) fails the build on any literal invisible or bidirectional control character in C#, TypeScript or Svelte (self-test fixtures added). Unit tests of the touched files pass; scan 0 errors.

- Summary: the daily report of one site and one local day (the site's airport time zone; a daylight-saving day has 23 or 25 hours), for border shift supervisors, terminal duty managers and administrators (`Report.View`). Per queue zone (lane, with its lane category): passengers, waits, the day's P50 and P90, the peak hour (highest P90 among hours with at least 20 waits), the longest queue, and every local hour with its own P50 and P90, merged from each minute's 30-second wait histogram (script 0036 adds `wait_buckets` and `wait_counts` to `queue_minute`; Stream writes them), never averaged; provisional and pre-histogram hours are marked. The day's alerts the reader's roles see, and each device's uptime from the outage record. Up to 400 days back. API: `GET api/v1/sites/{site}/reports/daily` (JSON) and `daily.csv?section=hours|alerts|devices` (UTF-8 with a byte order mark; formula prefixes neutralised; comma, semicolon, tab, quote and line breaks quoted; exact section names only). Schedules (`ReportSchedule.*`, audited, 100 per site): a local send time and 1 to 20 recipients, Ariva accounts only (enabled, past their first sign-in, not break-glass, allowed the site, holding a report role), checked again at every delivery. Ariva.Api.Cronz now runs TickerQ in memory: every five minutes `ReportDeliveries` owes each due schedule's previous local day to each recipient once (`report_delivery`, unique per schedule, day and recipient), then sends each delivery in its own transaction (claimed with SKIP LOCKED, the recipient's own roles' report, the three CSV files attached, headline figures in the body), recording Sent, Skipped or Failed (five attempts). The TickerQ dashboard is off unless configured with the SHA-256 of an operator key: its page under its own policy (TickerQ's two inline scripts by hash, no eval, no framing), its API only with the key, its hub only with a ticket cookie a keyed call returns (HMAC under a per-process key, HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, hub path only, 30 minutes) and from its own origin; no secret in any URL.
- Found on the way: TickerQ 10.0.2's own access check matches its API paths with case but routing ignores case, and its browser app sends no credential to its hub in host mode, while the hub still demands an Authorization value (all handled by the guard above); the outputs folder used for landing bundles is broken, worked around.
- Gates: unit 2073 (report builder, percentiles from histograms, day bounds over daylight saving, CSV neutralising and quoting, email attachments, dashboard guard: open list, case, CSP hashes, config script, ticket issue, expiry, tampering, other origin, marker not a credential); integration 309 passed, 1 pre-existing skip (report from stored minutes, alerts by role, schedule rules, deliveries once per day with disabled, role-lost and pending recipients skipped, histogram written by the stream); e2e 480 passed, 1 pre-existing skip (`reports.spec.ts`: report per role and site, CSV a spreadsheet cannot run, a schedule delivering through TickerQ in Cronz to the SMTP sink once per recipient; `jobs-dashboard.spec.ts`: the page under its policy, sign-in with the key, the delivery job listed, the hub connection held for ten seconds); docs gate, chart security test, security scan 0 errors.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-306, CWE-862, CWE-1236, CWE-79): FAIL on the first pass (required: the dashboard guard compared paths with case, so upper-case API paths were taken for the page). Fixed with tests, with its advisories (per-delivery transactions, pending accounts excluded, CORS closed, pinned inline script hashes, CSV `;` and tab quoting, exact section names, the recipient picker noted in the data boundary); re-review PASS. Its follow-up advisory that the hub might drop connections proved right; fixed with the marker and an E2E hold check; delta review PASS. Open: restricting the `api-cronz` ingress to the administration network is a deployment step (commented `whitelist-source-range` in the values files, wiki 04).

## 2026-10-04 ARV-061 Reports screen
- Summary: Ariva.Web's Reports screen (`/reports`, `Report.View`; the sidebar item Reports under Oversight is now live, so every item of the navigation is built). It opens on yesterday at the site, in the site's time zone (learnt from the first answer), with a date field up to 400 days back and Yesterday and Today so far; today's partial report says so. Four headline figures (worst peak hour at P90 with lane and hour, zone-hours above 15 minutes at P90, alerts with the critical count, lowest device uptime); every queue zone with passengers, waits, P50, P90, peak hour, longest queue and a strip of its local hours coloured by P90; a zone opens to its hours marked Final or Provisional (and when the histogram is missing); the day's alerts the reader's roles see and device uptime. The three CSV buttons save the server's files as they are (only a text/csv answer is accepted, retyped as CSV before it gets an object URL). With `ReportSchedule.Search` a Schedules tab lists the site's schedules and, by permission, adds, edits and deletes them; recipients come from the server's list, and an account already on a schedule that has since lost the right is shown so it can be removed. Without `Report.View` the screen shows the no-access panel and asks the server for nothing. English and Arabic.
- Found on the way: the Users screen's new-account form gave a site-limited administrator its one site by default only if the site list had loaded before the form opened, so on a slow answer the account had no site preselected (an intermittent E2E failure); the form now gives the site when the list arrives unless the user has changed the sites, and the test holds the site list back to prove it. The layout test expected at least one planned screen, which ARV-061 removes; it now checks every item of the role is a link.
- Gates: unit 2073; integration 309 passed, 1 pre-existing skip (no backend change); e2e 488 passed, 1 pre-existing skip (`reports-screen.spec.ts`: a border shift supervisor reads the planted lane hour, its hours, the alert with a markup rule name as text and saves the hours and alerts CSV files from the server; a terminal duty manager reads the same lane without the border alert, and the server leaves it out; schedules made, refused without recipients, changed and deleted through the screen with a markup name shown as text in the list, the form and the confirmation; a handler station manager has no item, gets the no-access panel and 403 from the server; Arabic; accessibility for `/reports` in both themes and Arabic; the users test with a delayed site list); web and docs gates; security scan 0 errors.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-79, with CWE-862, CWE-200, CWE-601 and CWE-918 checked): PASS on the first pass. Its three Low advisories taken: the CSV answer retyped (and refused unless text/csv) before it gets an object URL, the newest-request rule for the schedule list as for the report, and the confirmation's text asserted.

## 2026-10-04 ARV-066 .NET Aspire AppHost and service defaults
- Summary: `Platform/Cloud/Ariva.AppHost` (.NET Aspire 13.6, dependencies approved by Ahmad Al-Sadder 2026-10-04; development and test only, in no image or chart) starts TimescaleDB (the compose image, persistent volume), Kafka, Redis and smtp4dev on compose's ports (configurable), Ariva.Api.Main and then the other four hosts once Main is ready, the simulator and the SvelteKit dev server, with the Aspire dashboard. Hosts run in vm-local with their own `Database:*`, `Kafka:*`, `Redis:*` and `Email:*` settings (runtime login plus the migration owner, as with compose), generated passwords in the project's user secrets, and their readiness probe as the health signal; Aspire's switch that turns URL query redaction off is set back, so a SignalR `access_token` never reaches a span. The simulator holds only the SHA-256 of a generated operator key and its AMAN feed reaches the same broker (verified: a played minute's 34 AMAN records went through Kafka into Integration's intake). `Ariva.ServiceDefaults` now owns OpenTelemetry (one service instance across traces, metrics and logs), the health check service and HttpClientFactory resilience (no retry of unsafe methods, timeouts for the 60 second emulator clients); it maps no endpoint and adds no middleware. Readiness now checks the database (Unhealthy) and Redis (Degraded only, the cache is fail-safe). The aspire MCP server (`aspire agent mcp`, pinned local tool Aspire.Cli 13.6.0) lists resources, logs, traces and commands of a running AppHost and never shows variable values. `scripts/e2e-apphost.mjs` runs the Playwright suite against an AppHost configured exactly as Playwright would start the hosts (the config writes each host's variables; runtime, environment and OpenTelemetry experimental variables are refused); `ARIVA_IT_POSTGRES` points the integration tests at an existing server. Compose stays for CI.
- Found on the way: three races the AppHost's proxy latency exposed, all fixed: the zones screen's New draft was enabled before the site loaded (a draft request with no site answered 400; the button now waits for the site), the TickerQ dashboard test reloaded the page mid-request, and the AMAN feed test read one desk's row once instead of waiting for it; the AIDX oversized case now accepts a closed connection as a refusal (as the flight schedules test does). DCP launches projects through `dotnet run`, which takes `--environment` as its own option, so the environment is passed as variables only.
- Gates: unit 2088 (service defaults: telemetry off without OTLP, the orchestrator's service instance kept, no middleware or endpoint added, GET retried and POST or PUT never, timeouts; readiness Unhealthy without the database and Degraded without Redis; the AppHost model: vm-local and redaction on for every project, dependencies and logins, the simulator's digest only, the compose image and volume, the E2E switches, the variables file applied last and refused names); integration 309 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, also run against the AppHost's TimescaleDB with `ARIVA_IT_POSTGRES`; e2e 485 passed with Playwright starting the hosts, and the whole suite through the AppHost (484 of 485 on two runs, each remaining race fixed and its spec rerun); web, docs and chart gates; security scan 0 errors. Not verified here: a container image build, because NuGet's certificate chain is not trusted inside this sandbox's docker build (restore found the new project); the images workflow builds them at the next version tag or by hand.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-269 with CWE-532 and CWE-400): PASS on the first pass. Advisories taken: the E2E variables file may not set runtime, environment or OpenTelemetry experimental variables (tested), a test that the service defaults add no startup filter or endpoint, the failed-run log message says it holds the dashboard login link; container ports bind to 127.0.0.1 (seen on the running AppHost). Left as is: readiness opens a pooled database connection per probe (probes are a few seconds apart; a cached answer would report stale state).

## 2026-10-04 ARV-067 AsyncAPI 3 document for every Kafka topic
- Summary: `docs/architecture/asyncapi.yaml` (AsyncAPI 3.0.0, Kafka bindings 0.5.0) is generated by `Platform/Backplane/Ariva.UnitTests/Messaging/AsyncApi` from the code: one channel per `KafkaTopics` constant (32, with partitions, cleanup policy, retention and the `TopicCatalog` class), each message's JSON Schema from its record type with the wire's serializer options, the Kafka key and the outbox headers, send and receive operations with their consumer group ids, and the dead-letter topics as one parameterised channel. Producers, consumers and keys are the one hand-kept part (`TopicDescriptions`). The AMAN feed's four topics carry the V1 contracts, the aggregate-only rule, small-cell suppression, the ACL rule and `additionalProperties: false` (Ariva dead-letters an unknown member). Tests fail when the YAML or the wiki table (wiki 08, "All Kafka topics (AsyncAPI)") drift from the code, when a topic has no description or a producer has no payload type, when a consumer group is not registered in code, when a `$ref` does not resolve or an operation names another channel's message, when the simulator's AMAN publishes other topics than Ariva consumes, and when an AMAN schema, nested properties included, has a field named like a person, officer or document identifier. CI validates the YAML with the AsyncAPI CLI 6.2.0 (approved 2026-10-04) in a job of its own; the CLI's tree is not lockfile pinned (its advisories would fail Trivy), recorded as an open decision with a recommended alternative.
- Drift the document found, fixed: `ZoneProfilePublished.Version` hid the event's schema version on the wire (renamed `ProfileVersion`; the topic has no consumer yet; a dev cluster's compacted `ariva.topology.zone-profile-activated.v1` may still hold old records, so its first consumer purges the topic or tolerates a missing `profileVersion`); two hosts' `Kafka:GroupId` settings were never read (removed: groups are `ariva-<service>.<purpose>`); the architecture overview gave the device health topic the device key (it is the zone key).
- Found on the way: the zones screen's site loads raced (the default site's answers could land after another site was picked, leaving its levels or no level selected, so the plan upload was missing); a load now drops its results when its site or version is no longer the selected one.
- Gates: unit 2100 (backend gate), the AsyncAPI tests 7 of 7; integration 309 passed, 1 pre-existing skip (the zone profile workflow now reads `profileVersion`); e2e 488 passed, 1 skipped, and the zones spec three more times after the race fix; web check, lint and build; docs; security scan 0 errors; the CLI validates the document and fails a broken copy (checked).
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-501 with CWE-200, CWE-532 and CWE-829): FAIL on the first pass, two findings fixed: the integration test still queried the old `version` field, and the CLI ran inside the security gate job where a compromised package could alter the later gates' files (now its own read-only job). Advisories taken: `additionalProperties: false` and the ACL rule in the AMAN schemas, a wider identifier list checked through nested properties, no third-party Studio in the wiki, the decision note recommends `@asyncapi/specs` with ajv. PASS on the second pass (the zones screen fix reviewed: it fetches nothing new; a load also drops a profile that arrives after the site changed). Left for a decision: AMAN dead letters keep the raw record (up to 512 KB) for 30 days, which may include a field that should never cross the boundary (ADR-0018, predates this story).

## 2026-10-04 ARV-068 Consumer-driven contract tests for the AMAN feed (Pact)
- Summary: `Ariva.UnitTests/Contracts` writes Ariva's message pact for AMAN (`Ariva-AMAN.json`, Pact V4, PactNet 5.0.1 approved by Ahmad Al-Sadder 2026-10-04): one interaction per V1 contract with its topic, provider state and matchers for what Ariva's strict reader and intake rules need (codes and ids by Ariva's patterns, times with an offset in ASCII digits, enums by name, counts as integers, `intervalSeconds` exactly 60, the two maps by each key and each value). Every example is read by `AmanFeedJson` and `ImmigrationRules` while the pact is written. The pact's metadata carries the contract version, the data-boundary rule and small-cell suppression. Because Pact allows extra members and matches a pattern against a number's or a boolean's text, the provider harness (wiki 08) adds a shape check: only the example's members, each with the example's JSON type. Ariva runs that harness against the simulator's AMAN (open, paused and closed desks, gates with and without rejects, several flights), and broken messages must fail it on the body, not the transport. CI publishes the pact as the `aman-pact` artifact.
- Found on the way: PactNet 5.0.1 verifying messages only registers the provider with the scheme `message`, and the Pact FFI cannot call a `message://` URL; declaring an HTTP endpoint first (nothing is sent to it) makes the message transport plain HTTP. The FFI reports usage to Pact's analytics unless `PACT_DO_NOT_TRACK=true` is in the process environment, which .NET cannot set for native code at run time: `Ariva.UnitTests.runsettings` and `ci.yml` set it, and the pact tests refuse to run without it. The FFI keeps one value per metadata namespace. The disk of the build environment filled during one gate run (old review copies, cleaned); its 40 integration failures were that, and the rerun passed.
- Gates: unit 2123 (the pact tests 23, with AsyncAPI and layering 38); integration 309 passed, 1 pre-existing skip; e2e 488 passed, 1 skipped; docs; security scan 0 errors.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-501 with CWE-200, CWE-359 and CWE-829): FAIL on the first pass, fixed: the patterns used `\d` (any Unicode digit in Pact's engine) and a pattern also accepts a number or a boolean, so AMAN could pass the pact with a record Ariva dead-letters; now `[0-9]`, the shape check compares JSON types, and each of those cases is a regression test. PASS on the second pass. Advisories taken: a verification must show a matched body (a filter matching nothing passes vacuously), the simulator sample asserts all three desk states and is read by Ariva's reader, the pact is written per process and published by an atomic rename. Notes for AMAN's team: the each-key and each-value rules carry their example as text (pact_ffi 0.4.27), so confirm AMAN's Pact implementation reads them; the shape check is top level only, enough for V1's flat contracts. Open decision: a Pact Broker with AMAN (decisions.md).

## 2026-10-04 ARV-069 Mutation testing on the pure engines (Stryker.NET)
- Summary: Stryker.NET 5.0.0 (local tool, approved by Ahmad Al-Sadder 2026-10-04) mutates the engines in Ariva.Core: `Queueing/QueueStateEngine`, `QueueBins`, `ZoneProcessor`, `WaitStatistics` (realised wait attribution and percentiles), `Nowcast`, `Border/EgateCoupling` and `Flights/ArrivalWave`, and runs the unit tests that cover each mutant (`Ariva.UnitTests/stryker-config.json`; thresholds high 80, break 70). `.github/workflows/mutation.yml` runs it weekly and on demand with the HTML and JSON report as the `mutation-report` artifact; locally `node scripts/verify.mjs mutation` (about an hour on two cores).
- Found on the way: under VSTest Stryker could neither activate mutants nor capture coverage in the xUnit v3 test process, so a first four-hour run reported every mutant as surviving (score 0); the unit test executable now has `UseMicrosoftTestingPlatformRunner` and Stryker uses its MTP runner (in preview, per Stryker), with per-test coverage (35 minutes for about 1,550 mutants). `dotnet test` still runs the tests through VSTest (all 2211 discovered and passed, 88 more than before this story). The Pact FFI aborts Stryker's MTP test server (its stdout is not usable there), so the AMAN pact tests skip themselves in a Stryker run (`STRYKER_MUTANT_FILE`, or Stryker's `MutantControl` in Ariva.Core); they test no engine.
- Score: 79.59 percent on the first real run (WaitStatistics 67.2, ZoneProcessor 71.9, QueueStateEngine 78.7, ArrivalWave 81.2, EgateCoupling 82.2, QueueBins 83.7, Nowcast 87.5). Triage and tests added: F7's edges (`WaitStatisticsBoundaryTests`: the percentile domain, samples without weight or with a weight or wait that is not finite, empty input, a wait exactly at the target, histogram order, range, zero counts, the open-ended bucket and 64-bit overflow); every engine setting at its limits (`EngineSettingsBoundaryTests`, 44 bounds in six settings classes, each refused just past its limit with one problem naming it); the values that make a recomputed bin a new revision (`BinRevisionValuesTests`); each lane of the arrival wave's counts. Second run: 89.36 percent (WaitStatistics 96.6, Nowcast 93.3, QueueBins 92.1, ArrivalWave 91.7, EgateCoupling 89.0, ZoneProcessor 85.1, QueueStateEngine 85.0; 1,445 killed, 140 survived, 32 without coverage). Then a test that acknowledging a write removes every kind of pending output (minutes, bins, live minutes, recomputations, outages), which the second run showed was untested.
- Surviving mutants, triaged: (1) equivalent: `>=` against `>` where the percentile's relative tolerance or a bucket at zero makes them agree, a zero-weight sample in a sum, the loop guard of 1,000 engine steps; (2) bounded-buffer limits (`MaxStepRecords`, `MaxLateMinutes`, the handover rebuild threshold) where one off at the limit only changes when a bound bites, left as is; (3) test gaps to close later: ZoneProcessor's track samples (dropping them all fails no test), the late-event horizon counters (`_beyondHorizon`, `_unknown`) and the engine's watermark edge for an event exactly at the watermark. (4) Not tested at all: 306 mutants Stryker could not compile, whole methods dropped by its safe mode where one mutant leaves a local unassigned (`QueueStateEngine.Exit` and `FifoPartner`, `DeviceLiveness.EndAll`, and others); restructuring those methods would bring them into the run.
- Gates: unit 2211; integration 309 passed, 1 pre-existing skip; e2e 488 passed, 1 skipped; docs; security scan 0 errors; mutation run 89.36 percent (above the break of 70).
- Security review (independent reviewer, baseline with CWE-829 and CWE-494 for the tool and workflow): PASS. Advisories taken: the report upload warns when there is no report, and `verify.mjs mutation` sets `PACT_DO_NOT_TRACK` itself. Left as notes: `dotnet tool restore` relies on NuGet's repository signatures (no package source mapping yet); Stryker's MTP runner is in preview, so a bump of xunit.v3 or the test SDK should recheck that VSTest still discovers every test.

## 2026-10-04 ARV-070 Property-based tests for formulas and vendor parsers
- Summary: CsCheck 4.9.1 (approved by Ahmad Al-Sadder 2026-10-04) in `Ariva.UnitTests/Properties`. Formula properties (`FormulaPropertyTests`, 2,000 generated inputs each in every build) for every formula Ariva.Core implements: F1 footprints, F4 geometry, F7 wait statistics and histograms, F8 the nowcast and its display, F9 predicted peaks, F10 the desk state engine (every second of a minute in one state, transitions chained), F12 e-gate coupling, F14 the lane split and the arrival-wave projection (no passenger lost or added), F19 clock offsets, F20 alert rules, F22 the canonical ring text the geometry hash covers; docs/domain/formulas.md "Property tests" maps each formula to its property or why it has none (F2, F3, F21 are not in Core; F5, F6, F11 rest on the scenario and mutation tests; F13, F15 to F18 are not implemented). Parser properties (`ParserPropertyTests`, CWE-120): random bytes and each parser's valid samples cut short, mutated, with a value retyped or with an escaped lone surrogate, through the sensing ingest (Xovis, canonical, declarative), declarative mapping documents, AIDX, ACRIS, SSIM, AMAN's records and pull pages, outbound token answers and zone rings: each gives a result or Ariva's reason, never an exception, and a push never more events than its limit. `ARIVA_FUZZ_ITERATIONS` deepens a run (20,000 per parser generator and 50,000 per formula property were run once, all passing).
- Faults found and fixed, each with a regression test: invalid UTF-8 inside a JSON string parsed and then threw when read (sensing ingest, AMAN pull pages: the body is now checked as UTF-8 first); a null item in a canonical push list threw a NullReferenceException; AMAN's page position other than a number threw; a token answer that was not an object or gave `expires_in` as text threw an InvalidOperationException where callers expect an HttpRequestException; and (found by the security review) a member name or string escaping a lone surrogate (`\uDC00`), valid UTF-8 that parses, threw when read in the sensing ingest, AMAN's pages, the token answer and the ACRIS reader (where one partner endpoint could fail every polling round). Each of these readers now answers with its own "not JSON" reason. The push parsing moved into `SensingIngest.Read` (one static boundary, tested directly); the token parsing into `TokenHandler.ReadToken`. The formula properties found no formula fault; their first failures were generator and tolerance mistakes (a duplicate ring corner, 0.1 m footprint rounding, tick precision).
- Gates: unit 2247 (the properties 37); integration 309 passed, 1 pre-existing skip; e2e 488 passed, 1 skipped; docs; security scan 0 errors.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-120 and CWE-501): FAIL on the first pass, fixed: an escaped lone surrogate still threw past four readers and the generators could not produce one; the readers now answer with their "not JSON" reason, `Fuzz.Surrogates` inserts such escapes (checked: without the fix the properties fail, with it they pass), and a regression test covers every JSON reader. PASS on the second pass, which also confirmed no other partner parser reads JSON elements unguarded. Left as advisories: the no-echo check on reasons is only a length bound, the properties stay below the 256 KB, 5 MB and 20 MB body limits (covered by each reader's own limit tests), and properties that skip no-service inputs assert no minimum share of non-trivial cases.


## 2026-10-04 ARV-071 Load tests for ingest and live fan-out
- Summary: a load harness of our own, `Platform/Testing/Ariva.LoadTests` (console, base library plus StackExchange.Redis), instead of NBomber, which needs a paid licence for organisational use (decision log; open decision "Load test tool"). Scenarios: 40 canonical T3 sensors pushing 150 positions once a second, then 3x for the burst, with one device flooding past its rate during the burst; 150 dashboards on the live hub (SignalR JSON protocol over a plain WebSocket) and 50 displays polling their boards (200 connections); 20 bodies over 256 KB. The harness stands in for Ariva.Api.Stream by announcing the zone's snapshot through Redis once a second. Report: p50, p95, p99, throughput, CPU and memory per host, problems (any 5xx or transport error, a limit not answering 413 or 429 with Retry-After, a 429 to a measured device, a screen not joined or dropped, under 95 percent delivered, a board not 200).
- `tests/api/load.spec.ts` provisions a site of its own and runs the harness at smoke size in every E2E run; `ARIVA_LOAD_MODE=full` is the lab measurement (wiki 05, "Sizing notes: lab measurements"): no failures; burst p95 41 ms; delivery p95 19.5 ms to 150 screens; Ingest 21 percent of a core on average, Main 9. A second test checks the hub's cap of 8 connections per session (the ninth is closed, the eight keep receiving). `functional/live-under-load.spec.ts` watches the live screen in a browser while 40 other connections share the zone and snapshots arrive five times a second.
- Found on the way: the first run's 32 of 40 screens were refused because they shared one session (HubSessionRegistry cap of 8, working as designed): screens now sign in once per five. Display players need `?code=` with their key. CI on 109a3bd and ccb1922 failed in the E2E job because SDK 10.0.401 cannot restore dotnet-stryker from the same manifest as aspire.cli; fixed and landed separately (40da919: Stryker's own manifest in Ariva.UnitTests/.config).
- Gates: backend PASS (unit 2247); integration 309 passed, 1 pre-existing skip; e2e 491 passed, 1 skipped; docs PASS; security scan 0 errors (new SEC-020 allowlist entry for the harness's HttpClient, PENDING: Ahmad Al-Sadder).
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-120 and CWE-400): FAIL on the first pass (SEC-020 not allowlisted; the hub cap untested; the wiki claimed measured devices kept their budget while the flood ran after the burst). Fixed: allowlist entry, the cap test, the flood moved into the burst with a 429 check on measured devices, a minimal environment for the harness, harness-only error messages in the report. Second pass: PASS.
- Follow-up (same day): at Ahmad Al-Sadder's direction the harness's HTTP goes through Apizr 6.4.2 (typed Refit interfaces for the push and the display board) instead of a hand-built HttpClient, so the SEC-020 allowlist entry is withdrawn. Logging, retries and caching are off; the full run was repeated with it (wiki 05 now shows that run: burst p95 33 ms, all limits as before). Load smoke and full run pass; scan 0 errors.

## 2026-10-04 ARV-072 Failure-injection tests with Toxiproxy
- Summary: `Ariva.IntegrationTests/Faults` (Testcontainers.Toxiproxy 4.15.0, approved by Ahmad Al-Sadder 2026-10-04): PostgreSQL, Redis and Kafka on their own Docker network, reached only through one Toxiproxy, controlled through its HTTP API with an Apizr client (the product owner's preference over a hand-built HttpClient). Kafka advertises the proxy's address, so every broker connection goes through it. Scenarios, each cut off and restored while the code keeps running: broker outage (the outbox holds the events written meanwhile, none sent and none lost; readiness reports the bus; a direct publish fails, which Ingest answers 503; the relay resumes by itself and the consumer gets 1 to 8 in order, each once); Redis lost and stalled (publishes fail within the client timeout with what Stream's checkpoint catches, reads fail as outages, the subscription comes back without a restart, the kept snapshot keeps its old publication time so screens and boards go stale); database stalled and cut off (a registered device's credential lookup fails within 5 seconds as an outage and leaves no cached miss; the sensing archive waits the outage out and stores the batch once, also when redelivered).
- Production changes: `DependencyOutage` (Ariva.Infra/Resilience) and `DependencyOutageHandler` in every host: a request that meets a PostgreSQL or Redis outage or a timeout answers 503 with Retry-After 5 instead of 500, logged as a warning with the exception. The Ingest credential and zone lookups have a 5 second hard timeout (a stalled database held a push for 15 to 30 seconds before; verified red without it, 16.9 s). The architecture overview's failure table now says what is built: Ingest keeps no disk buffer (open decision "Ingest disk buffer"), and Redis and database stalls have rows.
- The data-quality reading per scenario: for the broker, readiness and the 503; for Redis, the snapshot's publication time that drives the stale state on screens (150 s) and boards (the display's threshold), and 503 on the board; for the database, the 503 to the device and the archived rows. Bin quality (`Degraded`) under sensor outages is covered by the stream tests, not repeated here.
- Also: CI on 40da919 failed the E2E log scan once ("a canary credential or an E2E account password" in the Main log, line 18705; host logs are not uploaded, so the line could not be read; fd8a2c9 and every local run were clean). The log scan now names which credential it found and the line's level, logger and message template, with every known secret and credential pattern masked, so a repeat can be traced.
- Gates: backend PASS (unit 2266); integration 314 passed, 1 pre-existing skip (the 5 fault tests about 4 minutes); e2e 491 passed, 1 skipped; docs PASS; security scan 0 errors.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-400): FAIL on the first pass (the teardown's printed template could carry a pattern-only credential; the stall test could not show that a timeout leaves no cached miss). Fixed: patterns masked too, a seeded device looked up during and after the stall with the same cache; the handler logs the exception. Second pass: PASS. Non-blocking, recorded: a slow client-controlled query that hits Npgsql's command timeout now answers 503 (a server-side statement_timeout would make it 500); Retry-After is a constant 5 seconds with no jitter (sensors may retry in step when the database returns; the per-device rate limit softens it); Testcontainers binds Toxiproxy's unauthenticated API on the host during the run.

## 2026-10-05 ARV-074 OWASP ASVS 5.0 Level 2 mapping
- Summary: `docs/security/asvs-l2.md` maps all 253 ASVS 5.0.0 requirements at Level 1 and 2: 122 Met, 72 Partly, 9 Gap, 50 N/A (122 of the 203 that apply, 60 percent). Each row cites the file, symbol and test checked in the code, or says what is missing. Four reviewers mapped the chapters against the code, and a fifth sampled about 40 rows and found no unsupported Met. Every Partly and Gap row belongs to exactly one of 17 stories in the new `backlog/prd-asvs-gaps.json` (ARV-080 to ARV-096, outside the Phase 0 count). The security reviewer now checks the touched ASVS rows on every story (`.claude/agents/security-reviewer.md` step 4; CLAUDE.md).
- Most important open gaps:
  - the Data Protection key ring is wrapped with RSA PKCS#1 v1.5 and AES-CBC without a MAC (ARV-080);
  - backend connections in the cluster are not encrypted (ARV-082);
  - every host shares one database login, one Kafka principal and one settings secret (ARV-083);
  - uploads are not malware-scanned (ARV-092);
  - there is no HSTS, and machine endpoints are silently redirected from HTTP (ARV-084).
- Found and fixed on the way:
  - ARV-081 (V16.5.3): the session, role, site and live zone join caches used the hosts' defaults (fail-safe for an hour, 500 ms soft timeout). A revoked session or a removed role could therefore pass when the database was slow or down. They now use `SecurityCacheOptions` (no fail-safe, no soft timeout, a 5 second bound, an outage answers 503), as the device credential cache already did. Tests:
    - `SecurityCacheOptionsTests`: the behaviour, plus source tests that every decision cache uses the options;
    - `DatabaseStallTests.Session_Should_FailAsAnOutageRatherThanPassOnAStaleCopy_When_RevokedAndTheDatabaseStalls` (red without the fix).
  - Chart (37e5495 and the fsGroup commit): two faults meant these pods could not have started in any cluster. The chart now has rules for both.
    - Ariva.Api.Integration's integration token key ring was never mounted.
    - The cronz, ingest, integration and stream pods had no `fsGroup`, so their non-root user could not read the 0400 key files they mount.
  - Docs:
    - The security and administration guides described OIDC sign-in through Keycloak, which is not built. Section 3 now lists every authentication pathway (users, integration clients, devices, displays, Cronz dashboard, simulator, services).
    - The CWE-89 row cited a test that does not exist; it now cites the real ones.
- Gates:
  - backend PASS (unit 2276);
  - integration 315 passed, 1 pre-existing skip;
  - e2e 491 passed, 1 skipped (production code as committed);
  - chart render clean for every environment with helm 3.16.4 (22 rules self-tested);
  - docs PASS; security scan 0 errors.
- Security review (independent reviewer, all 14 CWEs plus ASVS accuracy):
  - First pass FAIL: the ARV-081 wiring was untested; the CWE-89 citation was wrong; the simulator handler was misnamed; fsGroup was missing. All fixed.
  - Second pass PASS.
  - Non-blocking, recorded: the chart rules match secret names by text, so a renamed secret would pass them; `HubSessionSweeper` ends a pass at the first outage exception instead of going on with the other sessions.

## 2026-10-05 ARV-075 Visual regression baselines for the key screens
- Summary: `Ariva.E2E/tests/visual/screens.spec.ts` compares 14 screenshots with baselines committed in `tests/visual/__screenshots__`:
  - sign-in, live operations and immigration, in English and Arabic, light and dark;
  - the bilingual passenger display, light and dark.
- How the pixels are made comparable:
  - Rendering happens only in the official Playwright image pinned by digest (`scripts/visual-browser.mjs`). It runs as a Playwright server on 127.0.0.1 with a random path, no capabilities and no new privileges, and serves this checkout's own playwright-core. Tests and hosts run as usual, so CI and a developer machine get the same pixels.
  - Ariva.Web bundles its fonts.
  - Live values are planted: fixed snapshots for every DMO queue zone, and one fixed text in place of the publication times, whose digits move the column widths.
  - Anything that follows the clock or AMAN's feed is masked.
- Thresholds: colour distance 0.01 per pixel, 0.05 percent of pixels per screen.
  - Two consecutive clean runs passed, and so did a third after the machine restarted.
  - Changing the light-mode primary colour by five units failed both light immigration screens and nothing else (red check).
- Runs:
  - In CI the visual step runs first in the E2E job, on the fresh demo seed, and the suite still runs after a visual failure. A failure uploads `visual-diffs` (expected, actual, diff).
  - `workflow_dispatch` on `ci` with `update-visual-baselines` regenerates the baselines and uploads them as `visual-baselines`, for review and commit. The bot does not commit.
  - Locally: `node scripts/verify.mjs visual [--update]`. The run removes the display and snapshots it planted.
- Found on the way: the Arabic immigration screen was titled "الهجرة" while the navigation says "الجوازات". It is now "الجوازات" throughout.
- Gates:
  - web PASS; docs PASS; security scan 0 errors;
  - e2e 491 passed, 1 skipped; visual 14 passed.
- Security review (CWE-79 and the matrix): PASS with no required fixes. Notes acted on:
  - traces are off for the visual project (no credential in uploaded artifacts);
  - the Playwright server listens on a random path and runs with all capabilities dropped and no new privileges.
- Recorded:
  - the image digest is not covered by the base-image check (added to ARV-096);
  - the container name is fixed, so two checkouts on one machine share it.

## 2026-10-05 ARV-072 follow-up: Redis live operations bounded by their own timeout
- Found by the ARV-076 run inside the dev container: under the full integration suite, `RedisLossTests` hung twice with Redis cut off (once for 26 minutes, once until a 5 minute test timeout). A batch publish issued while the connection is down did not end at StackExchange.Redis's 5 second command timeout. Stream's checkpoint awaits that publish, so in production the same hang would stop the queue stream.
- Fix: `RedisLiveSnapshots.OperationTimeout` (6 seconds) bounds the snapshot publish, the snapshot read and the alert notice publish. Past it the call fails with a `TimeoutException`, which the checkpoint logs and moves past and which the board and the hub answer as an outage (503 with Retry-After).
- Every fault test now carries a 5 minute timeout, so a dependency that does not come back fails the run instead of hanging it.
- Gates: backend PASS (unit 2276); integration 315 passed, 1 pre-existing skip.

## 2026-10-05 ARV-076 Dev container for cloud agent sessions and Codespaces
- Summary: `.devcontainer/` builds one image for Claude Code cloud sessions, Codespaces and VS Code on a workstation.
  - Base: the same Playwright image the visual tests render in (Chromium and its fonts), pinned by digest.
  - Copied in from pinned images: the .NET 10 SDK, Node 22, uv, the Docker CLI with Compose, and Helm at CI's version.
  - Runs as `pwuser`, with no sudo and no privileged flags. Docker is reached through the host's socket (the docker-outside-of-docker feature, pinned by digest, with the Docker CLI pinned by version), so Testcontainers and the Compose services run on the host's engine.
  - `post-create.sh` runs `npm ci` for Ariva.Web, the E2E project and the chart tests, `dotnet restore`, and restores both tool manifests; `postStartCommand` starts the Compose services (`scripts/dev-up.mjs`).
- Pinning: `scripts/base-images.mjs --check` now covers the dev container's FROM lines (20 in all), keeps the Playwright image on the `@playwright/test` lock version and on the same digest as `scripts/visual-browser.mjs` (the ARV-075 gap), and keeps Helm at the version `ci.yml` installs. Dependabot watches `/.devcontainer` (Docker and dev container features); the Playwright image is excluded because it moves with `@playwright/test`.
- Privilege (CWE-269): `devcontainerProblems()` in the same check fails on a root or missing final USER, sudo, a root `remoteUser` or `containerUser`, privileged run arguments (`--privileged`, `--cap-add`, `--security-opt`, `--pid=host`, `--userns=host`, `--device`), `privileged` or `capAdd`, an unpinned feature, docker-in-docker, and a Docker CLI version that differs from the image's. A self-test on a good and a bad sample runs on every check and fails the gate if a rule stops firing.
- Verified inside the container (built from the pinned inputs, run as `pwuser` with the host's socket):
  - `node scripts/verify.mjs all --with-integration` passed every gate except the chart gate, because Helm was missing from the image. Helm was added (with the CI version rule, red-checked with 3.18.0), and `node scripts/verify.mjs security` then passed in the rebuilt image, chart gate included.
  - The same run found the Redis live operations hang (separate ARV-072 follow-up entry above).
- Gates on the host: backend PASS (unit 2276); integration 315 passed, 1 pre-existing skip; e2e 491 passed, 1 skipped (log scan clean); docs PASS; security scan 0 errors; base images PASS.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-269):
  - First pass FAIL: the privilege posture had no automated check; the CWE-269 row did not describe the container; the Dockerfile and README overstated how the Docker CLI was pinned. All fixed.
  - Second pass PASS.
  - Its non-blocking notes were acted on: the self-test's bad sample now exercises every rule, and `0:root` counts as root.
  - Recorded: the Docker socket is root-equivalent on its host. That is accepted on a disposable Codespace VM; on a workstation it is the developer's own Docker host, and the README says to keep forwarded ports Private. `parseJsonc` strips only whole-line comments, so a trailing comment makes the check throw (it fails closed).

## 2026-10-05 ARV-062 Complete Helm deployment
- Dependency: the PRD lists ARV-002, whose only open criterion is the first release on the dev cluster by a human. None of ARV-062's own criteria needs a cluster, so the story was built and verified by rendering, schema validation and running the database image as the pod runs it. The first cluster release (ARV-002) remains the real end-to-end check of this chart.
- TimescaleDB chart (`Charts/timescaledb`), installed by the Helmfile in every environment:
  - What it deploys: a StatefulSet with one replica and a `data-timescaledb-0` claim (50Gi, kept on delete); the `timescaledb` Service the base settings expect; a headless Service; a NetworkPolicy that admits pods of its own namespace only.
  - Image: `timescale/timescaledb-ha:pg17-ts2.30`, pinned by digest. `base-images.mjs --check` keeps it on the tag of `docker-compose.dev.yml`, which CI and the integration tests also use.
  - Pod privileges: runs as the image's `postgres` user (uid 1000) with a read-only root filesystem, every capability dropped, RuntimeDefault seccomp and no service account token. The only writable paths are emptyDirs for `/tmp`, the socket directory and `/dev/shm`.
  - Authentication: initdb gives peer on the socket and scram-sha-256 on every TCP connection, loopback included. The image's default is trust, which a port-forward would reach.
  - First start: the init script (env only, through psql `\getenv`) creates the migration login (CREATEROLE, not superuser) and gives it the database. Passwords come from the `timescaledb-credentials` secret.
  - Release guards refuse: an image without a digest, `latest`, a missing secret, and the superuser or an Ariva role as migration login.
- Kafka topics job:
  - `api-main --provision-topics` (new `TopicProvisioning` command) runs as a Helm hook next to the migration job.
  - It creates missing topics and dead-letter topics, never alters an existing one, refuses plaintext Kafka in k8s-prd as the hosts do, and exits 1 when the broker is still unreachable after five minutes.
  - With the job on, api-main runs with `Kafka__ProvisionTopics=false`.
  - `kafkaTopics.adminSecretName` gives the job a principal of its own, so the hosts' principal can lose create rights (recommended in production; empty by default).
- Helmfile: `timescaledb` (a state value, true in dev, demo, prd and localk8s) is applied first and waited for; `ariva-platform` needs it.
- Pipelines:
  - `Release-ariva-k8s-dev.yaml` creates `timescaledb-credentials` from two new variable group secrets. It refuses an undefined (macro text) or short password, and removes its files on exit.
  - `ci.yml` installs Helmfile 1.1.7 with a sha256 check, so the chart test renders every Helmfile environment in CI.
- Chart test (30 rules self-tested on fixtures), new rules:
  - third-party images pinned by digest;
  - no literal credential in `env`;
  - a StatefulSet needs a NetworkPolicy and no service account token;
  - a PostgreSQL container needs peer and scram initdb arguments and no `POSTGRES_HOST_AUTH_METHOD`;
  - no `CREATE ROLE` without `NOSUPERUSER`;
  - the topics job implies `Kafka__ProvisionTopics=false`.
  
  It also renders the database chart and its guards, and every Helmfile environment, and checks that production without a build number is refused.
- Tests:
  - `TopicProvisioningCommandTests` (unit: flag, Kafka off, plaintext in production, unknown protocol, unreachable broker within the deadline).
  - `TopicProvisioningTests` (integration: every topic created once with partitions, retention and min.insync; a second run succeeds; a hand-made topic is never altered).
  - `DatabaseChartBootstrapTests` (integration). It reads the digest, initdb arguments and init script from the chart and runs the image like the pod (uid 1000, read-only root, no capabilities). It checks: no password-free TCP from outside or on loopback; a wrong password is refused; the migration login is a non-superuser owner; the real migration runs as it; the runtime login cannot create a table.
  
  Red checks: changing the chart to trust fails both the chart test and the integration test.
- Also verified by hand:
  - `kubeconform -strict` (Kubernetes 1.30 schemas) on every render;
  - `api-main --migrate` as the non-superuser owner against the read-only container;
  - `api-main --provision-topics` end to end against the E2E broker (peak memory about 75 MB; the job's limit is 256Mi).
- Docs: wiki/04 (status; database options; secrets in 6.2; release order and hook jobs in 6.3; bootstrap in 7.1 and 7.2; the database pod) and `Platform/Cloud/CLAUDE.md`. In docs/security: the CWE-269 row; ASVS V12.3.3, V13.2.2 and V13.3.2.
- Gates:
  - backend PASS (unit 2281);
  - integration 317 passed, 1 pre-existing skip;
  - e2e 491 passed, 1 skipped (log scan clean);
  - chart security PASS with Helm 3.19.0 and Helmfile 1.1.7;
  - base images PASS; docs PASS; security scan 0 errors.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-269 and the matrix):
  - First pass FAIL, three fixes: no automated test held the database authentication (now a chart rule and the bootstrap integration test); the pipeline would have initialised the database with the public macro text if a variable was missing (now refused); the docs claimed the hosts' Kafka principal needed no create rights even without a job principal (now qualified).
  - Second pass PASS.
  - Recorded, non-blocking:
    - The database NetworkPolicy admits every pod in the namespace, and there is no egress policy.
    - The topics job mounts both appsettings secrets but needs only Kafka.
    - The migration job still mounts a service account token (predates this story).
    - The digest rule recognises Ariva images by name only.
    - The database runs without TLS (ARV-082).
    - wiki/Home.md's dated status table (1 October) still calls the TimescaleDB chart a placeholder.

## 2026-10-05 ARV-063 Dynamic security scanning pipeline
- Summary: OWASP ZAP 2.17, pinned by digest, scans the real hosts (Playwright project `zap` in `Platform/Testing/Ariva.E2E/tests/zap`, `node scripts/verify.mjs zap`, `.github/workflows/security-zap.yml` weekly and on demand).
  - API scans (passive and active) of the Main, Ingest and Integration OpenAPI documents with the token of `e2e.zapadmin`, a system administrator with no second factor, so every critical action answers 401 and none is carried out. The sign-in, session and hub operations are removed from the documents, because ZAP's import calls every operation, and excluded from the active scan and the spider. Each scan must still hold its session at the end.
  - Baseline scans of every API host, and of the web build under the web image's own nginx image and configuration.
  - When `ARIVA_DEV_WEB_URL` is set, a passive baseline of the dev deployment.
  - Gate: the run fails on a High risk alert without an approved entry in `security/zap-triage.json`. An incomplete entry is an error, and a PENDING approver accepts nothing. Reports (HTML, JSON, logs, `summary.md`) are uploaded as artifacts. A grep of the reports found no token, password or Authorization header.
- OpenAPI documents (needed by the API scan):
  - Microsoft.AspNetCore.OpenApi, which was already in the package catalog.
  - Served at `/openapi/v1.json` only with `OpenApi:Enabled`, only to SystemInfo.View, and never in k8s-prd: the host refuses to start (`OpenApiTests`). No UI.
- Findings and triage (final run: no High, Medium or Low left):
  - Fixed: four endpoints answered 500 to `siteCode=%00`, because PostgreSQL refuses NUL in text. The reviewer then found the same class elsewhere (search text, topology names). Two fixes:
    - Every controller now refuses a NUL character with 400 (`NulCharacterFilter` for the query, route values and forms; `NulRejectingStringConverter` for JSON strings). Tests: `NulCharacterTests`, red-checked; e2e `null-bytes.spec.ts`, 11 cases.
    - `SiteScopedAttribute` answers 404 before the lookup for any other value that cannot be a site code.
  - Fixed in the harness: the E2E log scan matched the numeric TOTP canary inside a log timestamp (".4938178" holds 493817), a false positive that can fail any long run, possibly the unexplained CI log-scan failure on 40da919. A digits-only secret now counts only as a whole number. The rule is self-checked on every teardown.
  - Informational, accepted with no action:
    - 4xx client errors from ZAP's placeholder values;
    - the CSP being set in a meta tag as well as the header (SvelteKit hash mode, by design);
    - "Modern Web Application".
  - Also found: the rate limiter answered most of the first scan with 429, so the scan run raises the global limit; the limiter's own tests are unchanged.
- Also fixed:
  - wiki/04 gave a break-glass command that cannot work: `dotnet Ariva.Api.Main.dll` does not exist in the runtime-deps image, and the pod had no settings. It is now `kubectl exec deploy/api-main-deployment -- ./Ariva.Api.Main --create-break-glass`.
  - `base-images.mjs` keeps the scan's ZAP image equal to the workflow's, and its nginx equal to the web image's base.
- Gates:
  - backend PASS (unit 2302);
  - integration 317 passed, 1 pre-existing skip;
  - e2e 502 passed, 1 skipped;
  - zap 5 passed;
  - docs PASS; security scan 0 errors; base images PASS.
- Security review (independent reviewer, all 14 CWEs):
  - First pass FAIL: the triage file could silence a High finding without approval; NUL bytes still reached PostgreSQL through other inputs; the log-scan change had no test. All fixed.
  - Second pass PASS.
  - Open, for the product owner: `.claude/hooks/guard-paths.mjs` protects only `security/allowlist.json` from agent approvals. Extending it to `security/zap-triage.json` changes the hooks, which needs your approval.
  - Follow-ups, non-blocking:
    - scan depth: ZAP fills placeholder values, so most Main requests end in 404 or 400, and Ingest and Integration are scanned without their own credentials. Seeded example values and device or integration credentials would reach further;
    - URL-scoped triage entries;
    - tests for the guard's route and form branches;
    - the web image serves `/nginx/default.conf` (pre-existing, low risk).

## 2026-10-05 ARV-073 Signed images with SBOM and provenance
- Summary: `.github/workflows/images.yml`, for every image pushed to GitHub Container Registry. Images are pushed only from a `v*` tag, or when the workflow is run by hand from `main`; any other ref builds and scans without pushing.
  - The digest is read from the registry and checked.
  - The image is signed with cosign keyless: GitHub OIDC, a Fulcio certificate naming the repository, workflow, ref and commit, and the Rekor log. cosign 3.0.6 via sigstore/cosign-installer v4.1.2, pinned by SHA.
  - Its CycloneDX SBOM (Trivy 0.75.0) is attached as a signed attestation.
  - Both are verified at once against this repository's `images.yml`, on `v*` tags or `main` only, with the GitHub issuer and `--certificate-github-workflow-repository`.
  - A digests job writes the table for the release notes, with the verify command, to the job summary and the `image-digests` artifact.
- Docs:
  - wiki/04 section 6.5:
    - verification and SBOM extraction;
    - what keyless signing publishes;
    - the trust preconditions (tag ruleset, protected `main`);
    - `oras copy -r` to carry signatures and SBOMs into Dalil Container Registry;
    - an optional Kyverno `SigstoreBundle` policy in Audit mode, labelled untested.
  - wiki/18: release notes carry the digests.
  - wiki/05: flow 24, Sigstore egress for verifiers.
  - Platform/Cloud/CLAUDE.md; ASVS V15.1.2; ARV-096 (SBOM per release delivered).
- Verified locally (keyless needs GitHub's OIDC, so not end to end here):
  - With cosign 3.0.6, Trivy 0.75.0 and a local registry, using a key and no transparency log: sign, a CycloneDX attestation, `verify` and `verify-attestation`, and the documented `jq` SBOM extraction.
  - `oras copy -r` to another repository keeps the digest, signature and attestation; `cosign copy` does not.
  - The signer pattern matches `images.yml` on a tag and on `main`, and refuses `ci.yml`, another owner and a feature branch.
  - The digests script ran on sample files.
  - The Kyverno policy passes `kubeconform -strict` against Kyverno's schema.
  - `docker inspect .RepoDigests` was found to return the upstream index digest, hence the registry lookup.
- Not verified: the first real signed run. It happens on the first `v*` tag, and CI is not running on this account (billing, see earlier). After the first copy to ACR, run the documented `cosign verify` there; ACR serves the native referrers API, whereas the local test used the fallback tags.
- Decisions for the product owner (docs/product/decisions.md):
  - **Keyless signing publishes the private repository's name, workflow, refs and commit ids in the public Rekor log** (keep it, or use a Key Vault key);
  - a ruleset protecting `v*` tags, plus protected `main`, is needed before the first signed release.
- Gates: workflow YAML parses; docs PASS; security scan 0 errors. No application code changed.
- Security review (independent reviewer, CWE-494 and the matrix):
  - First pass FAIL: the documented `cosign copy` loses the evidence; the Kyverno example matched the wrong registry and namespaces and read the legacy format; the tag-trust precondition and the public-log disclosure were missing; the ASVS and ARV-096 statements were off. All fixed.
  - Second pass PASS. Its wording notes on key-based signing and the summary command were acted on.
  - Recorded residuals:
    - `id-token: write` is granted to builds that sign nothing (a separate sign job would narrow it);
    - a window between push and the digest lookup (needs `packages:write` on a per-run tag);
    - no SLSA provenance attestation (GitHub artifact attestations for private repositories need Enterprise Cloud);
    - the Azure DevOps builds stay unsigned (Target);
    - CWE-494 is not a row of the 14-CWE matrix.

## 2026-10-05 ARV-002 Release to k8s-dev from GitHub Actions (follow-up)

- Owner's call: release to the dev cluster from GitHub Actions for now, and keep the Azure DevOps pipelines ready for the move to Dalil Azure. Those pipelines are unchanged.
- Delivered:
  - `.github/workflows/release-dev.yml`, run by hand on `main` with the tag the `images` workflow gave a build, in the `k8s-dev` environment:
    - verifies every image's keyless signature against this repository's `images.yml` and stops if one is not signed;
    - deploys each image pinned by the digest taken from the signed payload;
    - writes the appsettings, TimescaleDB and GHCR pull secrets (through files, never command lines);
    - checks the one-time secrets exist;
    - applies the Helmfile;
    - lists the pods and their security context as the ARV-002 evidence.

  - `images.yml` pushes to `ghcr.io/<owner>/ariva/<service>`.
  - Chart:
    - value `imageDigests`, rendered by the `ariva-platform.image` helper in all 9 workloads;
    - `validate.yaml` refuses unknown services and digests that are not sha256.
  - `chart-security.mjs` renders the Helmfile with the workflow's values and checks every image is GHCR at its own digest, that the chart renders no pull secret, and that bad digests are refused (mutation-tested).
  - wiki/04 section 6.6:
    - the one-time setup (environment, deployment branches `main`, plan limits, no repository secrets, namespace-scoped kubeconfig, self-hosted runner hardening);
    - the steps for each release;
    - switching the pull secret between the GitHub and Azure DevOps releases.
  - wiki/18; ASVS V13.3.1 and V15.1.2; wiki/13 CWE-269 row; Platform/Cloud/CLAUDE.md.
- Gates:
  - `actionlint` is clean on every workflow.
  - `chart-security.mjs` passes, including every Helmfile environment.
  - `base-images.mjs` passes.
- Not verified: a real run. GitHub Actions jobs do not start on this account (billing), and no cluster is reachable from here.
- Security review (independent reviewer):
  - First pass FAIL, all fixed:
    - jq read the credentials from argv;
    - tags could move between the check and the deploy;
    - helm-diff was not verified;
    - pull-secret ownership;
    - environment documentation;
    - tests.
  - Second pass FAIL, both fixed:
    - `runner` context in job env (the workflow would not start);
    - Helm deleting the workflow's pull secret on the first upgrade after an Azure DevOps release (now `helm.sh/resource-policy: keep`).
  - Final pass PASS.
  - Residuals:
    - the digest pin proves the image is signed by `images.yml`, not that it is that build; compare the release summary with the `images` run's digest table;
    - a Role written from the docs needs `patch` on secrets (for the annotation) and `get` on the namespace object;
    - actionlint is not part of CI.

## 2026-10-05 ARV-064 Scripted demo and runbook
- Summary: the reference evening at DMO (seed 9303) played in real time through the whole pipeline, with nothing planted: the simulator's sensor emulator pushes to Ingest as devices registered and calibrated by the demo, Stream computes the queues and evaluates the seeded rules, Main relays both to the live screens.
  - Three scripted events, each for the role that owns it: 18:05 the visitor wave takes the A-VIS nowcast past 15 minutes and R-001 reaches the border shift supervisor; 18:20 to 18:30 sensor S-17 is offline and R-003 names it; 19:10 check-in island C breaches its 15-minute P90 and R-004 reaches the handler station manager.
  - `tests/demo/reference-day.spec.ts` (Playwright project `demo`, only with `ARIVA_E2E_DEMO=1`, so CI's e2e job is unchanged) walks the three events; `node scripts/verify.mjs demo` runs it. It loads every counting sensor (20) by default and starts on the next whole minute with up to 14 demo minutes of pre-roll, so the 15-minute bins fall on quarter hours.
  - `scripts/demo-local.mjs` (`prepare | start | status | stop | accounts | code`) runs the same demo on a developer machine through Ariva.AppHost: demo accounts and the AppHost settings in `.demo/` (git-ignored, owner-only), loopback only unless `--remote`.
  - Owner decisions (2026-10-05): the full real-time rehearsal is its own Playwright project and the short live demo is documented (17:50 to 18:35); event 3 is R-004 plus the CI-C nowcast (no SLA engine); the AMAN desk term joins the nowcast, and "Data degraded" means a sensor or length problem only.
  - Desk term (F8): `DeskTerms.Compute` (open desks for at least 30 s of the latest closed minute; c from AMAN's lane cycle time, open time per transaction as fallback), `DeskTermSource` reads `desk_minute` and `border_desk_interval` every `Stream:DeskTermSeconds` (15). Live only: replays never set it, so the golden replay is unchanged.
  - Ingest takes a push's receipt time in its first middleware (`ReceiptTime`): the first credential check took over 500 ms, which F19 read as a clock offset and marked every device Unreliable for its first 10 pushes.
  - Simulator: a queue lead with an overflow band reports at most the band's cap, the band the rest (Ariva's sum double counted).
  - Web: `waitStatus` gives "Data degraded" for stale or degraded length only, "No estimate" when there is no nowcast, and an estimate marker (≈, with its hint) for exit-rate-only nowcasts.
- Fixed on the way (found by the rehearsal and the gates):
  - Alert evaluation judged a minute as soon as its row existed. The stream stores a minute's counts while it runs and its queue length and nowcast about half a minute after it ends, so a tick in between took the minute without a nowcast and never judged it again. R-001 missed the visitor wave in two rehearsals (A-VIS above 15 minutes for 7 minutes, rule state "sustained 0"); a 5-second probe showed the tick at :29 and the nowcast written at :32. `AlertInputs.LatestMinutesAsync` now counts only minutes with a queue length (the live part). Regression test `Tick_Should_WaitForTheLivePartOfAMinute_When_OnlyItsCountsAreStoredYet`, red before the fix.
  - The live alerts panel showed an alert's zone or, without one, its device, so R-003 said A-VIS and never S-17. It now shows both (functional test).
  - The guard for `Auth:DevelopmentUsers` and `Auth:TotpRequired` false ran only in Main's token issuing and read the `environment` configuration key. It now runs in `AddArivaAccounts` (every host and the break-glass command) with the host's own environment name; Cronz reads `Auth:TotpRequired` when it picks report recipients.
  - `ImmigrationIntakeTests` counted every AMAN desk session in the class's shared database; the XS2 test's two sessions failed it depending on order.
- Gates: backend PASS on 5896e1b (2,324 unit tests, golden replay unchanged, security scan, base images, chart security, NuGet and npm audits); integration 313 passed and 1 skipped, the 6 Toxiproxy fault tests not runnable here (their ghcr.io image is blocked in the cloud sessions); e2e 504 passed and 1 skipped (CI's command, local TimescaleDB, Kafka and Redis); web PASS (svelte-check, lint, build); visual 14 of 14 after 8 baselines were regenerated on fresh services (live and immigration screens, the intended changes reviewed); the demo rehearsal: event 1 passed in one run and events 2 and 3 in the next, after the two fixes above; the local path (`demo-local.mjs prepare` and `start` through Ariva.AppHost) showed R-001 to demo.border at 18:06 demo time. GitHub Actions does not start on this account, so no CI run.
  - Also fixed for the gates: the `DeskTerms` integration test left DMO desk minutes 40 days ahead in the class's shared database, which stopped the DMO desk feed of `DeskFeed_Should_DriveTheDeskEngine...` whenever xUnit ran it first; it now removes them.
- Security review (independent reviewer):
  - Cowork's review: PASS after one round (Medium: the TOTP bypass and development users were not enforced at runtime; fixed).
  - Delta review of this session's commits: first pass FAIL (Medium, CWE-287/306: the guard ran in Main only), fixed in 22a4b9e; second pass PASS. The alert change passed on the first pass (bound SQL; no input can write a row without a queue length).
  - Residuals: an image started without DOTNET_ENVIRONMENT falls back to `environment.json` (vm-local) and `Application:Environment` defaults to vm-local, so a hand-run container could enable the local-only settings (the chart always sets the environment); if the stream's live write stalls, BinP90 and PredictedNowcast rules stop without a signal that evaluation is behind; `LatestMinutesAsync` has no lower time bound; all hosts share `ariva_runtime`, which may write `queue_minute`.
- Learnings:
  - Visual baselines must be regenerated on fresh services: after the full e2e suite the masked panels on the live screen grow with leftover alerts and desks.
  - `--repeat-each` cannot repeat a test that signs in with TOTP: a code counts once per account.
  - In the cloud sessions `pkg-containers.githubusercontent.com` is blocked, so the Toxiproxy fault tests (ghcr.io image) cannot run there; everything else pulls from Docker Hub.
  - The repository hooks allow edits under the repository path only, so a second git worktree elsewhere cannot be edited with the edit tools.
- Follow-ups (not in this story):
  - MassTransit 8.5 sends usage telemetry (versions, OS, the server's time zone, Kafka topic names) from Main, Ingest, Integration and Stream at startup; nothing disables it. Disable it in code with a test.
  - Images should fail safe: `environment.json` should not default to vm-local.
  - Dependabot: cookie 0.6 via SvelteKit 2 (fixed only in SvelteKit 3; not shipped by adapter-static) and esbuild 0.19 via svelte-i18n 4.0.1 (latest; development only). Owner to accept or override.
  - Ariva.Web has no shadcn-svelte component layer (bits-ui is never imported); see the frontend review of 2026-10-05.

Before the pilot contract: ARV-097 (MassTransit telemetry, High) and the owner's approval of five PENDING allowlist entries.
