## 2026-10-06 ARV-097 No outbound usage telemetry from MassTransit
- Summary: MassTransit 8.5 reported versions, the OS, the server's time zone and the Kafka topic names to `usage-tracking.masstransit.io` about five minutes after a host started (Main, Ingest, Integration, Stream). `AddArivaMessaging`, where every host registers the bus, now calls `DisableUsageTelemetry()`, and `EnsureNoUsageTelemetry` refuses to start a host whose `MASSTRANSIT_USAGE_TELEMETRY` is true or non-zero (MassTransit's tracker reads it after the options are built and would turn reporting back on).
- Tests: `MessagingRegistrationTests` (telemetry off, red without the fix; the variable refused or allowed), `MessagingLayeringTests` (only `MessagingExtensions.cs` registers a MassTransit bus anywhere in `Platform/`, generic form included), and the E2E log scan fails a run whose host logs report usage telemetry (a backstop: the report comes about five minutes after start and only with Kafka).
- Docs: `docs/security/asvs-l2.md` (V14.2.3, V13.2.4, ARV-097 row), `wiki/13-Security-Guide.md`, `docs/product/phase0-exit-review.md` (item closed).
- Gates: backend PASS (2,330 unit tests, 14 CWEs, audits); e2e 504 passed and 1 skipped, the log scan clean over 9 minutes of host logs (the run outlasts the report delay). GitHub Actions does not start on this account, so these were local runs.
- Security review: first pass FAIL (Medium: the environment variable re-enabled reporting; Low: V13.2.4 row, PRD entry), fixed in 8de1aea; second pass PASS. The PRD entry came with #57, so `passes: true` and this entry landed after #57 and #59 merged (PR #60 merged as 963ab41 and 471152c).
- Learnings:
  - A library's options can be overridden after they are built: test the behaviour the library ends up with (here the tracker's environment variable), not only the options.
  - `dotnet test` in this repository runs vstest: arguments after `--` are run settings, so use `--filter "FullyQualifiedName~..."` to run a subset.

## 2026-10-06 ARV-098 Images fail safe without their environment
- Summary: a container or pod started without DOTNET_ENVIRONMENT used to run as vm-local (development accounts, sign-in without TOTP, SchemaUpdate, the demo seed): `ArivaEnvironment.Resolve` fell back to `environment.json`, shipped as vm-local in every image, and then to vm-local, and `appsettings.base.json` defaulted `Application:Environment` to vm-local.
  - Images are published without `environment.json` (`CopyToPublishDirectory` Never in the five API projects and the simulator); developer build output keeps it, so `dotnet run`, the E2E suite and the AppHost still get vm-local.
  - `Resolve` has no default and accepts only vm-local, k8s-dev, k8s-demo and k8s-prd (any case, trimmed, returned canonical); anything else stops the host. The simulator's copy moved to `SimulationEnvironment` with the same rule.
  - `Application:Environment` comes only from `appsettings.base.<environment>.json`, and every host refuses to start when it is missing or differs from the host environment (`ArivaEnvironment.EnsureConfigured`, right after configuration layering, before `--migrate` and `--provision-topics`). A release that replaces that file from the `APPSETTINGS_BASE` secret must keep the key (wiki/04).
  - Chart rule (`Ariva.K8s/tests/checks.mjs`): every container running a .NET Ariva image must resolve the release environment the way the host does (an `--environment`, `/environment` or bare `environment=` argument wins, then the last env entry, then the last envFrom ConfigMap; a Secret source or valueFrom counts as unknown).
- Tests: `SecuritySettingsTests` (no environment, unknown names from arguments and from the file, canonical names, publish exclusion per project, base settings name no environment, `EnsureConfigured`, every host refuses a mismatched `Application:Environment`), `SimulationEnvironmentTests`; chart self-test 36 rules with a bad fixture per bypass. Verified by hand: a published Main has no `environment.json` and stops with "No Ariva environment is set".
- Gates: backend PASS (2,358 unit tests, 14 CWEs, audits); e2e 506 passed and 1 skipped, log scan clean; chart test rendered for real for all four values files through an `alpine/helm` container (get.helm.sh is blocked in the cloud session, Docker Hub is not).
- Security review: first pass FAIL (Medium: the chart rule ignored `--environment` arguments; Medium: envFrom precedence read first-wins, prefixes and Secrets ignored); fixed in 84bb7ef together with the recommended `EnsureConfigured`; second pass PASS, its Low (the bare `environment=` argument) fixed in 9d38cf0.
- Learnings:
  - Kubernetes resolves duplicate variables last-wins, and explicit `env` beats `envFrom`; a manifest check must mirror the runtime's precedence, and its bad fixtures must cover overrides, not only absence.
  - Helm can run from Docker Hub's `alpine/helm` image (`HELM=<wrapper>`) where its download site is blocked, so the chart test need not be skipped in the cloud session.
  - An Aspire AppHost unit test waits forever when Docker is down; after a container restart start dockerd before the backend gate (a suggested task covers making it fail fast).

## 2026-10-06 ARV-080 Data Protection key ring with RSA-OAEP and AES-GCM, and crypto agility
- Summary: the key ring that protects refresh cookies, TOTP seeds and outbound secrets was wrapped by the framework's `EncryptedXml` (RSA PKCS#1 v1.5, AES-256-CBC without a MAC) and read every key in the table.
  - `OaepGcmXmlEncryptor`: each key element is AES-256-GCM under a fresh key wrapped with RSA-OAEP-SHA256, with the algorithm label and certificate thumbprint as associated data, and signed with RSA-PSS-SHA256 by the certificate's private key; the decryptor verifies the signature before decrypting. New keys protect payloads with AES-256-GCM.
  - `TrustedKeyRingRepository` in front of PostgreSQL: a key is admitted only in the exact shape Data Protection writes (one encryptedSecret at key/descriptor/descriptor, no masterKey or value elsewhere, one unqualified creationDate, activationDate, expirationDate and descriptor). Keys in the older format are read only while `DataProtection:LegacyFormatUntil` is in the future, and only as expired copies, so they decrypt but never protect. vm-local sets 2027-01-06; no committed cluster file sets it (test).
  - `DataProtectionCertificates.RequireStrong`: RSA 3072 or larger, current and previous; EC refused (it cannot wrap with OAEP).
  - Cronz `SecretReprotection` (daily 02:40 UTC): re-protects user and integration client TOTP seeds and outbound secrets under the current key when Data Protection reports they need migration, each with a compare-and-swap UPDATE; values under revoked keys are left alone; logs carry row ids only.
  - Split: algorithm labels on refresh token, recovery code, device and display credential hashes went to ARV-080a, so V11.2.2 stays Partly. ASVS: V11.3.1, V11.3.2 and V11.3.3 Met (125 Met, 69 Partly).
- Tests: `KeyRingEncryptionTests` (24: round trip, each part altered, relabelling, missing or altered signature, certificate swap, written keys free of rsa-1_5, CBC, EncryptedData and plaintext, AES-256-GCM descriptor, legacy readable only in the window, planted plaintext key, decoy encrypted secret, legacy key planted in the window, namespaced expiry decoy, production wiring, pinned decryptor type name, weak RSA and EC refused, cluster files keep the window closed), `SecretReprotectionTests` (6 against PostgreSQL: the three tables, unreadable values, a concurrent re-enrolment, a revoked key, no secret in logs), `JobsDashboardTests` (the job's schedule). Each attack test was run against the filter it fixes and failed there.
- Gates: backend PASS (2,382 unit tests, 14 CWEs, audits); integration 321 passed and 1 skipped, the 6 Toxiproxy fault tests not runnable here (ghcr.io blobs blocked), as in the exit review; e2e 506 passed and 1 skipped.
- Security review: four rounds. 1 FAIL (High: a key planted in the table, which the job would then use for every stored secret; Medium: no end to the legacy format, docs overclaiming, missing tests). 2 FAIL (High: a plaintext key with a genuine signed blob copied in as a decoy passed the filter, reproduced against the framework; Medium: a legacy key planted during the window could become the default). 3 FAIL (Medium: a namespaced expirationDate decoy kept a planted legacy key active). 4 PASS at 3dfa071.
- Learnings:
  - Data Protection stores the master key as an encryptedSecret in place of masterKey and reads dates by exact unqualified name; a filter over its XML must check the exact shape and names, not the presence of an element.
  - A test that plants a key must activate it after the genuine key, or the planted key never competes to be the default and the test proves nothing; run every attack test against the old code.
  - Since .NET 9 the key ring refreshes in the background: a test that rolls a key waits until the new default is visible.
  - Upgrading an installation that holds data: set `DataProtection:LegacyFormatUntil` before deploying, let the job run, then remove it (wiki/04); without it old TOTP seeds, outbound secrets and refresh cookies become unreadable. Belongs in the release notes.
