- Security review (CWE-862, CWE-863, CWE-501, CWE-120): see the verdict below.
- Decisions for the owner (docs/product/decisions.md): the estimator (Serving, not open, desk minutes over exits); the Proposed window, minimum exits, bounds, Unknown share and cap; NothingOpen not flagged for the cycle time; whether a site without AMAN should publish a sensor cycle time (the shadow now differs from the published nowcast there in the minutes where it has one).
- Deferred: a window longer than 10 minutes (needs its own read of the sensor-only minutes, so the published term's 15-minute read stays as it is); the walk-up gap bias (pilot data); an end-to-end stream test from `desk_sensor_minute` through the worker to `queue_minute_shadow.sensor_cycle_minutes` (the store, the desk term source and the zone are each tested); ARV-104f reads the new column through `ariva_validation_reader` (table-level SELECT, no change needed).
- Learnings: open time over exits is the realised throughput again (the F8 identity), so it cannot be a cycle time for the desk term; the AMAN emulator's one-minute interval statistics make AMAN's lane cycle time 1.00 min (60 s per transaction, at most one transaction a desk minute), which makes the published nowcast optimistic on the simulator.
- Security review (2026-10-07): verdict PASS, no blocking finding. The reviewer also checked on a scratch TimescaleDB that a chunk existing before 0045 and one created after it both deny the runtime role SELECT on `sensor_cycle_minutes`, and that the new check adds no probe bit beyond 0043's accepted one. Folded in: refused-settings cases for non-finite `MaximumCycleMinutes` and `MaxUnknownShare` (Low). Carried forward (Low): an integration test of the 0045 upgrade path on a database that already holds shadow chunks (the ad-hoc probe shows it works today). Owner decisions 2026-10-07: the Serving-based estimator accepted as Proposed, re-measured on pilot data; non-AMAN sites keep the published nowcast unchanged until then; the emulator's constant AMAN cycle time gets ARV-117c.

## 2026-10-07 ARV-117c Realistic AMAN interval statistics in the simulator (MVP, branch story/mvp-phase1; passes)
- Summary: the AMAN emulator's desk interval statistics now come from the scenario's served people. `AmanDeskProcess` (simulator, new) takes each minute's served share of every border desk (the lane's served people shared between its active desks by their speed, as before) and works it through as discrete transactions: a family of 1, 2 or 3 people (80, 15 and 5 percent, mean 1.25), each needing its people times the desk's service time per person times a variation of mean 1 (0.5 plus an exponential of mean 0.5, at most 4; hashed from the seed, the desk code and the transaction's index). A transaction is counted whole in the interval in which it completes, with its service time (working seconds) and its cycle time (previous transaction's start at the desk to its start, in open time: paused minutes left out, idle minutes in); the first transaction of a session, or after a gap over 3,600 s, has its own service time as its cycle; a pause keeps the transaction in progress, a close drops it. The interval reports transactions, documents, mean and nearest-rank P90 service time and mean cycle time, all zero when none. Computed once per day for the eight immigration queues and cached per `ScenarioDay` (`ConditionalWeakTable`, `Lazy`); `AmanFeed.Desks` reads it. E-gates, sessions, lane demand, source event ids and the HTTP surface are unchanged; seed 9303's scripted events are untouched (the scenario itself is not changed).
- Minute boundaries (the caller's question): not spread. `TransactionsProcessed` counts completions and Ariva's `LaneCycle` (F10) weights each interval's mean cycle by it, so counting each completed transaction once, whole, in its completion interval makes the lane cycle time the mean cycle per transaction; spreading a transaction over the minutes it spans would break both the count and the weighting. `AmanDeskFeed` takes only the transaction count (`DeskTransactionsCompleted` at the interval's end) and is unaffected. Documented in the simulator README and formulas F10 (to confirm with AMAN).
- Comparison (reference evening, seed 9303, 17:20 to 20:25, Ariva's own simulator, not field data; `SensorCycleScenarioTests`): AMAN's lane cycle time as the published term reads it, median 2.11 min (1.02 to 23.13; was 1.00 all evening). Published (AMAN n_open and lane cycle time) mean absolute error 1.85 min, bias +1.85 (111 minutes, 1 without a number; was 1.17, -1.07). Shadow with the sensor cycle time 0.74, +0.53; shadow without it 0.81, +0.61; open desk minutes over exits 1.01, +0.94 (all unchanged: the shadow reads no AMAN input). For the owner only (Ariva unchanged): lane cycle time per person (cycle seconds over documents) median 1.70 min, published 1.22, +1.11; per person in working time (idle left out, not published by AMAN) 1.49 min, published 0.63, +0.28.
- Per lane (`AmanIntervalScenarioTests`, reference day): while a lane runs at capacity for the whole 12-minute window, AMAN's lane cycle time per transaction over 1.25 people's service time has median A-VIS 1.00, A-CIT 0.99, D-VIS 0.95, D-RES 1.00 (per person 0.99, 1.03, 0.98, 1.04; asserted within 10 percent for every lane with at least 10 such minutes); over all minutes with a cycle 1.20 (A-VIS) to 10.0 (D-CRW), because a start-to-start cycle while open carries the idle time after a lull; in working time per person 0.89 to 1.00. Over the day, documents are within 4 percent of the scenario's served people in every lane with at least 200 (A-VIS 3,988 against 3,985), 1.25 people a transaction where at least 1,000 transactions; every desk record of the day passes `ImmigrationRules` (contract V1 bounds); deterministic across runs.
- Findings for the owner (docs/product/decisions.md entry and Open decisions row): the published nowcast is now pessimistic for two reasons in Ariva's reading of contract V1, which would hold against a field AMAN: (a) `LaneCycle` is per transaction while F8's c is per person (20 percent low throughput at 1.25 people a transaction); (b) idle time in a start-to-start cycle after each lull. Decisions: (i) a per-person lane cycle time in F10 (a published nowcast change, its own story); (ii) idle time in `MeanCycleSeconds` (contract wording with AMAN, or a bound in Ariva); (iii) the criterion "median within 10 percent" is met at capacity, not over all minutes.
- Golden replay: unchanged, 3cce63edfb17660f53a3fb7eead6c8ae92b20c491135b406a7df8cb032199a6f over 3,202 outputs (a replay has no desk term and no AMAN input); reference scenario golden unchanged (the scenario is not changed).
- Tests: unit `AmanIntervalScenarioTests` (new: per lane tracking at capacity, idle time over the evening, day totals and contract bounds, determinism and a cycle that is the desk's own, family and variation distributions); `SensorCycleScenarioTests` (AMAN intervals carry documents and the unpublished working-time cycle; the published lane cycle time is no longer constant; the owner's two variants measured); `FeedEmulatorTests.Intervals_Should_AddUpToWhatTheScenarioProcessed_When_SummedOverAnHour` tolerance widened from the larger of 8 percent and 6 people to the larger of 12 percent and 10 (an hour's edges carry the families in progress; the day adds up within 4 percent). Pact consumer and provider harness tests (`AmanFeedPactTests`, ARV-068), `ImmigrationRulesTests` and the data boundary tests pass unchanged (no identifier member; counts and times only).
- Docs: simulator README (Emulators); formulas F8 (measured paragraph) and F10 (attribution and the open consequences); decisions.md (entry and Open decisions row).
- Gates (2026-10-07): backend PASS (2,834 unit tests); integration PASS, 390 passed and 1 skipped (full run); scan.mjs 0 errors (the 6 pending allowlist warnings as before); docs PASS; quick PASS; e2e PASS, 528 passed and 1 skipped (full run: the simulator's HTTP surface is unchanged, its AMAN values changed). Backend rerun PASS after the last comment edits.
- Security review (CWE-501, 2026-10-07): verdict PASS, no required fix. Every emitted record of a whole day passes ImmigrationRules (contract V1 bounds) with realistic values; the per-day cache is keyed by the ScenarioDay instance (weak table), never by request input; draws are deterministic; the simulator's HTTP surface, auth and allowlist are unchanged and it is not deployed in production (`simulationEnabled: false`). The loosened hourly tolerance in FeedEmulatorTests is backed by a tighter whole-day conservation check. Owner decisions 2026-10-07: accepted with the tracking criterion measured at capacity; the F10 per-person and idle-time fix is ARV-117d, before the AUH seed.
- Learnings: the scenario is a fluid model with long lulls between waves, so any start-to-start cycle "while open" is dominated by idle time outside the waves; FNV of near-identical strings is a poor uniform source for per-transaction draws (the mean variation came out high), `ScenarioMath.H3` is the simulator's proper one.

## 2026-10-07 ARV-117d Published nowcast: AMAN cycle time per person in working time (MVP, branch story/mvp-phase1; passes)
- Summary: F10's lane cycle time is now per person and leaves idle time out. `LaneCycle.PerPerson` (Ariva.Core/Desks/LaneCycle.cs, pure; moved out of DeskStateEngine.cs) takes each AMAN interval as `DeskIntervalSample` (transactions, documents, mean and P90 service, mean cycle; no key), checks it against contract V1's bounds (`WithinContract`, the `ImmigrationRules` bounds, CWE-501), and gives c = sum(MeanService x Transactions) / sum(Documents) / 60 with a `LaneCycleResult` (minutes, intervals, refused, documents, `LaneCycleFallback` NoIntervals or NoDocuments). `DeskTerms.Compute` has an overload taking the result: when any interval was read, c is its value or none (no fallback to the desk minutes' open time per transaction), and refused intervals flag the published term; with no interval rows the old fallback stays. `DeskTerm.LaneCycle` carries the result (published term only). `DeskTermSource` reads documents, mean and P90 service with the cycle, passes them through `LaneCycle.PerPerson` with `CycleMethod` (MeanService), counts `RefusedIntervals` and logs the site and the count only. The old reading is kept as `LaneCycle.PerTransaction` for the comparisons only.
- Method (docs/product/decisions.md, Proposed pending the owner): mean service time per document, chosen over the cycle capped at the interval's P90 service. The plain cap is biased low: with one or two transactions in a one-minute interval its P90 is its own transaction's service while its cycle is the previous one's, so the cap takes the smaller of two consecutive times. Floored at the mean service (kept in code as `LaneCycleMethod.CycleCappedAtP90`, unused) it measured no better. What mean service gives up: the walk-up gap (D4), absent from the simulator; the shadow's sensor cycle time has the same bias, so the pilot compares like with like.
- Comparison (reference evening, seed 9303, 17:20 to 20:25, Visitors desks all AMAN, AMAN live; Ariva's own simulator, not field data; `SensorCycleScenarioTests.Published_Should_TakeTheLaneCyclePerPersonInWorkingTime_When_AmanPublishesFamiliesAndLulls`):

  | Nowcast | Lane cycle time median (range), min | MAE, min | Bias, min | Minutes (no number) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Published, per person in mean service (ARV-117d, built) | 1.51 (1.08 to 2.08) | 0.63 | +0.31 | 111 (1) |
  | Published, cycle capped at P90 service (rejected) | 1.14 (0.38 to 2.00) | 0.82 | -0.53 | 111 (1) |
  | Published, cap floored at mean service (alternative, unused) | 1.52 (1.08 to 2.08) | 0.65 | +0.35 | 111 (1) |
  | Published before (per transaction, idle included) | 2.11 (1.02 to 23.13) | 1.85 | +1.85 | 111 (1) |
  | Published per person, idle kept (comparison) | 1.70 (0.38 to 23.13) | 1.22 | +1.11 | 111 (1) |
  | Published per person in working time (not published by AMAN) | 1.49 (0.36 to 2.03) | 0.63 | +0.28 | 111 (1) |
  | Shadow with the sensor cycle time (unchanged) | | 0.74 | +0.53 | 109 (3) |
  | Exit term alone (shadow without a cycle time, unchanged) | | 0.81 | +0.61 | 108 (4) |

  Per lane over the reference day (`AmanIntervalScenarioTests`): the published lane cycle time over the scenario's service time has median 0.92 to 0.99 over all minutes and 0.96 to 1.03 at capacity (A-VIS 0.95 and 0.96; A-CRW 0.94; D-CRW 0.92).
- Shadow unaffected: the shadow's every value on the evening is identical with the old and the new published lane cycle time (scenario test); the sensor-only part of the term is identical whatever the lane cycle time, and a refused interval does not flag it (unit). The published rows still do not depend on the sensor cycle time (ARV-117b's test unchanged).
- Golden replay: unchanged, 3cce63edfb17660f53a3fb7eead6c8ae92b20c491135b406a7df8cb032199a6f over 3,202 outputs (a replay has no desk term); reference scenario golden unchanged. Live snapshot shape unchanged (the lane cycle result is on the desk term only); published values at AMAN sites change.
- Tests: unit `LaneCycleTests` (new: the 13-row F10 table with ARV-064's reading beside it; the alternative method; refused method and null input; `PerTransaction`; F8 desk term and nowcast: families 6.0 min, lull 7.5 and 7.89 with exits, ARV-064's 57.5; zero documents, refused, partly refused and no intervals; the shadow term unchanged), `SensorCycleScenarioTests` (published median within 20 percent of the service time; the comparison above; shadow identical; deterministic), `AmanIntervalScenarioTests` (the published reading tracks the service time at capacity and over all minutes, the idle crew desk included); the old `LaneCycle` test moved from `DeskStateEngineTests`. Integration `ImmigrationIntakeTests.DeskTerms_Should_GiveEachQueueTheDesksOfItsLaneOnItsLevel_When_TheStreamAsks` (families and a lull: (4 x 80 + 100) / 6 s a person; a NaN service time planted with the column check dropped and restored: left out, counted, the term flagged, c from the rest, one warning with site and count only and no value).
- Docs: formulas F8 (c per person, the AMAN exception to D4's cycle time, implementation paragraph, an F8 test case, the measured paragraph) and F10 (Lane cycle time per person: formula, symbols, reasons, rules, the table of cases); decisions.md (entry and Open decisions row); data-boundary (the three V1 fields' use); wiki 15 (Nowcast: published waits drop at AMAN sites; service and cycle times); pilot-to-confirm TC-84 (AMAN: semantics of MeanCycleSeconds, DocumentsProcessed and MeanServiceSeconds; blocks go-live); simulator README.
- Gates (2026-10-07): backend PASS (2,853 unit tests; golden replay unchanged); integration PASS, 390 passed and 1 skipped (full run); scan.mjs 0 errors (the 6 pending allowlist warnings as before); docs PASS; e2e full run 527 passed, 1 skipped, 1 failed: `live-under-load.spec.ts` (the ARV-071 burst of planted Redis snapshots to 41 screens, no desk term or Stream involved; it also failed intermittently in the ARV-118 runs) failed twice in the full run and passed alone on rerun (1 passed); quick PASS. After a log-message rewording in `DeskTermSource`: backend PASS again (2,853) and `ImmigrationIntakeTests` PASS (26).
- Security review (CWE-501): verdict PASS (2026-10-07); Low test-hygiene item (plant and restore in try/finally) fixed; INFO on per-lane refusal counts fixed. The NaN plant's assertions are in try/finally; the finally deletes the planted row as admin and re-adds script 0030's check only when pg_constraint lacks it (`RestoreIntervalCheckAsync`); the whole desk terms test is in an outer try/finally that also re-adds script 0044's two sensor-minute checks when missing (`RestoreSensorChecksAsync`, the same defect in ARV-117b's plant) and runs the end-of-test clean, with `CancellationToken.None` so a cancelled run still cleans. `DeskTermSource` now reads each interval's row id and counts refused intervals once per row over the lanes with a term (a desk listed under several lanes still flags each lane's term); the integration test makes AR-08 a Citizens desk as well and asserts one refusal and one warning with Count 1 (it read 2 before the fix); F10's rules in formulas.md say so.
- Owner decisions (Ahmad Al-Sadder, 2026-10-07; docs/product/decisions.md): the mean service per document method is accepted as Proposed; the walk-up gap is to be re-measured on pilot data (ARV-104), with the cycle capped at P90 and floored at the mean service (`LaneCycleMethod.CycleCappedAtP90`) kept in code as the switch (`DeskTermSource.CycleMethod`) if the gap is long. Still proposed: the fallback and flag rules (no documents: F8 fallback without flagging the term; refused intervals flag it; no per-transaction fallback once AMAN intervals were read); TC-84 with AMAN.
- Gates after the review fixes (2026-10-07): `ImmigrationIntakeTests` PASS (26); backend PASS (2,853 unit tests; golden replay unchanged); integration PASS, 390 passed and 1 skipped (full run); scan.mjs 0 errors, 6 warnings (the pending allowlist entries as before); quick PASS.
- Learnings: a per-interval cap mixes a cycle (the previous transaction's time) with this interval's service bound, which at one transaction a minute is biased low by construction; mean service per document needs no cap and no assumption about what a desk does between passengers.
- Focused security re-look of the post-review changes (row-id dedupe in DeskTermSource, try/finally restores in ImmigrationIntakeTests), 2026-10-07: PASS, no required fix; a code comment corrected to say refused rows are counted over the lanes that have desk minutes in the read.

## 2026-10-07 E2E live-under-load flake: root cause and fix (branch story/mvp-phase1; not a story)
- Symptom: `tests/functional/live-under-load.spec.ts` timed out with the zone row at "Load Zone No data yet" (ARV-117d and ARV-118 full runs; alone about one run in three). Not a lost last message: the page had received none of the 50 snapshots.
- Reproduced before any change: alone, `--repeat-each=20`, 4 of the 6 runs that got past sign-in failed the same way (the other 14 were refused sign-ins, see the test gotcha below). A probe recorded the frames the page's own hub socket sent: in every failing run the other 40 screens had the last snapshot (Redis, the relay and the hub delivered everything), and the page sent `LeaveZone("<load site>/Load Zone")` and then `JoinZone` for the demo airport's 16 zone names keyed with the load site's code (`LWABKZ/A-CIT` ...), which the hub refuses; the load site's zone was never joined. Passing runs joined the 16 `DMO/...` zones, left them, then joined the load site's zone.
- Root cause, two product defects in Ariva.Web that interleave when a site is chosen while the screen's first site (the demo airport, chosen on mount) is still loading. (1) The live operations page's `loadSite()` read the `siteCode` state after its awaits: the first load built its zone keys with the newly chosen site's code and still called `live.watch`. (2) `LiveConnection.watch()` was not serialised: a watch made while the first was inside `connection.start()` found the connection Connecting, so `connect()` returned and `leaveAll`/`joinAll` did nothing (not Connected); the first watch then resumed, left the second's zones and joined its own earlier list. The screen showed "connected" and watched nothing of the site on screen until a reconnect. Under load the first site's REST reads and the hub start take longer, so the test's site change lands inside that window more often. The hub, the relay lease, Redis keys, TTLs, Cronz and other specs were not involved.
- Fix: `Live.svelte.ts` runs connect, join and leave one step at a time (`serially`) towards the latest watch, and tracks what the current connection has joined (`joinedZones`, `joinedAlerts`; reset on reconnect, since the hub knows a reconnected connection under a new id); a zone is counted as joined before asking, so a join that fails late is still left. `(modules)/+page.svelte` reads and keys everything with the site the load began with and stops, changing nothing, once another site is chosen; the alert, desk and wave loads drop a stale site's answer. `(modules)/immigration/+page.svelte` has the same guard (`load()` returns false for a stale site).
- Tests: the spec is now serial with one load site provisioned in `beforeAll`, and has two deterministic regression tests: the hub's first negotiation held until the load site's zones are on screen (failed 3 of 3 on the old code with "No data yet"), and the demo airport's zone profile read held until the load site is shown (failed 3 of 3 on the old code: the row disappeared). The burst test's assertions are unchanged; no timeout raised, no retry added.
- Test gotcha: a TOTP code is accepted once per step and only after the last step accepted (CWE-287 replay guard), so repeating `provisionLoadSite` within 30 seconds was refused (401). `unusedTotpCode` (tests/support/accounts.ts) takes the next unused step within the one step of skew and waits for the step boundary only when it would be two ahead; Playwright starts a worker process per test here, so the last step is kept in a temp file named by a hash of the run seed and the secret (no secret on disk).
- Gates (2026-10-07): the spec alone, `--repeat-each=30 --retries=0`, 90 of 90 passed (30 consecutive runs of each of its three tests); full `node scripts/verify.mjs e2e` on fresh services PASS, 530 passed, 1 skipped (the accessibility skip as before), no test retried; web PASS (svelte-check, lint, build); E2E `tsc --noEmit` clean; scan.mjs 0 errors (the 6 pending allowlist warnings as before); quick PASS. Backend unchanged.
- Security review (CWE-79, CWE-863, CWE-384, CWE-287 for the test helper), 2026-10-07: PASS, no required fix; the hub still authorizes every join server side and a stale load can no longer put another site's zones on screen. Advisory folded in: the TOTP step file moved from the shared temp folder to the suite's git-ignored `test-results/.totp`, owner-only, never following a planted link, and a stored step more than two steps ahead is ignored. Rerun after it: the spec passed 15 of 15 (`--repeat-each=5 --retries=0`).

## 2026-10-07 ARV-139a Second seed site modelled on a real airport: AUH Terminal A arrivals (illustrative) (MVP, branch story/mvp-phase1; passes)
- Summary: a second demo seed, `AuhTerminalASeed` (Ariva.Infra/Services/Seed), runs after the DMO seed under the same guard (`Seed:DemoTopology`, refused outside vm-local, k8s-dev and k8s-demo by `DemoSeedExtensions`). `DemoSeedService` now runs every registered `IDemoTopologySeed` in order, each in its own scope, unit of work and retry loop (a seed that gives up does not stop the next). Site `AUH-TA` (flagged illustrative), airport `AUH`, terminal `A`, level `ARR` (lower arrivals level), checkpoint `IMM` (Immigration): 38 counters IC-01 to IC-38 and 34 smart gates SG-01 to SG-34 (both counts "reported"), queue zones `A-CRW`, `A-DIP`, `A-CIT`, `A-RES`, `A-GCC`, `A-VIS`, `A-TRF`, `A-EG` with their lanes, entry and exit lines and assumed capacities, overflow bands `A-VIS-OV` and `A-EG-OV`, a staff and a service zone per counter (`IC-nn staff`, `IC-nn service`, linked to the desk; ARV-116 desk zones), 84 sensors in Commissioning without credentials (grid over queues and bands, one desk sensor per four counters of a lane), the illustrative floor plan, and zone profile v1 published by `demo-seed` (86 zones, 18 lines). No AMAN codes (the counters are sensor-derived), no alert rules, users, roles, grants or credentials. Layout in `AuhTerminalALayout.cs` (facts and every assumption labelled), plan drawn from it by `AuhTerminalAPlan.cs` (SVG, 10 px per metre, through `FloorPlanFiles.Inspect` and `IFileStorage`, the upload pipeline's typing and sanitising). Idempotent as DMO: by code, profile only while the site has none (a draft is left alone), plan only while the level never had one, sensors only while the site never had a device and the published profile has their zones; advisory lock 0x41524956_00000139 and the site row lock. Refuses to write into an existing non-illustrative `AUH-TA` or into AUH terminal A of another site.
- Illustrative flag (CWE-269): `Site.IsIllustrative`, set only by `Site.CreateIllustrative` (a unit test fails when anything outside Ariva.Infra/Services/Seed calls it; no method changes it; the create and update site requests have no such member). Script `0046_site_illustrative.sql` (SHA-256 61226b65ad18f0bc9b1d2e8efd8be7bcf96bf73cac3cd8b1d5ed517ded54dde0 in checksums.lock) adds `site.is_illustrative` and revokes the runtime role's UPDATE on `site`, granting it back on `name` and `modified_*` only (NHibernate's dynamic update writes only those on a rename; `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` still works). `SiteViewModel.Illustrative` and `DisplayBoardViewModel.Illustrative` carry it out read-only.
- Web (CWE-79): `IllustrativeBanner.svelte` (role note, status warning tokens, text from `site.illustrative.title` and `.detail` in en and ar) under the header of every site screen (live operations, immigration, zones, devices, topology, alert rules, displays, reports); the passenger display shows board key `illustrative` in English and Arabic under its title for an illustrative site. Text interpolation only.
- Assumptions (also in the seed source and docs/demo/auh-terminal-a.md): level extent 200 by 150 m and floor 0; hall arrangement (flow along x, gates at low y, one counter line); pitches 2.4 m (counters) and 1.2 m (gates) and every position; lane split CRW 2, DIP 1, CIT 4, RES 6, GCC 6, VIS 17, TRF 2 counters; every smart gate on EG only; the transfer lane itself; the two overflow bands; capacities at 1.2 m2 per person; sensors (overhead stereo at 5 m, BOQ footprint, grid placement); the profile's name and version; the plan's drawing. Public facts are cited by publication only ("URL to confirm"): the network blocked every publisher site, so no URL was verified.
- Tests: unit `AuhTerminalASeedTests` (profile validates; 8 queues with their lanes, 2 bands, 38 staff and 38 service zones linked to their desks and lane queues; zone keys fit ARV-114c; hash stable; counts, lane split and bounds (CWE-120: zones, lines, vertices inside the level, capacities, at most 200 sensors); every sensor reaches its zone and every queue and band vertex is under a footprint; the plan passes the floor plan inspection at 2000 by 1500; data boundary: no identifier-like text and no real staff or system name in any seeded string or the plan's text; the flag's setter, methods, request models and callers), `DemoTopologySeedTests` (both seeds registered in order; the service runs the second after the first gives up). Integration `IllustrativeSeedTests` (DMO and AUH-TA seeded, AUH-TA twice concurrently, then both again: nothing created, updated or audited; DMO unchanged; counts, lanes, desk zones named after their desks, no AMAN codes, sensors without credentials, the plan's stored bytes match its hash; `ZoneGeometrySource` links 34 desk zones to A-VIS, 4 to A-CRW, none to A-EG; a runtime login made by `ariva_ensure_runtime_login` renames the site through `SvcSites` and re-runs the seed with its row lock but cannot set, clear or recode the flag or delete the site (42501), and the `Site` mapping keeps dynamic update; a non-illustrative AUH-TA, and AUH terminal A of another site, stop the seed with nothing written and no file in storage). E2E `tests/api/illustrative-site.spec.ts` (flags in the sites API; create and update cannot set or clear the flag under five spellings and several value types; 401, 403, 404 for another site without data; SQL and markup payloads in the code; the seeded profile and plan through the API; an AUH-TA board says illustrative, a DMO board does not, the board stays device-authenticated) and `tests/functional/illustrative-site.spec.ts` (every site screen: banner for AUH-TA in English and Arabic with RTL, none for DMO; the passenger display note in both languages for AUH-TA, none for DMO; no console errors or CSP violations). - Security review: first verdict FAIL on two Medium test gaps and one Low docs gap (no code defect). (1) Medium, CWE-269: the runtime role test used `SET LOCAL ROLE ariva_runtime` on an admin connection and a hand-written UPDATE. Fixed: `IllustrativeSeedTests.RuntimeLogin_Should_RenameAndLockASiteButNeverSetOrClearTheIllustrativeFlag_When_ConnectedAsTheHostsAre` now creates a real login with `ariva_ensure_runtime_login` and, under it (a second `AccountsHost` with `Database:Username` and `Database:Password` set to that login), renames AUH-TA through `SvcSites.UpdateAsync` and NHibernate (succeeds, flag still true), re-runs the AUH seed (its `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on site; creates nothing), and fails with 42501 on the four forbidden statements (clear the flag, set it, recode the site, delete it); it also asserts that the `Site` persister uses dynamic update (checked: with `EnableDynamicUpdate` off the test fails). (2) Medium, CWE-269/863: the seed's refusal of AUH terminal A bound to another site had no test. Fixed: `Seed_Should_StopWithoutWriting_When_TerminalABelongsToAnotherSite` (new database `IllustrativeSeedForeignTerminal`) creates site OTH, airport AUH and terminal A of OTH through the services, runs the seed, expects InvalidOperationException, and checks the whole snapshot is unchanged (no AUH-TA row, no level, desk, zone, profile, plan, device or audit entry) and that no file is left in storage. No seed change: the terminal check comes before the plan is written, so no file reaches storage (and the site saved before the check is rolled back with the transaction). (3) Low, docs: docs/security/cwe-controls.md CWE-269 row (Control: the column privileges of script 0046, the flag set only by the seed, the seed's two refusals; Test: `IllustrativeSeedTests`, `AuhTerminalASeedTests`, e2e `api/illustrative-site.spec.ts`), docs/security/asvs-l2.md V8.1.2 (field-level write rule for `site.is_illustrative`) and V13.2.2 (column-level privilege example); no status change. Security re-review (2026-10-07): PASS, all three findings closed, no new finding; full integration gate after the fixes: 394 passed, 1 skipped.

Recorded in docs/product/decisions.md and docs/demo/auh-terminal-a.md. The other proposed points (URLs to confirm, demo users stay on DMO, the flag can never be cleared) stay as recorded.

- Learnings: column privileges on a table the runtime role already updates need a table-level REVOKE first, then column GRANTs; NHibernate's dynamic update keeps a rename within them. The account seed of vm-local creates the sites its accounts name, so a test account must not name a site only a seed may create.

The real-time demo project (`ARIVA_E2E_DEMO=1 --project=demo`, not part of the e2e gate), on fresh services: over 18:00 to 19:36, DMO 18:05, 18:20 and 19:10 and AUH-TA 18:12 (A-VIS over target under the banner, R-001) and 18:25 (Q-RES-04 Offline, R-003) passed through the whole pipeline (Ingest, Stream, the hub; nothing planted); Stream also raised AUH-TA's R-002 for A-EG at demo 19:15, but the 19:13 check ran after DMO's 19:10 check (which waits for its bin to be judged, here until 19:28), when the alert had resolved; with the checks reordered by event time and the overflow alert looked up through the alerts API, a second run over 19:00 to 19:25 passed it (A-EG 304 people, R-002 raised). The spec also reads an estimated wait ("≈", AUH-TA has no AMAN cycle time) as a number.
- Security review: not yet run (CWE-306, CWE-287 to review).
- Deferred: the AMAN emulator for AUH-TA (decision pending; it would need AMAN codes for the counters and gates, which the seed does not create, and V1 lanes for GCC, DIP and TRF, ARV-130); the visual baselines (ARV-075) not rerun (no screen changed; the AUH-TA screens show data only while a demo plays); the k8s-demo cluster plays both sites with no chart change (one simulator replica), not verified on a cluster.
- Learnings: making a ported, bit-exact engine site-aware is safe with instance members named as the old statics (C# finds members of the type before `using static` imports, so every static method that touched site data fails to compile until it becomes an instance method), and the parity fingerprints catch any drift; a real-time demo spec with several sites must run its checks in event order, since a check waiting for a judged bin can hold the serial queue past another site's short-lived alert; AUH-TA's waits are estimates (exit term, F8) because a site without AMAN has no cycle time, so on screen they carry "≈" and read higher than the scenario's staffed-rate nowcast (40 to 45 minutes against 29 at the A-VIS peak).

## 2026-10-07 ARV-139b follow-up: the AUH-TA replay's first breach depended on the devices' ids (branch story/mvp-phase1)
- Symptom: `AuhTerminalAReplayTests.Replay_Should_PassFifteenMinutesAt1809_When_TheAuhVisitorWaveArrives` passed one run and failed the next with the same build (18:09 or 18:12). Not wall time, shared statics or floating point: the ingested records were identical run to run except for their Guids (the test drew device ids with `Guid.CreateVersion7()`, and Ingest derives each batch id from the device id), and eight runs gave eight different output hashes (3,789 or 3,790 outputs).
- Root cause (product, `Ariva.Core/Queueing/QueueStateEngine`): the replay orders records by receive time, kind, then batch id, so the A-VIS zone reading (Q-VIS-01) and the A-VIS-OV band reading (O-VIS-01) of the same instant came in an order set by the ids; in the stream the order is whichever batch Kafka delivers first. F5 re-anchoring was checked as each reading was applied: at 18:06:00 (zone 0 then 10, band 0) a band reading applied first was added to the zone's previous 0, the engine took the queue for empty, dropped the 10 people it held (Fragmented) and counted their exits as duplicates, so minute 18:06 lost 7 exits, the exit throughput fell (3.8 against 5.2 a minute) and the nowcast passed 15 minutes three minutes early. The 18:09 written into the story was that fault, not a property of the exit term.
- Fix: re-anchoring now runs once every event of the reading's instant has been applied (`Reanchor`, called from `ProcessUpTo` when the next event's time differs, at the end of the run, and before a full step returns, so nothing new enters the snapshot). The test's devices have fixed ids (as `ReferenceReplay`'s), and a new test ingests the evening again with other ids, checks that the ties come out in another order and that the output hash is the same. Engine tests: zone and band read at the same instant in either order (no re-anchor while the zone holds people; exactly one when both read 0). The new tests fail on the old engine. Added after the review (`Restore_Should_ContinueLikeAContinuousRun_When_AFullStepSplitsTheReadingsOfAnInstant`): with MaxStepRecords at 1,000 a step fills between the band's and the zone's readings of one instant (band first), and the engine restored there from its JSON snapshot gives the same steps as a continuous run. The test also records the bound the review accepted: the full step checks F5 with the instant's events applied so far, so there the band's 0 beside the zone's previous 0 still re-anchors although the zone reads 50 at that instant (108 people fragmented and their exits counted as duplicates; 992 waits against 1,100 when the step is not split). It needs a step of MaxStepRecords (100,000 by default) to end inside such an instant; carrying the pending check in the snapshot would remove it.
- Values: Ariva's first A-VIS breach is 18:12 (the scenario's minute too); the test, docs/demo/auh-terminal-a.md, the simulator's Scenarios/README.md, wiki 10, docs/product/decisions.md (ARV-139b point 8) and formulas.md (F5 implementation) say so. Seed 9303's golden replay unchanged (3cce63ed... over 3,202; no band device in it). The overflow case's lock changes, approved by the owner (Ahmad Al-Sadder) on 2026-10-07 (ARV-139b re-approval): d971a3170539758f5248bfcc01e181e3bfd9ab1aa51dfba638d8908be9948b9a over 1,692 becomes 8df69d9f46b3eb15d871b5e0e37c82f0c52ac62d2f1db6e022b0df0d7db569ba over 1,693 (its band reading was ordered first at 17:59 and 19:28, so the exits at the start of each wave now count; overflow rows and changes, line minutes, the outage and queue lengths byte-identical). By type, 1,693 outputs: 567 minute (566 before), 224 bin, 224 health, 211 live, 253 line, 1 outage, 209 overflow, 4 overflow-change. The one added output is a further provisional revision of minute 19:27 (18 entries, 8 exits, 18 waits): the 10 people who entered 19:27:27 to 19:27:58, dropped at 19:28:00 before, now exit 19:28:01 to 19:29:06 across two steps (to 19:28:30 and to 19:29:30), so minute 19:27's waits go from 8 to 13 and then 18 with a provisional row each, where they stayed at 8; at 17:59 the 2 people held exit within one step, so no row is added there (both ledgers exported and compared per type and key). AUH-TA fingerprints in auh-terminal-a-golden.json unchanged (the simulator's outputs).
- Gates (2026-10-07): `AuhTerminalAReplayTests` 20 of 20 consecutive runs; `GoldenReplayTests`, `OverflowReplayTests` and `AuhTerminalAScenarioTests` 20 of 20 (locked values each run); backend PASS twice in a row (2,920 unit tests); integration PASS (394, 1 skipped); scan and quick below. E2E not run: no scenario or simulator code changed.
- Learnings: a replay's tie-break by id is deterministic for one archive but not across archives of the same pushes, so engine state must never depend on the order of simultaneous records from different devices; a test that draws ids per run turns such a dependency into a flake, which is how this one showed.

Follow-ups recorded (Low): one ACRIS key shared by both sites' simulated AODB routes; no test that a refused call on one site's AODB leaves the other's token untouched.

## 2026-10-08 ARV-114d Queue engine: carry the pending empty-queue check across a full step (MVP, branch story/mvp-phase1; passes)
- Summary: a full step (`MaxStepRecords`) that ends inside an instant no longer runs the F5 empty-queue check with part of that instant applied. At a full step the only check that can still be due is the one of the next event's instant (any other ran at the top of the loop), so `QueueStateEngine.ProcessUpTo` now returns with it still pending (`_anchorDueUtc`, the instant one tick after the watermark where the step stopped), and the next step runs it once the rest of the instant has been applied, exactly as an unsplit step does. The check is part of the snapshot: `QueueEngineState.PendingAnchorUtc` (null when none) and `ZoneProcessorState` version 8 (`PendingAnchorSinceVersion`). The engine's own `QueueEngineState.CurrentVersion` stays 1, as for the ARV-113 and ARV-114a additions to it (an absent property reads as null).
- Restore checks (CWE-501): `QueueStateEngine.Restore` accepts a pending check only where a full step leaves one: a plausible UTC time (2000 to 9000), exactly one tick after the watermark, equal to the cursor (the last processed event) and the time of a held occupancy reading of the queue's own zones; anything else is an `InvalidDataException`. `SnapshotChecks` also refuses one in a snapshot older than version 8, in a zone that never stepped, after the zone's reference clock, or implausible against the host's clock. An older snapshot restores with no check pending (the engine that wrote it ran the check when the step filled). Bound (CWE-120): one nullable time per zone.
- Story wording: the criterion says "an aligned plausible instant not after the watermark". By construction the pending instant is exactly one tick after the watermark: the full step stops the watermark one tick before the unprocessed events so that none of them can become late, and moving it onto the instant would make the instant's remaining events late. The check is therefore "exactly the tick after the watermark": an instant at or before the watermark (applied whole, its check already run) and any later instant are both refused.
- Tests: `QueueStateEngineTests.Restore_Should_ContinueLikeAContinuousRun_When_AFullStepSplitsTheReadingsOfAnInstant` now asserts the unsplit result for the split run, continuous and restored from JSON (no re-anchor, no resolution so no Fragmented, no duplicate, the unsplit run's 1,100 waits in the same order; the snapshot after the full step carries 18:02 and the one after the next step none); the KNOWN DEFECT note is gone. New: `Reanchor_Should_RunInTheNextStep_When_AFullStepSplitsAnInstantWhereEveryZoneReadsZero` (the carried check still re-anchors once, in the next step: 50 Fragmented at 18:02, as unsplit), `Restore_Should_RefuseAPendingCheck_When_NoFullStepLeavesItThere` (11 hostile cases: at, before, two ticks after and a minute after the watermark; cursor moved; no reading of the instant; only another zone's reading; years 9500 and 1990 with everything else aligned; start and end of the calendar), `Restore_Should_RunNoPendingCheck_When_TheSnapshotWasWrittenBeforeItCarriedOne`, and `ZoneProcessorTests.Restore_Should_CarryThePendingEmptyQueueCheck_When_TheSnapshotIsVersion8_And_RefuseItOtherwise` (the check survives the zone snapshot and the next tick runs it; version 7 restores without one and is refused with one; a check after the zone's clock, in a zone that never stepped, misaligned, or after the host's clock is refused). With the old full-step call put back, 14 of the 15 new cases fail (the 15th, the old-snapshot path, behaves the same on both).
- Golden values unchanged: seed 9303 golden replay 3cce63edfb17660f53a3fb7eead6c8ae92b20c491135b406a7df8cb032199a6f over 3,202 outputs; overflow case 8df69d9f46b3eb15d871b5e0e37c82f0c52ac62d2f1db6e022b0df0d7db569ba over 1,693 outputs; AUH-TA replay output head 1fcf8f6b42260af45c603df2484eecdb0dc29f6b5b11ee139f880eca5e0f97b6 over 3,790 outputs before and after the change (that replay has no locked hash: measured with a temporary probe test, removed). An output changes only where a step of 100,000 records ends inside an instant, which none of these replays has.
- Docs: formulas.md F5 (implementation paragraph), docs/product/decisions.md (ARV-139b point 8: the accepted bound is removed by ARV-114d).
- Gates (2026-10-08): backend PASS (build with the security analyzers, the reference golden up to date, 2,935 unit tests, scanner self-test, scan, base images, chart security, NuGet and npm audits; the first run failed only `DeskZoneTests.ZoneProcessor_Should_ContinueExactly_When_RestoredFromASnapshotWithDeskZones`, which pinned the zone snapshot version at 7 and now uses `ZoneProcessorState.CurrentVersion`); integration PASS (394 passed, 1 skipped, as before); `node scripts/security/scan.mjs` 0 errors (1,065 files; the 6 pending allowlist warnings, in files this story does not touch); docs PASS; quick PASS. E2E not run: no API, hub or web change.
- Security review (CWE-501, CWE-120), 2026-10-08: PASS, no required fix. Folded in: restore also requires the earliest buffered event to be at the pending instant (the event that stopped the step stays buffered), with a tampered case for a check whose instant has no buffered event left. Notes: the acceptance criterion's 'not after the watermark' corrected in the PRD to 'exactly one tick after the watermark', which is where a full step stops; a rollback to a build at snapshot version 7 refuses version 8 snapshots and those zones start afresh (logged), as with earlier version bumps.
- Learnings: the restored engine's invariants make a tight check possible for a value a snapshot could otherwise abuse (a pending check equals the watermark plus one tick, the cursor and a held reading's time), so a tampered snapshot cannot make the engine drop people at an instant of its choosing.

## 2026-10-08 Intermittent unit test: root cause and fix (branch story/mvp-phase1; not a story)
- Symptom: one `node scripts/verify.mjs backend` run failed its "unit tests" step with no failing test captured, and the next three full runs passed (2,936 tests); during ARV-116 `AmanFeedPactTests` failed once on "a race in its output collector" and passed on rerun.
- Hunt (trx per run): 15 full runs of Ariva.UnitTests and 4 more under CPU load (four busy loops, plus a second test process running `AmanFeedPactTests` alone in a loop): all passed. That second process, the real test host, died in 1 of 369 runs under the load with `Unhandled exception. System.InvalidOperationException: Please call the Start() method before calling this method.` at `HttpListener.BeginGetContext`, `HttpListener.GetContext()`, `PactNet.Verifier.Messaging.MessagingProvider.HandleRequest()`: the process ended, with no test summary and no failed test, which is what a "unit tests" step that names no test looks like.
- Root cause (the test's use of PactNet 5.0.1; PactNet's main branch has the same code): `WithMessages` starts an HttpListener and a foreground thread (`new Thread`) that loops `while (server.IsListening) { try { server.GetContext(); } catch (HttpListenerException) { break; } ... }` and writes "Successfully simulated message ..." to the outputters after it has answered. `Verify` disposed the verifier (`using var`) as soon as the Pact FFI returned; disposal stops, then closes, the listener. In .NET 10's managed HttpListener a `GetContext` call on a stopped listener throws InvalidOperationException, on a closed one ObjectDisposedException; only a call already waiting is ended with an HttpListenerException. A disposal that lands between the thread's `IsListening` check and its wait therefore kills the thread with an exception nothing catches, and the runtime ends the test host. The same thread also appended to the test's plain StringBuilder while the test thread appended or called `ToString` (the ARV-116 symptom: a StringBuilder used from two threads throws ArgumentOutOfRangeException or loses text).
- Evidence (scratch harness on PactNet 5.0.1 with the published pact, the test's Verify sequence): a probe outputter saw the listener write in every verification; its last line came after `Verify` had returned in 28 of 200 verifications beside a suite run and 160 of 1,000 under six busy loops, with writes from both threads overlapping. Holding the listener in its last line until `Verify` returned (what preemption does) and then disposing after a swept delay aborted the process (exit 134, InvalidOperationException or ObjectDisposedException from `MessagingProvider.HandleRequest`) in 2 of 5 runs of 3,000 verifications, and 2 of 3 runs of 2,000 under load. The old collector given the new test's workload (two threads, 20,000 lines each) failed 2 of 5 runs (ArgumentOutOfRangeException from `ToString`; from `AppendLine` with 19,890 lines lost).
- Fix (test code; no product change; no retry, skip, longer timeout or collection): `Verify` disposes the verifier through its collector, `Collect.DisposeWithTheListenerHeld`. The collector locks every access, and holds PactNet's listener thread in its "Successfully simulated message" line until the verifier is disposed; let go, its loop finds the listener stopped and ends. A message the listener took but answered with an error has no such line, so that case waits at most 10 s, by which time the thread waits inside `GetContext`, where disposal ends it with the HttpListenerException it catches. New tests: `WriteLine_Should_KeepEveryLine_When_TwoThreadsWriteAtOnce` and `DisposeWithTheListenerHeld_Should_DisposeTheVerifierBeforeTheListenerLeavesItsLastLine_When_TheMessageWasSent` (fails with the hold removed: "found True"). Wiki 08: a note for AMAN's harness on disposing PactNet's verifier.
- Proof: harness with the fixed collector and random delays on both threads: 15,000 verifications, then 6,000 under load (1,200 of them broken contracts), no abort, every output with its verdict. Suite after the fix: 15 full runs (10 idle, 5 under the same load with the pact class looping beside them), all passed with 2,938 tests; the pact class alone ran 444 times in the real host under that load with no abort (before: 1 in 369).
- Also seen, not fixed (never a failure, exit code 0): in 4 of 34 full runs the VSTest results lost one theory row: base runs 4 and 7 reported 2,935 of 2,936 (a row of `AlertRuleTests.Rule_Should_BeRefused...` and of `EngineSettingsBoundaryTests.Settings_Should_AcceptTheLimits...` missing), and two post-fix runs reported a row as `<unknown test ID ...>`. That name comes from xunit.runner.visualstudio 3.1.4's execution sink when its message metadata cache has no entry for the test; it is reporting, not a test. ARV-114d's "2,935 unit tests" was probably one of these.
- Gates (2026-10-08): backend PASS (build, the reference golden up to date, 2,938 unit tests, scanner self-test, scan, base images, chart security, NuGet and npm audits); `node scripts/security/scan.mjs` 0 errors (1,065 files; the 6 pending allowlist warnings as before, none in the touched files); quick PASS. Integration and E2E not run: test code and wiki only.
- Learnings: a third-party component's own threads are part of a test's concurrency, so read what they do before calling a failure flaky; an unhandled exception on any thread ends the test host and the run names no test, so keep a trx and the console per run, and loop the suspect class alone under load to see it.

## 2026-10-08 ARV-104a Validation campaign and manual count capture API (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1; passes)
- Summary: validation campaigns (Planned, Running, Closed) bound to a site, one published zone profile version (number and geometry hash), the queue zones and lines in scope and 1 to 31 planned local days, and manual counts per line, 15-minute bin and observer. Core: `ValidationCampaign` with `ValidationCampaignZone` and `ValidationCampaignLine` (rich methods `Start`, `Close`, `Capture`, `Correct` and the rules `ScopeProblem`, `CaptureProblem`, `CorrectionProblem`, `AreValidDays`, `AreValidTargets`), `ManualCount` (append-only revisions: a correction is the next revision with a reason and the id it corrects), `ValidationErrors` (404, 409 and 400 texts, none repeating a request value), `ValidationRules` (Fx.Specification), criteria with sort and status allowlists. Infra: `SvcValidationCampaigns` and `SvcValidationCapture` (site checked first, 404 outside; the published version read under a share lock; start and close lock the campaign row; create, start, close and every correction audited in the same unit of work; unique and trigger refusals mapped to 409 with nothing committed). Main: `api/v1/sites/{siteCode}/validation/campaigns` (search, create, get, start, close, counts) and `.../validation/capture/campaigns` (running campaigns with their lines, own counts, capture, correct), `[SiteScoped]`, bodies of at most 32 KB and 4 KB, an optional `Idempotency-Key` that returns the stored count (200) on a resend.
- Permissions and role: `Validation.View`, `Validation.Capture`, `Validation.Manage` (new actions Capture and Manage); BorderShiftSupervisor and TerminalDutyManager hold View and Manage, SystemAdministrator everything but `Validation.Capture` (owner decision below; `RolePermissions.NotForAdministrators`), HandlerStationManager none; new role `ValidationObserver` (rank 1) with `Validation.Capture` only, the only role holding it. Closing is critical (`critical-actions.json`, new category "Close a validation campaign"). `RoleCodes.AlertRoles` keeps alert owners to the four roles alert_rule accepts (script 0020), so the observer can never own an alert rule. The permission matrix has a ValidationObserver column on every row (403 on every permission row it lacks, as HandlerStationManager elsewhere) and ten new rows. Web: the Users and access screen can grant the role (users.ts role list, English and Arabic labels); no other web change.
- Script 0047_validation_campaign.sql, SHA-256 a794cb4163850447f97f1f52368e404e5db0c7abd9d569db016168ec953414c9 (checksums.lock): `validation_campaign`, `validation_campaign_zone`, `validation_campaign_line`, `manual_count`; new unique constraints `zone_profile (id, site_code, version)` and `line (profile_id, id)` so composite keys hold every scope row and count to the campaign's version and site; checks (15-minute bins, revision 1 without reason, bounds, key shape); unique indexes for one count per line, bin, observer and revision, one correction per revision, and one key per observer; triggers: a campaign is planned over the published version (share lock against a concurrent publish), changes only Planned to Running (while still published) and to Closed, a closed one never changes, nothing is deleted; scope rows only while planned and only queue zones in scope and their lines (names copied and checked); a count only in a running campaign (share lock against a concurrent close) and a correction only as the next revision. Runtime role: no UPDATE, DELETE or TRUNCATE on scope and counts, UPDATE only of a campaign's lifecycle columns.
- Data boundary: people are Ariva user ids only (created, started and closed by, the observer); the campaign does not use the auditable base class (no user name columns). `ValidationTests.Schema_Should_HoldNoNameOrDocumentColumnOfAPerson...` lists the exact columns and finds no person name, contact or document column; a unit test does the same over the persisted properties.
- Decisions (docs/product/decisions.md entry and Open decisions row). Owner decisions (Ahmad Al-Sadder, 2026-10-08): (a) only the ValidationObserver role captures: `Validation.Capture` is removed from System administrators (the "every permission" seed rule leaves it out so that the ground truth stays independent of anyone who configures the system), and the account that created or started a campaign never counts for it, also when it holds a manager role and the observer role (403 `ValidationErrors.OwnCampaign` from the capture service, checked before any replay; script 0047's insert trigger refuses such a row from any statement, 23514); (b) retired profile version accepted as built (counts for bins ended before the retirement stay valid, later bins 409). Still Proposed, as recorded: concurrent first counts and corrections resolve by unique indexes to one stored and one 409, a resend with the same Idempotency-Key gets the stored count; corrections by the observer only, latest revision only, while running; planned days from 31 days back (paper counts) to 366 ahead; lines only on queue zones in scope or their bands; placeholder targets 20 bins per line and 30 tracer runs (TC-04) marked as placeholders; a planned campaign may be closed. Point (9) revised after the first security review: an account whose only role is ValidationObserver is never given every site (`RoleCodes.NeedsNamedSites`; `SvcUsers` on creation and site change, `SvcRoleAssignment` on a grant or revoke that would leave it so; 400 `AdministrationErrors.ObserverNeedsNamedSites`).
- Screens: none of its own (the observer tablet is ARV-104c and ARV-104d, the campaign screens ARV-104h). The gate's "Playwright functional tests for this story" are one functional test that the observer role reaches no operational screen (`functional/validation-observer.spec.ts`: no module in the sidebar, the home screen's no-access text and no API call, no access on /users and /reports, the role named in English and Arabic, clean console and CSP).
- Tests: unit `ValidationCampaignTests` (planning, scope table, days and targets bounds, lifecycle, capture table in Dubai time with the clock tolerance and retirement, corrections, key and crossing rules, persisted properties), `PermissionModelTests` (the three codes, the observer's single grant, the grants per role), `LiveHubTests` (alert audience); integration `ValidationTests` (10 tests, services under a real runtime login: lifecycle with audit, idempotent resend, six concurrent captures keep one, nothing after close and no edit even for the migration login, cross-site 404, version and scope refusals with payloads stored as text, retirement, schema columns, 42501 for the runtime login on every change or delete, keys and triggers on bypassing statements); E2E `api/validation.spec.ts` (create, start, capture, idempotent resend, correct, read, close without and with step-up, capture and correction after close 409, audit; role rows on real ids; cross-site 404; SQL injection and markup in names, text, sorts, statuses, days, bins, counts, keys, reasons and routes: 400 or stored verbatim; 413 for each body), the ValidationObserver column of `permission-matrix.spec.ts`, the close route in `step-up.spec.ts`, and `administration.spec.ts` and `functional/users.spec.ts` updated for the fifth role.
- Docs: formulas F18 (capture of N_manual), data-boundary (retention row), architecture overview (Validation context), decisions.md, pilot-to-confirm TC-04, cwe-controls (CWE-863, 501, 269, 89), asvs-l2 (V8.2.2, V8.2.3), security/README.md (roles, validation bullet), wiki 01 (role row), wiki 07 section 8 (running a campaign), wiki 11 (critical functions, roles, new section 2c), Ariva.Api.Main.http.
- Gates (2026-10-08): backend PASS (3,029 unit tests; build, reference golden, scanner self-test, scan, base images, chart security, NuGet and npm audits); integration PASS, 404 passed and 1 skipped (full run; the first full run failed 47 later tests with 53300 "too many clients": each test of the new class opened its own runtime connection pool, so the class now shares one password and clears its pool after each test); web PASS (svelte-check, lint, build); e2e PASS, 566 passed, 1 skipped, none flaky (full run on fresh services; the first full run failed `administration.spec.ts`'s role list, updated); `node scripts/security/scan.mjs` 0 errors (1,085 files; the 6 pending allowlist warnings as before, none in the touched files); docs PASS; quick PASS.
- Security review, first verdict (2026-10-08): FAIL, one Medium and one Low, both test gaps, no code vulnerability. Medium (CWE-863): no test proved that an Idempotency-Key belongs to one observer (the control: `SvcValidationCapture.ReplayAsync` filters on the observer, and `ux_manual_count_idempotency` is unique on observer and key). Low (CWE-269, CWE-863): no test proved the observer is refused as an alert owner or recipient.
- Fixed after the first review (2026-10-08): (1) Medium: integration `ValidationTests.IdempotencyKey_Should_BelongToOneObserver_When_AnotherObserverSendsTheSameKey` and e2e `validation.spec.ts` "an Idempotency-Key belongs to the observer who sent it": a second observer sending the first one's key with the same body gets 201, its own observerId and Replayed false; the same key with another body gets 201, not 409; the same two cases for a correction key; each observer's resend still returns its own count. (2) Low: `AlertRuleTests` refuses the observer as owner and as escalation role (and accepts each of the four alert roles), `LiveHubTests` names the four alert roles of an unowned notice, asserts the observer is in no audience, and lists notices owned by or escalated to the observer as not plausible. (3) Recommended: `ValidationTests.CloseAndCapture_Should_NeverLeaveACountAfterTheClose_When_TheyRace`, deterministic in both lock orders (a raw runtime connection holds the campaign FOR UPDATE and closes while the service's capture waits on the trigger's share lock: 409 Closed, nothing stored; a raw count holds the share lock while the service's close waits: the count commits first and stays, nothing after); repeated five times, stable. (4) Owner decisions (a) and (b) above, with unit (`ValidationCampaignTests`: creator, starter, planned, correction; `PermissionModelTests`: administrators lack only Capture and audit writes), integration (`ValidationTests.Capture_Should_RefuseTheCampaignsCreatorAndStarter_When_TheyAlsoHoldTheObserverRole`, including the trigger for bypassing statements) and e2e tests (a new dual-role account `e2e.valdual`: 403 as creator and as starter, 201 in another manager's campaign; the administrator's role row 403 on every capture endpoint); the permission matrix's four capture rows now expect 403 for SystemAdministrator. (5) All-sites observer refused, `SiteScopeTests.SiteAccess_Should_NeverBeEverySite_When_TheAccountsOnlyRoleIsValidationObserver` and e2e `administration.spec.ts`. (6) ASVS V2.3.1 (Planned, Running, Closed) and V2.3.4 (one count per line, bin and observer; the close and capture lock order) cite this pathway; PRD notes on ARV-104c (UUID Idempotency-Keys, no names in reasons, XSS probes on names and reasons) and ARV-104h (XSS probes on campaign names and reasons, no names in either, administrators have no capture control). (7) Docs: decisions.md (owner entry, point (9), Open decisions row), wiki 01, 07 (section 8) and 11 (sections 2 and 2c), security/README.md, cwe-controls (CWE-863, CWE-269), asvs-l2 (V2.3.1, V2.3.4, V8.2.2), formulas F18, Ariva.Api.Main.http (a UUID key). Script 0047 changed before any merge (the trigger's creator and starter check), new SHA-256 7f9e0d73090496e2cdc0a611a140b221ae70fcb48e499d2e03f1d42acccd2afb in checksums.lock.
- Gates after the fixes (2026-10-08): backend PASS (3,044 unit tests; build, reference golden, scanner self-test, scan, base images, chart security, NuGet and npm audits); integration PASS, 408 passed and 1 skipped (full run); e2e PASS, 569 passed, 1 skipped, none flaky (full run on fresh services; the permission matrix changed); `node scripts/security/scan.mjs` 0 errors (1,085 files; the same 6 pending allowlist warnings, none in the touched files); docs PASS; quick PASS; web not run (no web change). The backend gate was run again on the final code after comment-only edits, PASS.
- Security re-review (2026-10-08): PASS, both findings closed; no path lets an administrator, or a campaign's creator or starter, capture (service and trigger agree; a role granted later does not help, the check is on stored user ids). Follow-ups (Low): the named-sites rule is checked then written without a per-user lock, so two administrators acting at once could leave an observer-only account with every site (fix: lock the user row in SvcUsers.SetSitesAsync and SvcRoleAssignment grant and revoke); CreateUserRequest.Roles is capped at 4 while there are 5 roles (no security effect; grant the fifth afterwards).
- Deferred: tracer runs and desk observer logs (ARV-104b); the comparison rules for a line's direction and for two observers of one bin (ARV-104e); report freezing at close (ARV-104g); screens (ARV-104c, 104d, 104h). Noted, not changed: `CreateUserRequest.Roles` allows at most 4 roles while there are now 5, so an account with every role is created in two steps (create, then grant).
- Learnings: a BEFORE INSERT trigger runs before foreign keys, so a bypassing statement meets the trigger's check first (tests accept either code where both guard); the API's JSON does not escape markup, so inert means a JSON value with nosniff and the API's CSP, not an escaped string; integration test classes that open their own login pools must share and clear them, the shared server is close to its connection limit. From the review fixes: a race is tested deterministically by holding the first lock on a raw connection (pooling off), waiting until `pg_blocking_pids` shows the second session waiting on it, then committing; a static field initialised after the static property that reads it is still null at that point (`RolePermissions.NotForAdministrators` sits above `SystemAdministrator`); `ORDER BY 1 COLLATE "C"` is an integer expression, not a column position; a rule on who may hold every site must also guard role grants and revokes, or a revoke recreates the state it refuses.

## 2026-10-08 ARV-104b Tracer runs and desk observer log capture API (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1; passes)
- Summary: timed tracers and per-minute desk observer logs bound to a validation campaign, reusing ARV-104a's permissions (`Validation.View` to read, `Validation.Capture` for the observer's side), role, site check first (404 outside the caller's sites, a campaign of another site and another observer's record answer 404), per-observer Idempotency-Keys, the 403 refusal of the campaign's creator or starter (service and triggers), Running-only capture with the same close lock order, audit and runtime privileges. API only: the tracer and desk screens are ARV-104c and ARV-104d, so this story adds no Playwright functional test.
- Core: `TracerBatch` and `TracerRun` (the device's clock reading per batch, the offset measured by the server at receipt as device minus server to the millisecond, refused beyond 5 minutes; each run's device times, the offset and the corrected times; tracer codes `T-` and 2 or 3 ASCII digits, `\z` and `[0-9]` in the .NET pattern), `DeskObservationBatch` and `DeskObservation` (one observer's 15-minute UTC bin, exactly 15 states per desk with null for a minute not observed, revisions with a reason), `ValidationCampaignDesk` (staffed immigration or emigration desks of the site, in service, set at planning; `ValidationCampaign` is now partial with `ValidationCampaign.GroundTruth.cs`: `TracerRunProblem`, `TracerBatchProblem`, `RecordTracerRuns`, `DeskObservationProblem`, `DeskBatchProblem`, `ObserveDesks`, `DeskCorrectionProblem`, `CorrectDeskObservation`, `DeskScopeProblem`, `IsBorderDesk`), `ObservedDeskState` (Closed, Idle, Serving, Paused), `RequestFingerprint` (SHA-256 of a batch without the device's clock reading, so a retry with the clock read again is recognised), new `ValidationErrors` (400 texts never repeat a value; 403 `DesksNeedBorderRole`; 409 `TimeAfterRetirement`, `RunAlreadyRecorded`, `AlreadyObserved`), `ValidationRules` for both batches, corrections and searches (allowlisted sorts), `CreateValidationCampaignRequest.DeskIds` (optional, last parameter; campaigns planned before have no desks).
- Infra and Main: `SvcTracerRuns`, `SvcDeskObservations` (projection: the manager's read of desk states answers only a caller with `BorderDesks.View`, others an empty page marked `desksIncluded` false), `SvcValidationCampaigns` (desks in scope only by a border caller, 403 otherwise; the campaign view lists desks and their observed minutes for border callers only, and tracer runs per zone), `SvcValidationCapture.RunningAsync` (zones, desks, the offset bound); `TracerRunsController` and `DeskObservationsController` (seven endpoints under `api/v1/sites/{siteCode}/validation/...`, `[SiteScoped]`, batches at most 16 KB, corrections 4 KB). All hosts: model binding messages no longer quote a value that failed to bind (`ControllerExtensions.WithoutAttemptedValues`): the first E2E run showed `zoneId=<script>...` coming back in the 400 answer as JSON text (inert, nosniff and the API's CSP, but a reflection the story's "400 without echo" rules out).
- Script 0048_validation_tracers_desk_observations.sql, SHA-256 db3f15569a19206c5b5462c0cb213e4167aa3f11fcde3ff8d6b37f3d5cd8b365 (checksums.lock): `validation_campaign_desk`, `tracer_batch`, `tracer_run`, `desk_observation_batch`, `desk_observation`; `desk (id, site_code)` made unique for the composite key; checks (tracer code pattern, offset within 5 minutes and equal to device clock minus receipt, corrected time equal to raw minus offset, run of at most 3 hours and not in the future beyond a minute, whole minutes, 15-minute bins, four states, revision 1 in a batch without reason and later revisions with one); keys (a run carries its batch's campaign, observer and offset, its zone is in the campaign's scope; a state's desk is in scope); unique indexes (one batch per observer and key, one run per observer, tracer code and device join time, one state per desk, minute, observer and revision, one correction per revision, one correction key per observer); triggers (a desk joins only a planned campaign and only as a live staffed border desk of its site, share-locked; every batch, run and state only in a running campaign under a share lock and never from the creator or starter; a state inside its batch's bin; a correction is the next revision); nothing changes or is deleted (script 0047's `validation_row_immutable`); the runtime role has no UPDATE, DELETE or TRUNCATE on any of the five tables.
- Data boundary: tracers are labels (who carries which stays on the campaign's paper roster), observers Ariva user ids; `GroundTruthTests.Schema_Should_HoldNoNameOrDocumentColumnOfAPerson...` lists the exact columns of the five tables and finds none naming a person, and checks the tracer code CHECK; a unit test does the same over persisted properties. Desk observations are desk-level border data: they stay in the border deployment (never in the border-to-airport feed) and only border roles read them; read paths checked: the campaign view and the manager's search (projection), the observer's own list (what it recorded), the capture list (desk codes of the scope only), audit entries of corrections (administrators only, who hold `BorderDesks.View`). Documented in docs/domain/data-boundary.md and wiki 14 section 3 with retention (indefinite, Proposed; a shorter period after the report is accepted To confirm).
- Decisions (docs/product/decisions.md entry and Open decisions row, all Proposed): (1) the device's clock reading per batch and the 5-minute bound, (2) the one-minute tolerance, the 3-hour longest run and the join-day rule, (3) abandoned tracers left out of the wait error and bias and reported apart in ARV-104e, (4) tracer labels, (5) no correction of tracer runs, (6) desks in scope (staffed border desks, set at planning, by border roles), (7) desk states for border roles only, (8) desk batches (part bins allowed), (9) required batch keys, (10) limits, (11) the model binding change. Formulas F18 has the capture rules and tests.

- Security review, first verdict (2026-10-08): FAIL, one Medium and three Low, with notes. Fixed (not committed): (1) Medium, data boundary (CWE-863): an account holding an airport role and the Validation observer role (a terminal duty manager or handler station manager who also observes) received border desk ids in the capture list and could log, correct and read back desk states. Option (b): one rule, `Ariva.Core/Security/BorderDeskAccess.cs` (`Sees` = holds `BorderDesks.View`; `Observes` = sees, or holds no role but Validation observer; any role that is neither border nor administrator nor observer counts as airport-side, unknown codes included; no role reaches nothing), asked by every desk path through `ValidationServiceBase.DeskAccessAsync`: planning desks and the campaign view and manager search use `Sees` (as before), and now `SvcValidationCapture.RunningAsync` lists desks (`CaptureCampaignViewModel.DesksIncluded`, new) and `SvcDeskObservations.CaptureAsync`, `CorrectAsync` and `OwnObservationsAsync` answer only when `Observes`, right after the site check and before any row is read (403 `ValidationErrors.DeskObservationsNeedBorderRole`, also for a campaign that does not exist); a pure observer keeps desk capture; line counts and tracer runs are unaffected. Documented in docs/domain/data-boundary.md (the desk observations row rewritten: the wording "only border roles read it" and "each observer reads back only what it recorded" did not hold for the dual account), wiki 14 section 3 (same correction), wiki 11 section 2c (a permission row and a paragraph), wiki 07 section 8, wiki 01, formulas F18, cwe-controls (CWE-863), asvs-l2 (V8.1.2, V8.2.3), security/README.md, architecture overview. (2) Low-Medium (CWE-501): JSON body errors echoed member names (System.Text.Json's message and the JSON path key, `$['<script>alert(1)</script>']`). `AddAppControllers` sets `JsonOptions.AllowInputFormatterExceptionMessages` false and an `InvalidModelStateResponseFactory` (`ControllerExtensions.ValidationProblemWithoutBodyPaths`) that rebuilds the default ProblemDetails with every key starting with `$` replaced by `body` (`WithoutBodyPaths`); the NUL refusal of `NulRejectingStringConverter` is now an `InputFormatterException` so its own fixed text survives (`NulCharacterTests` adjusted for the exception type only; the unit and e2e checks of the "NUL character" text are unchanged). (3) Low (CWE-501): a tracer run's device times far from the batch's `deviceClockUtc` (year 1 with a device ahead, year 9999 with a device behind) overflowed `TracerBatch.ToServerTime` (500). `TracerBatch.IsNearDeviceClock` (32 days before to one minute after, compared in ticks so no value overflows) is checked in `TracerBatchProblem` before any correction; the answers reuse the rules the corrected time would break (`TimeInFuture` above the window, since device time minus its clock reading equals corrected time minus receipt; `RunOutsideCampaign` below it), 400 without the value. (4) Low, data boundary: ARV-104d's PRD note now requires the "do not name people in reasons" warning, and the data-boundary row names the residual (free-text reasons beside a desk and a minute). Notes done: (5) at most 500 tracer runs per observer and campaign (409 `TooManyRuns`, `TracerRun.MaxRunsPerObserver`, Proposed point (12) in decisions.md; concurrent batches may pass it by one batch each) and the duplicate check now compares the exact tracer code and device join time pairs in the database (`SvcTracerRuns.SameRunAs`, an OR of typed equality pairs, parameters only) instead of loading every run with the codes; (6) script 0048's `validation_campaign_desk_check` reads the campaign status `FOR SHARE` (0048 is unmerged), new SHA-256 c2d0208cf2b52e3466ee5c0a303719a94472c19ed13952b79df20b5603aec397 in checksums.lock; (7) `AuthSettingsTests.Defaults_Should_MatchAdr0026_When_BaseFileIsRead` pins the global limit (1,000 a minute, 60-second window, no queue; code default and k8s-prd too) and the PermissionMatrixTests comment now says the test raises both limits in its own host and where their behaviour and values are tested; (8) ASVS V8.1.2 gains the desk projection as the fourth field-level rule (the row said "two" while listing three); (9) the owner decisions below and ARV-104e's PRD notes (flag batches whose offset differs from the observer's median by more than a few seconds, each observer's offset spread, the wait error's sensitivity to a plus or minus offset shift).

Point (7) revised and (11) extended after the review; new Proposed points (12) and (13); points (2), (4), (8) to (10) still open.
