- Deferred: screens (ARV-104c tracer timer, ARV-104d desk grid); the comparison rules (ARV-104e tracer matching and abandoned runs, ARV-104f desk-state agreement and two observers of one desk and minute); a withdrawal of a wrong tracer run (decision point 5); e-gates and airport counters as observed desks.
- Learnings: ASP.NET Core's default binding messages quote the attempted value of a query or route parameter, so "400 without echo" needs `ModelBindingMessageProvider` set once for all hosts; a stale device clock reading on a retry would shift the offset, so the client must read its clock at every send and the fingerprint must leave the reading out; when two sessions wait on one lock, wait for both in `pg_blocking_pids` before committing or the test races its own setup; adding endpoints can push the in-process permission matrix past the per-address global rate limit (429), which is a test setting, not a reason to raise the product's limit. After the review: a permission check per endpoint cannot express "this role, unless that one too", so a mixed-role account is a projection rule in the service, written once and asked by every path; `AllowInputFormatterExceptionMessages` false hides System.Text.Json's message but not its key (the JSON path), so the keys need replacing as well; a converter that throws `InputFormatterException` keeps its message through System.Text.Json and the body binder (key ""); C#'s `is` binds tighter than `??`, so `a ?? b is { } x` needs parentheses; a bound on raw device times relative to the device's own clock reading is the same as the future-time rule after the correction, so it can answer with that rule's text.

## 2026-10-08 ARV-104e F18 comparison engine: count accuracy, wait error, bias and track completion (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1; passes)
- Summary: a pure engine in `Ariva.Core/Validation/Comparison` (namespace `Ariva.Core.Validation.Comparison`; no I/O, no persistence, no clock, no endpoint): `ValidationComparison.Compare(ComparisonInput, ComparisonSettings)` takes plain records (the campaign's scope via `ComparisonScope.Of(campaign, siteZone)` with the planned days as UTC windows, `ManualCountRow.Of`, `TracerRunRow.Of`, line counts per 15-minute bin as `line_minute_15m` sums them, `QueueMinuteRow`, `QueueBinRow`, `ZoneHealthBin` as stored, `QualityInterval` for `zone_outage` and uncovered lines) and returns immutable results, each with the profile version: `LineBinAccuracy` and `LineAccuracy` (count accuracy per line, bin and direction, and per line), `TracerComparison` and `TracerZoneSummary` per zone and over all zones (wait error, tolerance, bias, abandoned runs apart with the system's own abandonment, `WaitSensitivity`), `ZoneTrackCompletion`, `ObserverOffsetSpread` and `BatchOffset`, and `LeftOutInputs`. Shared shapes for ARV-104f to 104h: `ComparisonStanding` (Good, NoSystemWait, Degraded, Unknown, Provisional, OtherVersion, in precedence order), `StandingTally`, `CriterionCheck` (value, target, verdict) and `CriterionVerdict` (NoData, Pass, Fail); targets come from `ComparisonSettings` (F18 defaults), so the report can pass a campaign's own.
- Matching rule (Proposed, formulas F18 and docs/product/decisions.md): a tracer is compared with Ariva's final realised wait of the people who entered the same queue zone at the tracer's corrected join time, read from `queue_minute`: each minute's mean realised wait at the minute's centre, a straight line between the two centres around the join (a neighbour minute not final, of another version or without waits is not used; the join minute's mean holds on that side). The join minute must be final, of the campaign's version and hold a realised wait; otherwise Provisional, OtherVersion or NoSystemWait, reported apart. Rebuilt cumulative curves (the PRD's example) were not chosen: they re-derive a wait Ariva does not publish (per-track waits at T3 sites), need an anchor, and carry count drift into the wait error.
- Decisions for the owner (all Proposed, points (1) to (11) of the decisions entry and its Open decisions row): the matching rule; the 5-second offset outlier bound around each observer's median batch offset (flagged runs stay in); the 2-second sensitivity shift; track completion from `zone_health_bin` over every bin of the planned days, pooled over Good bins (no stored output is missing; only T3 zones have tracks); directions per line role (in for entry and overflow entry, out for exit, both for count lines); two observers as the mean with their difference (ARV-104a point 3); accuracy floored at 0; a line passes when every judged bin does; a zone's wait error passes when every compared tracer does (a share the KPI annex may set); standing and precedence (a tracer's covers its whole wait; continuous checks do not change a standing until ARV-114b); guards and bounds (1,000,000 rows of a kind, 50 zones, 200 lines, 62 windows, 3,200 window bins, times 2000 to 2999, quarter-hour bins only). Abandoned runs as the owner decided on 2026-10-08.
- Guards: every row is checked again as the scripts check it (CWE-501: a stored row is not trusted to be what the table allows), null, NaN, infinite, negative, inverted, non-UTC, implausible, out-of-scope and conflicting rows are left out and counted per kind; quality intervals are merged per zone and quality and searched by binary search, items are grouped per line and zone, so no part of the engine is quadratic in its inputs (CWE-400); empty sets give no data, never a division by zero; results are independent of input order (sums over sorted items).
- Tests: `Ariva.UnitTests/Domain/Validation/ComparisonTests.cs` (table-driven from F18: 96 of 100 gives 0.96 and the line passes, 94 of 100 fails it and 95 passes exactly; manual 0 gives the absolute error and no verdict; the floor at 0; directions per role; two observers; latest revisions of counts, queue bins and health; tracer 8 against 8.9 passes and 20 against 22.5 fails; interpolation between minute centres; unusable neighbours; why a run is not compared; Degraded and Unknown bins and runs counted apart; quality intervals nested, extending, touching and overlapping; abandoned runs excluded and counted beside the system's abandonment, a provisional or other-version bin adding nothing; offset outliers and the bound's edges; the sensitivity, symmetric and unequal; track completion pooled with Degraded and Unknown apart, revisions, a 25-hour day, windows off the quarter hour; every kind of unusable row; conflicting duplicates; batches that disagree; invalid and oversized scopes; settings; mapping from the entities) and `ComparisonPropertyTests.cs` (CsCheck: accuracy within 0 and 1 and never rising with the error; tolerance and monotone "within"; the bias's sign follows the errors; track completion a share; the sensitivity symmetric when the minute means are linear; a generated campaign's result independent of input order, with the bias sign checked in every zone summary). Run once with `ARIVA_FUZZ_ITERATIONS=5000` (25,000 inputs per formula property, 5,000 generated campaigns): all pass.
- Mutation: `**/Validation/Comparison/*.cs` added to `Ariva.UnitTests/stryker-config.json`. Scoped runs only (from `Platform/Backplane/Ariva.UnitTests`: `dotnet tool run dotnet-stryker -- --mutate "**/Validation/Comparison/*.cs" --output <folder>`; the command line's list replaces the configuration's, so 541 and then 550 mutants were tested instead of the configured engines' 1,550 or so, thresholds from the configuration, break 70). First run 94.82 percent (511 killed, 2 timeouts, 28 survived; 18 minutes), on the code before the triage. Tests added for the real gaps (profile version and revision 0, a valid beside an invalid window, the first instant of 2000, output order across zones and among tied tracers, the largest sensitivity change over several runs, a provisional queue bin under a final health row, `LeftOutInputs.Total`); an unobservable sort removed (the order of observers' whole counts) and `Worst` written with Math.Max. Final run on the final engine: 98.55 percent (537 killed, 5 timeouts, 8 survived of 550; 60 minutes, because Stryker's MTP runner could not capture the coverage of 17 tests and ran them against every mutant). Of the 8: 6 equivalent (the median's sort direction; First against FirstOrDefault on groups that are never empty, twice; `>=` against `>` at a minute's centre, where the neighbour's weight is 0; merging touching spans or not; `>=` against `>` when two span ends are equal) and 2 real (a nested interval not merged, a span's end not extended), killed by `Compare_Should_MergeOverlappingIntervals_When_OneIsNestedOrExtendsAnother`, added after the run and checked by applying each mutant by hand (the test fails with either and passes without). The full configured run (every engine, about an hour) was not run.
- Docs: formulas F18 (comparison engine rules and tests, the F18 row of the property table), docs/product/decisions.md (entry and Open decisions row), wiki 15 section 8 (how the comparison is computed), wiki 07 section 8 (pointer), wiki 16 (mutation scope and a scoped run).
- Gates (2026-10-08): backend PASS (3,488 unit tests, 302 of them new; build, reference golden, scanner self-test, scan, base images, chart security, NuGet and npm audits); `node scripts/security/scan.mjs` 0 errors (1,112 files; the 6 pending allowlist warnings as before, none in the touched files); docs PASS; quick PASS; scoped mutation 98.55 percent (above the break of 70). Not run: integration, web and e2e (no persistence, API or screen change), and the full configured mutation run.
- Security review, first verdict (2026-10-08): FAIL, two Medium and four Low, with a note; the reviewer's probe cases are now tests. Fixed (not committed): (1) Medium, CWE-501: rows were value-checked and de-duplicated before the highest revision was chosen, so a refused or conflicting latest revision let the previous one stand in (a health bin's revision 2 of 80 of 100 tracks with 101 abandoned gave revision 1's 0.95, a pass; a Degraded queue bin revision 2 with more abandoned than entries let the Good revision 1 be judged; an observer's correction of 100 to 120 that conflicted with a second revision 2 used 100, accuracy 1; a campaign-version line row of 1,000,001 crossings read as 0 crossings, Good). `ComparisonData` now groups every row whose key is valid by its table's key and lets the highest revision decide before any value is checked (`Decide`): a refused or conflicting deciding revision makes the key unusable, its rows are counted, the key is listed in the new `LeftOutInputs.UnusableKeys` (`UnusableKey`: kind, zone, line, bin or minute, the observer for counts, reason `Refused` or `Conflicting`; `LeftOutInputs` equality now covers the keys) and no earlier revision stands in: a queue bin, health bin or queue minute counts as not stored (a tracer whose join minute is unusable is Unknown, no longer NoSystemWait), a campaign-version Ariva line row leaves N_system null and the item Unknown (`LineBinState.Unusable`), a line and bin with an observer's unusable count is not judged and the other observers' counts of it are left out with it (`UnusableCountBins`); a row of another version flags OtherVersion whatever it holds; superseded revisions are never used (refused or conflicting ones are counted). (2) Medium, CWE-501: the matching rule's neighbour minute was checked for status and version only, so a minute in the previous bin, or before the join under an outage, weighed up to half while the run stayed Good (8, within, where the join minute alone gives an error of 4 against 1). `TracerWaits.UsableMean` now also needs `Over(zone, m, m + 1 min, requireFinal: true)` to be Good, `SystemWaitAt` adds the join minute's own standing from its start (the minutes read), and the sensitivity reads give no error when the minutes they read are not Good (`ShiftedError`). (3) Low, CWE-400 and CWE-501: the 1,000,000-row bound and the left-out counts trusted `IReadOnlyList.Count` (a list that said 1 and yielded 3 gave -2). `ComparisonData.Bounded` reads every input and scope list row by row, stops at the first row beyond its bound, and the counts come from the rows read. (4) Low, docs: docs/security/cwe-controls.md, CWE-501 and CWE-120 rows, now carry ARV-104e's evidence and test names. (5) Low, CWE-501: a manual count's bin or a run's corrected join outside the planned days is left out and counted (`IsPlanned`, merged windows searched by binary search), and two runs of one observer, tracer code and device join time (script 0048's `ux_tracer_run_join`) are a conflict, both left out, each counted once. (6) Low, verdicts on a tiny sample (1 bin judged beside 9 Degraded gave Pass): the engine's verdicts are unchanged; formulas F18 (new "Verdicts and sample size" bullet), decisions point (12), wiki 15 section 8 and wiki 07 say they are not acceptance verdicts on their own and that ARV-104g's campaign verdict needs the campaign's targets of Good judged items (`target_bins_per_line`, `target_tracer_runs`), otherwise no data, and shows the excluded share; new counts for it: `LineAccuracy.JudgedBins` and `ExcludedBins` (bins not Good, plus bins not judged for an unusable count), `TracerZoneSummary.Excluded`, `ZoneTrackCompletion.ExcludedBins` (beside `Compared` and `Good.Bins`). Note done: every result list is read-only (`AsReadOnly`; the keys are copied on init).
- Review fixes, tests (`ComparisonTests`, `ComparisonPropertyTests`; 342 comparison cases, all passing): one per probe case, `Compare_Should_NotFallBackToAnEarlierHealthRevision_When_TheLatestIsUnusable`, `..._NotFallBackToAnEarlierQueueBinRevision_...`, `..._NotJudgeTheLineAndBin_When_AnObserversLatestCountIsUnusable` (refused and conflicting each, with a second observer), `..._LeaveNSystemUnknown_When_TheLinesRowOfTheCampaignsVersionIsUnusable`, `..._HoldTheJoinMinutesOwnMean_When_TheNeighboursStoredResultsAreNotGood` (Degraded bin under an outage, Degraded, Unknown, provisional, other-version or missing bin, outage or uncovered line over 18:14 only), `..._CountTheRowsItReads_When_AListsCountUnderstatesThem` and `..._ReportJudgedAndExcludedCounts_When_OneBinIsJudgedAndNineAreDegraded`; and `..._JudgeAgainstTheObserversCount_When_ItsLatestCountHasNoConflict`, `..._UseTheLatestRevision_When_AnEarlierOneIsRefusedOrConflicting`, `..._CountTheBinAsOtherVersion_When_ARowOfAnotherVersionCannotBeUsed`, `..._InterpolateWithTheNeighbour_When_ItsStoredResultsAreGoodAndFinal`, `..._SetTheRunApart_When_AnIntervalCoversTheJoinMinuteBeforeTheJoin`, `..._LeaveTheShiftedErrorOut_When_AShiftedJoinReadsAMinuteWhoseStoredResultsAreNotGood`, `..._CompareTheRowsItReads_When_AListsCountOverstatesThem`, `..._UseNeitherRun_When_TwoRunsShareTheObserverTracerAndDeviceJoin`, `..._LeaveOutCountsAndRunsOutsideThePlannedDays_When_TheyFallBetweenThem`, `..._CountACountLinesBinOnce_When_BothDirectionsAreJudged`, `..._ReturnListsThatCannotBeChanged_When_ItCompares`; the bound theory gained lists that say 1 (or 0) and yield more, for inputs and the scope's zones, lines and windows. Changed: every `LeaveOutAndCount*` theory now says whether the row's key is valid (listed unusable) or not (only counted), a refused campaign-version line row is Unknown without N_system (was 0 crossings, Good), a refused join minute makes the run Unknown (was NoSystemWait), the planned-day cases join the count and run theories, and tests that used rows outside the planned days or a list whose Count overstated its rows were moved inside or given real rows. Properties: the generated campaign's left-out counts stay between 0 and the rows given and its keys are unique, and a new property refuses the highest revision of a generated bin and count and checks nothing over them stays Good (run once with `ARIVA_FUZZ_ITERATIONS=3000`: pass).
- Gates after the fixes (2026-10-08): backend PASS (3,557 unit tests; build, reference golden, scanner self-test, scan, base images, chart security, NuGet and npm audits); `node scripts/security/scan.mjs` 0 errors (1,114 files; the 6 pending allowlist warnings as before, none in the touched files); docs PASS. Not run: Stryker (the score is measured at the checkpoint after this story, on the fixed code) and E2E (no API change).
- Security review re-check (2026-10-08): FAIL, three Low findings (non-UTC latest rows, refused run twins, unreadable quality intervals) fixed as follows (not committed); the six first-review fixes held, and the reviewer's second probe cases are now tests. (1) Low, CWE-501, non-UTC latest rows: the key filters checked `Kind == Utc`, but DateTime equality (and `timestamptz`) ignores the kind, so a latest row at the same instant without the UTC kind was dropped before the grouping and the earlier revision decided (a count's revision 2 of 50 gave revision 1's accuracy 1; a health revision 2 of 80 of 100 gave 0.95; a Degraded queue bin revision 2 gave Good; a minute twin of 30 against 8 and a line bin twin of 10 against 100 were not conflicts). `ComparisonData` places every key by its ticks (`IsInRange`, `IsBinTicks`) and checks `IsUtc` with the values in `Decide`, on every row of the deciding revision (so a copy equal but for its kind is refused in either order); unusable keys are reported in UTC (`AsUtc`). (2) Low, CWE-501, refused run twins: `IsUsableRun` ran before the run id, join key and batch checks, so a refused twin let the other run be used (one run id with an exit before the join; a join twin whose exit did not match its offset, or in a zone out of scope; a batch whose other run had a 400,000 ms offset). `TakeRuns` now places runs by their ids only, groups by run id and by observer, tracer code and device join ticks over every keyed row, and leaves out whole any batch whose rows disagree on the observer or offset or hold a run that cannot be (ARV-104b refuses such a batch at capture). (3) Low, CWE-501, unreadable quality intervals failed open: an interval outside the years 2000 to 2999 (an open outage ending at `DateTime.MaxValue`, as `AlertInputs` passes it) was dropped and the bins under it stayed Good (track completion 0.925, a pass, instead of 0.85). `TakeIntervals` clamps the ends to the engine's years (`DateTime.MinValue` and `MaxValue` are open ends whatever their kind); one wholly outside them is left out; one of a zone in scope that still cannot be placed (another non-UTC time, an end not after its start, an undefined quality) makes that zone Unknown over the whole comparison (`_unplaced`, checked in `Over`; one `UnusableKeyKind.QualityInterval` key per zone), chosen over a Problem so the other zones keep their results. Information notes documented: a refused or conflicting neighbour minute is treated as absent (F18 matching rule), and the finality asymmetry (join minute and wait span read without bin finality, neighbour with it) is decisions point (13) for the owner, not aligned in code (requiring final bins over the whole wait would hold back runs whose own wait is final). Tests (366 comparison cases, all passing): `Compare_Should_NotJudgeTheLineAndBin_When_TheLatestCountsTimeIsNotUtc` (alone and beside a UTC copy in either order), `..._LeaveTheBinUnknown_When_ItsLatestQueueOrHealthRevisionsTimeIsNotUtc`, `..._SeeAConflict_When_AMinutesOrALineBinsTwinIsNotUtc`, `..._UseNoRunOfAKeyOrBatch_When_AnyOfItsRowsCannotBe` (the four probe cases, a batch with a run over 3 hours, a copy whose join is not UTC), `..._DegradeTheItemsUnderAnInterval_When_ItsEndsLieBeyondTheEnginesYears`, `..._JudgeTrackCompletionWithoutTheBinUnderAnOpenOutage_When_ItEndsAtDateTimeMaxValue`, `..._MakeTheZoneUnknownThroughout_When_AQualityIntervalCannotBePlaced`, `..._ListAZoneOnce_When_SeveralOfItsIntervalsCannotBePlaced`, `..._HoldTheJoinMinutesOwnMean_When_TheNeighbourMinuteIsUnusable`; the non-UTC cases of the count, health, minute, queue bin and line bin theories now expect a listed key, and the interval theory keeps only intervals that touch nothing compared. Docs: formulas F18 (guards, matching rule, tests), decisions points (11) and (13) and the Open decisions row, wiki 15 section 8, cwe-controls CWE-501. Gates (2026-10-08): backend PASS (3,581 unit tests; build, reference golden, scanner self-test, scan, base images, chart security, NuGet and npm audits); `node scripts/security/scan.mjs` 0 errors (1,114 files, the same 6 pending allowlist warnings, none in the touched files); docs PASS. Not run: Stryker (measured at the checkpoint after this story) and E2E (no API change).
- Second re-check (2026-10-08): FAIL, two Low gaps left in the run fix, both needing stored states the primary key, `ux_tracer_run_join` and the all-or-nothing batch capture rule out: (1) a run id whose own rows disagree was dropped by `Unique` before the join check, so its join twin in another batch was used; (2) a row with an empty observer or run id was dropped before the batch check, so its batch's other run was used. Fixed by the lead: `TakeRuns` finds join keys held by more than one run id over every non-null row (an empty id counting as one) before `Unique` and leaves out every run with such a key, and groups batches over every row with a batch id, so a row that fails `IsUsableRun` spoils its batch. Test `Compare_Should_UseNoRunOfAKeyOrBatch_When_ARowLeftOutForItsIdsWouldHideTheConflict` (the reviewer's R2, R3a, R3b).
- Security review final confirmation (2026-10-08): PASS. The reviewer reran its probe on the rebuilt engine (R2, R3a, R3b use no run; every earlier probe line unchanged; a check of normal input, two copies of a run plus a valid two-run batch, still compares both runs), `node scripts/security/scan.mjs` 0 errors over 1,114 files (the 6 pending allowlist warnings, outside this diff). CWEs touched: CWE-501 and CWE-120, controls and tests in place and recorded in docs/security/cwe-controls.md; the other twelve not affected (pure domain code). No ASVS row claimed. Carried to ARV-104g's notes: the campaign verdict's sample rule, observer-level results restricted to validation roles of the site, site and campaign filters on every read, the queue-minute volume and single-flight run, line_minute_15m's 3-day refresh, target authorisation, comparing with the campaign's full scope (a subset scope drops whole batches that span zones), and treating a nonzero `LeftOut.QualityIntervals` as needing review.
- Final gates (2026-10-08): backend PASS (3,584 unit tests; build, reference golden, scanner self-test, scan, base images, chart security, NuGet and npm audits); docs PASS. Mutation: 98.55 percent was measured before the review fixes; the first checkpoint, which runs right after this commit, measures the fixed engine (break 70), and a score below the threshold is fixed before the next story.
- Gotchas: Stryker's MTP runner could not capture coverage for 17 tests, which made the scoped run 60 minutes instead of about 20; three review rounds were needed because each fix moved a check after a filter that dropped rows silently: check every key-placing filter for rows it hides from the conflict checks.
- Deferred: the report, its targets per campaign and a campaign-level verdict per criterion (ARV-104g; the engine gives per-line and per-zone verdicts against the targets it is given); desk-state agreement and nowcast error (ARV-104f, which can reuse `ComparisonStanding`, `StandingTally` and `CriterionCheck`); reading the stored rows for a campaign (ARV-104g's service: line counts per bin of the Ariva source, the latest revisions, `zone_outage` rows as Degraded intervals, open outages ending where the read ends).
- Learnings: FluentAssertions' `AllSatisfy` fails on an empty collection, so a filtered "every value is a share" check is a loop; CA5394 refuses `System.Random` in tests too, so generated scenarios draw from CsCheck values; `--mutate` on Stryker's command line replaces the configuration's list and scopes a run to one engine's files; Stryker's MTP runner sometimes cannot capture a test's coverage ("Dubious") and then runs that test against every mutant, which tripled the second run; a test of an index (merged spans and a binary search) needs inputs where the index alone decides, or a later input hides the fault (a third touching interval repaired the unmerged spans).

## 2026-10-08 Owner requests and fixes alongside ARV-104e (branch story/mvp-phase1; not stories)

- Report generation moves to ARV-146 for the Insight report tool used in AMAN (owner decision; ARV-119, 120, 104g, 104h, 104i and 144 keep what is not report generation; ARV-137 depends on ARV-146). Commit d38bb5b.
- Test cadence (owner decision): `verify.mjs story` per story and `verify.mjs checkpoint` every 5 stories, at phase ends and before go-live; open stories' gate criteria reworded; `docs/harness/test-cadence.md`. Commit 22c7ab5. Gotchas found on its first wide run: the full E2E suite needs fresh services (seven "already exists" failures on reused ones, 581 passed after `reset-services.sh`), and `CI=true` fails the chart step when `helm` is missing.
- `run-ariva.ps1` Docker check through cmd (owner's commit b4860fc, cherry-picked as 568edeb).
- Topology bug (owner report): the airport time zone was free text; it is now a select of the browser's IANA zones grouped by area with the UTC offset, keeping a stored zone the browser names differently. Commit da1ec62.
- Windows bug (owner report: "unknown time zone" on adding an airport): the hosts run with invariant globalization, so .NET on Windows could not map an IANA id (ICU is off); airports were refused, reports and calendars used UTC and the availability ledger skipped the site on Windows machines. `SiteTimeZones` resolves through TimeZoneConverter 7.2.0 (embedded mapping); airports accept IANA ids only. Full gate on fresh services: 3,215 unit, 418 integration, 581 E2E. Commit 690a5bb. Gotcha: tests on Linux cannot exercise the Windows registry lookup; the owner's machine is the proof.
- Security scan `--changed` missed files inside new untracked folders (git reports the folder) and renamed files; found by the ARV-104e review. Commit fbe537c.

## 2026-10-08 ARV-104f F18 comparison engine: desk-state agreement and nowcast error with and without AMAN (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1; passes)
- Summary: an extension of the ARV-104e engine in `Ariva.Core/Validation/Comparison` (same namespace, `ValidationComparison.Compare`; pure, no I/O, no persistence, no clock, no endpoint; the lead's decision of 2026-10-08 keeps the read path for ARV-104g). Reused, not forked: `ComparisonData`'s intake (`Bounded`, `Decide`, `IsPlanned`, `Over`, `Worst`), `ComparisonStanding`, `StandingTally`, `CriterionCheck`, `LeftOutInputs` and `UnusableKey`, `ComparisonSettings`. New plain inputs: `ScopeDesk` (`ComparisonScope.Desks`, mapped by `ComparisonScope.Of` from the campaign's desks), `DeskObservationRow.Of`, `DeskMinuteRow` (desk_minute named by checkpoint and desk codes), the live part of `QueueMinuteRow` (published nowcast, no-service reason as stored, flag; optional parameters, so ARV-104e's callers are unchanged) and `ShadowMinuteRow` (queue_minute_shadow). New immutable results, each with the profile version: `DeskMinuteAgreement`, `DeskAgreementSummary` per desk and overall (`DeskStateCount` confusion, throughput agreement), `NowcastMinuteError` with a `NowcastReading` per nowcast, `NowcastZoneErrors` per zone and overall (`NowcastErrorSummary` for the published and the shadow nowcast, `NowcastPairedErrors` on the minutes both judged, `NowcastErrorStats`, `NoServiceCount`). Formulas public and table-tested: `DeskStateAgreement.Dominant`, `Agrees`, `CountsForThroughput`, `Agreement`; `NowcastErrors.Error`, `IsUnderCut`, `Median`, `IsWithinTarget`, `Stats`, `ReasonOf`. Settings: `DeskAgreementTarget` 0.95, `NowcastErrorTargetMinutes` 2, `NowcastWaitCutMinutes` 20.
- Decisions for the owner (Proposed, points (1) to (11) of the decisions entry and its Open decisions row, F18): the dominant state (most seconds, unaccounted seconds Unknown, Unknown wins any tie, known ties to the more active state; differs from the live desk screen on Unknown ties on purpose); Unknown and missing or unusable stored minutes as disagreement, Degraded minutes judged and tallied; several observers judged only when they agree (otherwise excluded and counted); corrections as each observer's latest revision, an unusable latest state leaving the desk minute unjudged with the others' states; throughput agreement and confusion beside, no target; the nowcast of minute m against the final mean realised wait of minute m + 1's entrants (as F8's reference measurements); the standing over both minutes and the entrants' mean wait in Good, final bins; flagged nowcasts judged and reported beside (not set apart); the strict 20-minute cut on the realised wait, minutes at or above it, without service (per reason) and Degraded reported apart; the proof each over its own minutes and both on the common ones, sensor cycle minutes apart; guards. Point (12), for the lead: `ShadowNowcastExposureTests` changed on purpose (below).
- Lessons of ARV-104e's three reviews applied from the start: keys placed by identifying fields only (instants by ticks) and grouped before any value check; the highest revision decides; a refused or conflicting deciding row makes the key unusable (`UnusableKeyKind.DeskObservation`, `DeskMinute`, `ShadowMinute`; `UnusableKey.DeskId` for desks), never replaced; non-UTC times refused as values; a row of a desk minute without an observer id spoils the minute instead of being dropped; every list counted as read (1,000,000 rows of each new kind, 100 desks); fail closed: an unusable queue minute in the planned days is a nowcast minute of standing Unknown, an unusable shadow a shadow reading Unknown, an observed minute without a usable stored minute a disagreement; no upper bound on a stored nowcast (a published nowcast of hours is a real error).
- Data boundary: no person in any result (observers only as pseudonymous ids in unusable keys of desk states). `ComparisonResult`'s documentation says what ARV-104g must restrict: desk-state results and desk keys to border roles of the campaign's site only (never airport roles, the feed, AMAN or an airport deployment), the shadow's figures only in the validation results to `Validation.View` holders of the site. docs/domain/data-boundary.md and wiki 14 now say the shadow and desk states are compared by ARV-104f's engine and read by ARV-104g's service.
- ShadowNowcastExposureTests (changed on purpose, for the lead): the engine must name the shadow it compares, so the guard admits the comparison engine's namespace (pure, no I/O) for members named after the shadow, `ComparisonResults.cs` and `NowcastErrors.cs` for the shadow's value and four engine files for the sensor cycle time; the table name stays only in the stream store and the scripts (the engine's docs do not name it), and a new test `ShadowRows_Should_StayInTheComparisonEngine_When_TheirHoldersAreReflected` pins `ShadowMinuteRow`, `NowcastReading`, `NowcastZoneErrors` and `ComparisonResult` to the engine until ARV-104g. The pg_depend dependents test of QueueStreamTests is untouched (no database change); ARV-104g changes both with its read path.
- Tests: `Ariva.UnitTests/Domain/Validation/DeskAgreementTests.cs` and `NowcastErrorTests.cs` (table-driven from the criteria: 19 and 18 of 20 minutes, no observed minutes, every dominant tie, Unknown, missing and unusable stored minutes as disagreement, Degraded minutes, several observers agreeing and disagreeing, corrections, conflicting, refused and non-UTC latest states, a row without an observer, revisions 0 and 101, rows outside the planned days or scope, desk scopes invalid or at 100 desks, bounds; the pairing with the next minute, medians odd and even, the 2-minute target's edge, the 20-minute cut's edge, each no-service reason, flagged nowcasts, all degraded, an outage over the entrants' wait, every reason a minute is not judged, every unusable nowcast and shadow row, the side by side with coverage, a shadow without its queue minute, the pooled median against the median of medians, settings, mapping from the entities, read-only results, order independence), and `ComparisonPropertyTests` (CsCheck: agreement within 0 and 1 and rising with an agreeing minute; the dominant state holds the most seconds and is Unknown on every Unknown tie; the median absolute error never negative, within the errors and never falling as one grows; the generated campaign now with desks, desk states, desk minutes, published and shadow nowcasts, its order independence, tallies and left-out bounds; a refused desk state's minute never judged). Run once with `ARIVA_FUZZ_ITERATIONS=3000`: pass.
- Mutation: the namespace is already in `stryker-config.json` (`**/Validation/Comparison/*.cs`); Stryker not run (it runs at the checkpoint). Edge tests were added for the branches a self-review expected to survive (a state outside the enum against Closed, a cut that is not a number, NaN in `Stats`, revisions 0 and 100, the new `LeftOutInputs` counts in equality, unusable keys ordered by desk).
- Docs: formulas F18 (the ARV-104f block, the desk observation pointer, the F18 row of the property table), docs/product/decisions.md (entry and Open decisions row), docs/domain/glossary.md (shadow nowcast, dominant desk state, desk-state agreement, nowcast error), docs/domain/data-boundary.md and wiki 14 (readers), docs/security/cwe-controls.md (CWE-501 and CWE-120 evidence), wiki 15 section 8 (how the agreement and the error are computed), wiki 07 section 8 (pointer).
- Gates (2026-10-08, on the final tree): backend PASS (3,839 unit tests, 255 more than ARV-104e's 3,584; build, reference golden, scanner self-test, scan, base images, chart security, NuGet and npm audits); `node scripts/security/scan.mjs` 0 errors (1,120 files; the 6 pending allowlist warnings as before, none in the touched files); docs PASS. Not run: Stryker (at the checkpoint), integration and E2E (no persistence, API or screen change; the E2E services are in use by a checkpoint in another worktree).
- Security review (2026-10-08): FAIL, no High, one Medium and six Low; the change to ShadowNowcastExposureTests judged acceptable (a pure engine cannot name its input otherwise and nothing on the read side loosened). The reviewer's probe (scratchpad probe3) is now tests. Fixed (not committed): (Medium-1, documented now, the rule waits for ARV-104g) two exclusions are levers: a second observer contradicting only the minutes the system got wrong turns 16 of 20 (0.80, a fail) into 16 of 16 (1.00, a pass), and minutes whose published nowcast said NothingOpen while the next minute's entrants waited are not judged (30 such beside one judged minute pass the zone). Stated with these numbers in decisions points (3) and (9), F18 "Verdicts and sample size", the result types' docs and wiki 15; the stricter rule (excluded minutes counted against the system) fails closed; the engine now exposes `DeskAgreementSummary.StrictAgreement` (agreeing over judged plus excluded minutes) and `NowcastErrorSummary.NoServiceUnderCut`, beside the counts ARV-104g needs to count them against the system or cap their share (`DeskAgreementTests.Compare_Should_ExposeTheExclusionLeverAndTheStrictAgreement_When_ASecondObserverContradictsTheWrongMinutes`, `Compare_Should_CountUnusableObservationsInTheStrictAgreement_When_AStateCannotBeUsed`, `NowcastErrorTests.Compare_Should_ExposeTheNoServiceLever_When_TheNowcastSaidNothingOpenWhilePeopleWaited`, `Compare_Should_CountOnlyNoServiceMinutesUnderTheCut_When_SomeEntrantsWaitedLonger`). (Low-1, CWE-501) stored nowcasts of 1e308 (published) or 1.7e308 (shadow), finite and accepted by the script, made the median and the means Infinity and the result unserialisable (a results endpoint would answer 500): each error's size is now capped at a billion minutes (`NowcastErrors.MaxErrorMinutes`, in `Error` and in `Stats`), and the even median halves the two middle values before adding them; such an error still counts and fails, and the documented rule of no upper bound on a stored nowcast stands. ARV-104e's statistics were checked: every value they read is bounded, so they cannot overflow (new `ComparisonTests.Compare_Should_GiveFiniteResultsThatSerialise_When_EveryStoredValueIsAtItsBound`); the generated campaign now includes such nowcasts, so the order property serialises them. (Low-2) `ShadowNowcastExposureTests.IsComparisonEngine` now takes the type and matches Ariva.Core's assembly and the engine's namespace exactly (no sub-namespace, no other assembly), and the holders test covers every type carrying the shadow's figures (`ShadowMinuteRow`, `NowcastReading`, `NowcastMinuteError`, `NowcastErrorStats`, `NoServiceCount`, `NowcastErrorSummary`, `NowcastPairedErrors`, `NowcastZoneErrors`, `ComparisonResult`). (Low-5) wiki 14's desk observations row: the agreement per desk and minute is border per-desk data served only to border roles of the campaign's site (`BorderDeskAccess.Sees`), as data-boundary.md says. Low-4 was not in the fix list sent to the engineer (no change here). Waiting for ARV-104g (the lead adds the carry-overs to its notes): the Medium-1 rule in the campaign verdict (the strict agreement or a cap; no-service minutes under the cut counted or capped), the signature-level guard check, and Low-6.

## 2026-10-08 ARV-104g split into ARV-104g1, ARV-104g2 and ARV-104g (backlog; lead decision)

ARV-104g had grown with the ARV-104e and ARV-104f security reviews to hold a new database login with its grants, chart secret and guard tests, every campaign read with exact keys, the engine run in slices beyond its 1,000,000-row bound, campaign verdicts that the exclusion levers cannot inflate, the freeze at close with a content hash, and a JSON API with E2E: more than one session. Split: ARV-104g1 the validation reader login (the shadow read path and its authorization), ARV-104g2 the campaign results service (reads, comparison in slices, campaign verdicts; no endpoint), ARV-104g keeps its id for the freeze, the serving rules and the API, so ARV-104h, 104i, 137 and 146 keep their dependencies.

## 2026-10-08 ARV-104g1 Validation reader login: the shadow nowcast's read path with its own authorization (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1; passes)
- Summary (as first built; the review fixes below change the function's input, its refusals and the guard's check): the validation service gets a database login of its own, the only one that holds `ariva_validation_reader` (script 0043) and so reads the shadow nowcast. Script 0049_validation_reader_login.sql, SHA-256 13dc2a80a2c4a1559970120b9ec79aaab328068b35224964c6deb3405882fca7 (checksums.lock), adds `ariva_ensure_validation_reader_login(name, password)` (the migration login's; EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC): it grants the reader role and CONNECT on the database and nothing else, and refuses a name that is not a plain lower-case identifier, starts with pg_, is an Ariva role or the migration login, a password under 16 characters, and an existing role that is privileged, holds any role but the reader role, is granted to a role other than the migration login (PostgreSQL 16 and later make the creator its administrator) or owns objects; an error while creating or altering the login is raised again without the statement, so the password reaches no error context. The migration job (`DatabaseMigrator`) checks the reader's settings before anything, creates or updates the login after the runtime login, and stops (exit 1) when the runtime login holds the reader role; without the section it leaves an existing reader login as it is.
- Settings: `Database:ValidationReader` (`ValidationReaderSettings`, Ariva.Infra/Settings), never part of `DatabaseSettings`, so no runtime connection can be built from it. Its connection is built only by `ValidationReaderSettings.BuildReaderConnectionString` (host, port, database and TLS from `Database`, application name `ariva-validation-reader`, its own pool of at most 4), called only by the validation service's shadow read: `SvcValidationResults` (Ariva.Infra/Services/Validation), created as a partial class whose only part, `SvcValidationResults.Shadow.cs`, reads `queue_minute_shadow` by exact zone keys of one site (`ZoneKeys.For`), 1 to 50 zones, a UTC window of at most 33 days, at most 1,000,000 rows (refused beyond, never cut short), 120 s command timeout; ARV-104g2 builds the service out under that name. Every other validation read stays on the runtime login.
- Start-up refusal: `ValidationReaderGuard` (every host, registered by `AddArivaPersistence` after the development migration and the schema check) refuses to start when the reader is misconfigured next to the host's logins (the runtime or the migration login by name, in any case; an Ariva role; a bad name; a password under 16 characters or equal to the runtime or migration password, compared in constant time; a password without a name) and, whenever the host already reaches the database at start-up (schema check outside vm-local, development migration in vm-local), when its runtime login is a MEMBER of `ariva_validation_reader` (any grant, through any role, or a superuser).
- Clusters: the chart passes the secret `ariva-validation-reader` (keys `username` and `password`, value `validationReader.secretName`) as `Database__ValidationReader__Username` and `Password` to api-main and the database-migration job only (helper `ariva-platform.validationReaderEnv`); optional in dev, demo and localk8s, required in k8s-prd (`validationReader.optional: false`; validate.yaml refuses true there and a secret name that is not a Kubernetes name). Chart test: new rules (only api-main-deployment and the database-migration job may get it, never as a literal, both must get it from one secret with keys username and password, never optional in k8s-prd), a render assertion per environment (exactly those two workloads carry it) and three release guards; self-test 40 rules.
- vm-local, AppHost, E2E, dev container: `scripts/dev-up.mjs` writes `Database:ValidationReader` (`ariva_validation`) into `appsettings.local.json`, drawing `ARIVA_DB_VALIDATION_READER_PASSWORD` into `.env` when missing (`.env.example` could not be read or edited in this session: add `ARIVA_DB_VALIDATION_READER_PASSWORD=generate` there); every host on a developer machine loads it, as it loads the migration login. The AppHost gives api-main alone the reader (parameter `database-validation-reader-password`); `scripts/e2e-apphost.mjs` passes the same password to the AppHost and the Playwright run; `playwright.config.ts` gives api-main alone `Database__ValidationReader__*` (user `ariva_validation`, password `ARIVA_E2E_VALIDATION_READER_PASSWORD`, random per run, only with a database), and the log scan in `global-teardown.ts` now fails a run whose host logs carry that password. The E2E run's runtime login must not be a superuser (CI's `ariva_app` is not).
- ShadowNowcastExposureTests (changed on purpose): exactly the reader's file (`SvcValidationResults.Shadow.cs`) added to the table-name scan (and to the sensor cycle scan, since it reads that column); exactly the validation service (`Ariva.Infra.Services.Validation.SvcValidationResults`) and the results projection (`Ariva.Core.Domain.ViewModels.ValidationResultsViewModel`, named now for ARV-104g), each by full name and assembly with the types nested in it, admitted in the holder allowance (`ShadowRows_Should_StayInTheEngineAndTheValidationReadPath_When_TheirHoldersAreReflected`) and the member allowances (the projection alone among exposed types); new `Signatures_Should_NeverTakeOrReturnTheShadow_When_TheTypeServesScreensRulesReportsOrContracts` (no controller, hub, view model, report, alerting, live, display or contract type takes or returns `ComparisonResult`, `ComparisonInput` or a shadow-bearing type, except that projection; parameters, returns, properties, fields and events, generic arguments and arrays unwrapped) with a probe control. It found `LiveZoneSnapshot.From(QueueLiveMinute)`: the live snapshot's factory took the stream's minute, which also carries the shadow (dropped on copy). Moved to the stream's side as `Ariva.Infra.Streaming.LiveMinuteSnapshots.From` (the worker and three unit tests call it), so no live type takes it; the snapshot itself is unchanged.
- Dependents test (changed on purpose): `QueueStreamTests.Migrations_Should_LeaveNoReadPathOverTheShadowOrTheSensorOnlyDeskMinutesButTheReaderRole_When_Applied`; the read path is a client login, not a database object, so the allowlist of dependents stays empty; it now also lists the grantees that may read a value of `queue_minute_shadow` (table, chunks or value columns, PUBLIC included, owner and the runtime role's key columns aside) against an exact allowlist, `ariva_validation_reader` alone, with a rolled-back control (a value column for the runtime role, a new role, PUBLIC on a chunk: all caught), and checks neither `ariva_runtime` nor `ariva_migration` holds the reader role.
- Tests: unit `Persistence/ValidationReaderLoginTests` (settings refusals by name, Ariva role, runtime and migration login in any case, password length and reuse, password without a name, messages without secrets; the reader's connection; the shadow read refusing without connecting when unconfigured, misconfigured or out of bounds, 14 cases; the guard by name; its registration after the schema services in every composition; no committed appsettings file carries the reader; the type graph of the settings' holders; the one caller of the reader's connection; script 0049's grants), `AppHostModelTests` (api-main alone gets it), `ShadowNowcastExposureTests` (above). Integration `Validation/ValidationReaderLoginTests` (the migration job creates the reader with the reader role only; through the validation service it reads exactly the requested site's keys in the half-open window, refuses beyond its row limit; the runtime login gets 42501 on shadow values; the reader gets 42501 on queue_minute, desk minutes, users, campaigns, schema_version, every write, DDL and both login functions; a second migration rotates its password; without the section the login is left alone; twelve refused logins and nothing granted to them; a host refuses to start and the job exits 1 when the runtime login holds the role directly or through another role, and when the reader is named as the runtime login), `DatabaseChartBootstrapTests` (the chart's CREATEROLE, non-superuser migration login creates and updates the reader twice on PostgreSQL 17; the reader reads the shadow and not queue_minute; the runtime login cannot read a shadow value), the dependents test above. TestDatabase gains `ValidationReader`, `ValidationReaderRefusals`, `ValidationReaderGuard`.

- Security review (2026-10-08): FAIL, no High, two Medium, four required Low and recommended hardening; all fixed (not committed) except L7, carried to ARV-104g2 by the lead. Script 0049 is not merged, so it was changed: new SHA-256 04007bd78ea4bd51b442ca3ca0759c068b684215e4169a216cb1470aa40ad367 (checksums.lock; was 13dc2a80...). Fixes:
  - M1 (CWE-269): an existing role was checked for attributes, memberships, grantees and four kinds of ownership only, so the development read-only role (SELECT on every table through default privileges, no membership, owns nothing) could be named the reader, giving the reader users, sessions, TOTP and audit, and the job would change its password. `0049_validation_reader_login.sql:80` now refuses any `pg_shdepend` row for the role in this database or a shared catalog except its ACL entries on `pg_database` (this replaces the four ownership checks), and `:85` refuses any privilege on this database other than CONNECT (or CONNECT with grant option) through `aclexplode(datacl)`. Integration `EnsureValidationReaderLogin_Should_RefuseEveryLoginThatIsNotOnlyAReader_When_Called`: roles with SELECT on a table, SELECT on a column, a default privilege, CREATE on schema public and CREATE on the database are refused, none gets a password or the reader role, and the reader itself passes on a re-run (its CONNECT entry allowed); `DatabaseChartBootstrapTests` confirms the re-run under the chart's CREATEROLE, non-superuser owner on PostgreSQL 17.
  - M2 (CWE-269, CWE-863): the guard and the job asked only `pg_has_role(..., 'ariva_validation_reader', 'MEMBER')`, so `pg_read_all_data`, a value column granted to the runtime login, to `ariva_runtime` or to PUBLIC (on the table or a chunk), or ownership all passed. New `Ariva.Infra/Timescale/ShadowReadAccess.cs:18` (query used by `ValidationReaderGuard.cs:54` and `DatabaseMigrator.cs:85`): a MEMBER of `ariva_validation_reader` or `pg_read_all_data`, or `has_column_privilege` SELECT on any column but `zone_key` and `minute_utc` (not dropped) of `to_regclass('public.queue_minute_shadow')` and of its chunks through `pg_inherits` (grants, PUBLIC, ownership and superusers covered). Integration `Host_Should_RefuseToStart_When_TheRuntimeLoginCanReadTheShadow`: for the reader role directly and through a role, `pg_read_all_data`, a value column granted to the login and to `ariva_runtime`, the table and a chunk granted to PUBLIC, the host refuses to start and the migration job exits 1, and starts again once revoked; a superuser runtime login is refused by the host and the job. The access check names the table, so `ShadowNowcastExposureTests`' table-name scan admits `ShadowReadAccess.cs` too (privileges only, it reads no row), and its query constant is named `Query`. Docs say exactly what is checked: asvs-l2 V8.1.2, cwe-controls CWE-269, wiki 04 sections 5 and 7.1, wiki 13, decisions point (4).
  - L1 (CWE-532, CWE-287): the password reached the server as a bind parameter (logged with `log_statement = 'all'` or `log_min_duration_statement` at 0 or more, kept by `pg_stat_statements` with `track = 'all'` in the nested ALTER ROLE text). Option (a): `Ariva.Infra/Timescale/ScramVerifier.cs:35` computes the SCRAM-SHA-256 verifier (PBKDF2-SHA-256, random 16-byte salt, 4096 iterations, StoredKey and ServerKey per RFC 5802 and 7677, `SCRAM-SHA-256$4096:<salt>$<StoredKey>:<ServerKey>`); `DatabaseMigrator.cs:73` passes it; the function (`0049:54` and `:58`) accepts only that shape with at least 4096 iterations and stores it as given. The length rule stays in `ValidationReaderSettings.Problems`, which now also requires printable ASCII (`ValidationReaderSettings.cs:104`), so the verifier is exact without SASLprep. Unit `ScramVerifier_Should_MatchTheRfc7677Exchange_When_GivenItsPasswordAndSalt` (the RFC's server signature and client proof reproduced) and `..._DrawANewSaltAndRefuseFewIterations_...`; integration: `pg_authid` holds `SCRAM-SHA-256$4096:...`, not the password, the login signs in with the original password, and five non-verifiers (a password, a truncated and an md5 value, 1000 iterations, a verifier with a statement appended) are refused. The 0049 header, cwe-controls CWE-269 and wiki 13 now say what is true (the verifier, not the password, can reach such logs); the same residual for script 0001's runtime login (still created from its plain password; 0001 unchanged) is recorded in cwe-controls CWE-269, wiki 04 section 7.1, wiki 13 checklist item 9 and decisions point (10).
  - L2 (CWE-89): integration case with the password `it-quote'back\slash$$dollar"; ALTER ROLE it_vr_quote SUPERUSER;` through the migration job: the login signs in with exactly that password, a different one is refused (28P01), `rolsuper` is false and the stored password is a SCRAM verifier.
  - L3 (docs): (a) values.yaml (line 54 on) now says an upgrade without the secret fails at the pre-upgrade migration job before any pod changes, while a first install runs the job post-install and the new api-main pods wait in CreateContainerConfigError; also wiki 04 (Helm values, section 9) and decisions point (3). (b) wiki 04 section 6.2: withdrawing the reader (`ALTER ROLE ariva_validation NOLOGIN` or `REVOKE ariva_validation_reader FROM ariva_validation` as the migration login; removing the secret alone leaves the login active with its last password; in k8s-prd a withdrawal lasts until the next release, so rotation is the immediate answer to a leak). (c) asvs-l2 V8.1.2 and V13.2.2 state the residual that every host pod mounts the migration (owner) login in `base-appsettings-secret`, which owns the shadow table (ARV-083, V13.3.2 Partly).
  - L4 (ASVS V13.3.2): the row now cites the `ariva-validation-reader` secret limited to api-main and the migration job, enforced by the chart test, as evidence (still Partly).
  - L5 (CWE-863, recommended, done): `ValidationReaderSettings` is internal (`ValidationReaderSettings.cs:26`; Ariva.Di reaches it through InternalsVisibleTo); unit `ReaderSettings_Should_BeNamedOnlyByTheFiveFilesThatNeedThem_When_SourcesAreScanned` pins `\bValidationReaderSettings\b|Database:ValidationReader|ValidationReader__` to exactly the settings, the shadow read, the migrator, the guard and `PersistenceExtensions`.
  - L6 (CWE-863, recommended, done): outside vm-local a host that has not declared itself the validation service's host refuses to start when `Database:ValidationReader` is set (`ValidationReaderGuard.cs:41`; marker `ValidationReaderHost`, registered by `AddArivaValidationReaderHost`, `PersistenceExtensions.cs:55`, called only by `Ariva.Api.Main/Program.cs:93`). Unit `Guard_Should_RefuseTheReader_When_TheHostDoesNotRunTheValidationServiceOutsideVmLocal` and `Host_Should_StartWithTheReaderLoginOnlyWhenItIsMain_When_RunInACluster` (the five real hosts in k8s-dev: Main starts, the other four refuse).
  - L8 (recommended, done): the function runs with `SET search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp` (`0049:41`; the unit script test checks it).
  - L7 (rows held in memory before the row-bound refusal; pool exhaustion surfacing as an exception): carried to ARV-104g2 by the lead, not done here.

- Security review re-check (2026-10-08): FAIL; every finding of the first review closed, two items left, both fixed (not committed):
  - Blocking, introduced by the L1 fix (fails closed): wiki 04 section 6.2 created the secret from `openssl rand -base64 24 > validation-reader-password`, whose trailing newline `--from-file` stores, and the printable-ASCII rule refuses it, so api-main would not start and the migration job would stop on a k8s-prd release made as documented. The command now strips it (`openssl rand -base64 24 | tr -d '\n' > ...`), the paragraph after it says why and gives `printf '%s'` as the other way, and the refusal now says what to look for without showing the secret: "look for a control character, a trailing newline from a password file for example" (`ValidationReaderSettings.cs:104`); unit cases `password-0123456789\n` and `\r\n` (message checked). No other doc or script creates this secret from a file: the release workflows do not create it, and dev-up, the AppHost and the E2E configuration draw it in code. Reported, not changed (not this story's behaviour): the TimescaleDB chart's secret instructions in the same section (`openssl rand -base64 24 > superuser-password` and `> migration-password`, then `--from-file`) store the same trailing newline, so those two passwords end in a newline in the database, and a `Database:Migration:Password` copied into the appsettings without it does not match; `release-dev.yml` and the Azure DevOps release write those secrets with `printf '%s'` and are not affected.
  - Low (CWE-269): 0049 checked `pg_shdepend` and the database ACL of this database only, so a role owning objects or holding grants in another database of the cluster, or CREATE or TEMP there, could become the reader and its password would open that database with those rights. The function now checks `pg_shdepend` in every database (no dbid filter; only ACL entries on `pg_database` rows exempt) and the ACL of every database (CONNECT only, without grant option). New SHA-256 bab01b631f662ef780e13d03395a5cd5eb0eb821f3cff104ec75d9510f8f724d (checksums.lock). Integration: in a second fixture database (`it_validation_reader_other`, migrated) a role owning a table, one with a table grant, one with CREATE and one with TEMP there are refused here, none gets a password or the reader role, and a reader shared by both databases passes in each and on a re-run and reads the shadow in the second.

E2E not run (the coordinator runs it).
- Second re-check (2026-10-08): PASS. Both items fixed and tested (the documented secret file without a trailing newline and a refusal message that names it; the login's checks over every database of the server); every finding of the first review (M1, M2, L1 to L6, L8) stays closed and L7 is carried to ARV-104g2 (a159f2c); `node scripts/security/scan.mjs` 0 errors. The cosmetic note (wiki 04 quoted a log text that did not match the message) fixed by the lead. Final gates by the engineer: backend 3,924 unit tests, full integration alone 421 passed and 1 skipped, docs PASS. Reported and fixed separately: the TimescaleDB superuser and migration secret instructions in wiki 04 had the same trailing newline issue. Gotcha: `pgrep -f` matches a poller's own command line when the pattern is in it; check with `ps` or a pattern the poller does not contain..
- Decisions for the owner (Proposed, docs/product/decisions.md): the login's grants (reader role and CONNECT only, other reads on the runtime login); the secret optional in dev, demo and localk8s and required in k8s-prd, not written by the release workflows; hosts refusing to start when their runtime login is a member of the reader role in any way (a superuser or the migration login included, a behaviour change from a warning); the reader's bounds; the two named types in the exposure tests and the live snapshot built on the stream's side.

## 2026-10-09 ARV-104g2 Campaign results service: reads, comparison in slices and campaign verdicts (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1; passes)
- Summary: `SvcValidationResults` (Ariva.Infra/Services/Validation, partial: `.Shadow.cs` the reader login's read, `.Reads.cs` the runtime reads, `.cs` the computation) implements `ISvcValidationResults.GetAsync(siteCode, campaignId)`: the site and the campaign are checked against the caller's sites first (ISiteScope; NotFound across sites), then one computation per campaign runs apart from its callers (`SingleFlight<T>`: shared by every caller meanwhile, its own DI scope, a timeout of 3 minutes with the wait for a turn included, at most 2 campaigns at once per host; a caller that stops waiting cancels nothing). Reads: ground truth by campaign id and site; queue zones by `ZoneKeys.For`, desks by `DeskKeys.For` (exact keys only), lines by name within those keys; line counts summed per 15-minute bin from `line_minute` with `date_bin` (not `line_minute_15m`, which refreshes only 3 days); queue minutes and bins per zone and contiguous run of planned days with margins (3 minutes; bins 15 minutes before and a day and 15 minutes after); line counts, health bins and desk minutes per planned day; desk minutes only at the minutes this campaign's observers recorded for that desk; `zone_outage` as Degraded intervals (open ones end at the read's end); the shadow through the reader login per zone. Every read LIMITed to `Validation:Results:MaxRowsPerRead` (100,000) and refused beyond; at most 1,000,000 rows of a kind per campaign. Results: `ValidationResultsViewModel` (the name ShadowNowcastExposureTests reserved) with sections for ARV-104g's serving rules: desk-state results (`Desks`, border only), observer-level results (`Observers`), the shadow's figures (`Nowcast`), and the rest; built by `ValidationResultsViewModel.From`.
- Engine additions (pure, `Ariva.Core/Validation/Comparison`): `ComparisonSlices` (`TracerMinutes`, `ForTracers`, `ZoneScope`, `ZonesOf`, `Slice`, `CompareZone`, `Pool`, `Coverage`, `CompareSliced`) and `NowcastCoverage`; `NowcastErrors.Summarise` reusable for the pooled overall, `NowcastErrors.Coverage`, `NowcastErrors.MedianWithMissing`; `ComparisonData.PlannedMinutes`; `ValidationComparison.Compare(ComparisonData)` and `Nothing` split out of `Compare` (no behaviour change). Verdicts: `Ariva.Core/Validation/CampaignVerdicts.cs` (`CampaignTargets`, `CampaignVerdictSettings`, `ExclusionRule`, `CampaignCriterionVerdict`, `CampaignCriterionUnit`, `CampaignReview`).
- L7 (ARV-104g1 review): `ReadShadowAsync` counts through a LIMITed subquery before reading (refused without holding a row), still stops at the bound while reading, and maps an `NpgsqlException` (the pool of four in use until the connect timeout, a connection that cannot be opened, a server refusal) to an error result (`ValidationResultsErrors.Busy`, or a fixed text) with a log line naming only its kind; the computation's boundary maps the runtime login's connection failures the same way. The constructor of `SvcValidationResults` moved to the full service (still in `.Shadow.cs`, the one file allowed to name `ValidationReaderSettings`); tests build the reader-only service through a helper.
- Decisions for the owner (Proposed, points (1) to (13) of the decisions entry and its Open decisions row; formulas F18 "Campaign verdicts and the comparison in slices"; wiki 15 section 8 "Campaign verdicts"): strict exclusion rule by default, capped as the alternative (`Validation:Results:Exclusions`, `MaxExcludedShare` 0.05); counts of judged items per criterion (desk and nowcast minutes derived as bins per line x 15 until the campaign has targets of its own); a unit below its count is no data even when it fails; review flags; results without the shadow when no reader login is configured (`ShadowRead` false, `ProofNotRead`); slices and read bounds; calibration records without notes or person; availability over the planned days only.
- Tests: unit `CampaignVerdictTests` (22 cases: sample counts, per-unit rules, the desk and no-service levers strict and capped, availability, review flags, the projection's sections), `ComparisonSlicesTests` (9: sliced equals whole with a 1-minute sensitivity shift and a batch spanning zones, the base's tracer minutes, rows of no zone, unusable keys' order, 1,000,002 queue minutes compared in slices, invalid and zoneless scopes, `Pool` refusals, coverage), `ComparisonPropertyTests.F18_ComparisonInSlices_Should_EqualTheWholeComparison_When_TheCampaignIsWithinTheBounds` (generated campaigns, a random shift up to a minute), `SingleFlightTests` (4). Integration `ValidationResultsTests` (5, PostgreSQL with the runtime and reader logins): a seeded campaign at one site whose every criterion passes on its own rows while another site with the same zone, line and desk names and a second campaign of the same site hold rows that would fail every criterion (counts 100 against 98, tracers 8 against 8.2, nowcast error 0.2, shadow 0.8, desks 15 of 15, track completion 0.95, 100 of 101 minutes available, one calibration record without its notes); sliced equals whole on PostgreSQL; NotFound across sites and a cross-site campaign that reads nothing of the other site; results without a reader login; the shadow read refused at its bound and a full reader pool answered with an error result. The seeded campaign is planted rows, not the simulator's reference evening: exact expected figures and cross-site traps need rows written for them.

- Security review, first verdict (2026-10-09): FAIL, no High; site and campaign isolation holds. Findings M1 to M3 and L1 to L7; M3 and L7 carried to ARV-104g by the lead. Fixed (not committed):
  - M1 (CWE-120, CWE-400): the runtime reads are `internal` and tested at their limits on PostgreSQL (`ValidationResultsTests.Reads_Should_BeRefusedRatherThanCutShort_When_TheyPassTheRowLimit`: desk states, a ground-truth read, 15 at 14 refused and at 15 read; Entry A's line bins, a per-day read, 4 at 3 refused; Q-A's 61 queue minutes, a per-zone read, at 60 refused; queue bins 18 at 17 refused; the computation answers `TooLarge` at limits 14 and 60 and compares at 61). The runtime pool boundary is `SvcValidationResults.Guarded` (`SvcValidationResults.cs`), unit-tested with an `NpgsqlException` wrapped in NHibernate's `GenericADOException`: every caller of the flight gets `Busy`, a defect is not swallowed (`SingleFlightTests.Guarded_Should_AnswerEveryCallerOfTheFlightWithBusy_When_TheRuntimePoolIsExhausted`).
  - M2 (CWE-501, fail open): track completion no longer drops a zone without tracks (`CampaignVerdicts.TrackCompletion`): every zone in scope is a unit, and a zone that counted no track makes the criterion no data (reason `ZonesWithoutTracks`) when another zone counts tracks, not in scope when none does, a failing zone still failing; review flag `ZonesWithoutTracks`. The capability tier is not recorded on devices, so this is the fail-closed fallback (decisions point 14, Proposed). Tests `TrackCompletion_Should_FailClosed_When_AZoneInScopeCountedNoTrackAllCampaign`, `TrackCompletion_Should_PassOnlyWhenEveryZoneCountsTracksAndPasses_When_EachHasItsGoodBins`; the integration seed now gives Q-B tracks.
  - L1: `SingleFlightTests.RunAsync_Should_HideTheCallersAsyncLocalState_When_TheWorkRuns`. L2: `ComparisonSlices.Pool` refuses zone results that are not exactly one per zone of the base's scope (twice, missing, foreign; `Pool_Should_RefuseZoneResultsThatAreNotOneZoneOfTheBasesVersion_When_Given`). L3: desk minutes read per 12 hours (`HalfDays`), a campaign still beyond the bound refused (decisions point 16). L4: `SingleFlight.Start` checks `ExecutionContext.IsFlowSuppressed()` and `RunAsync` removes a flight that could not start (`RunAsync_Should_StartAndLeaveNoBrokenFlight_When_TheCallerHasAlreadySuppressedTheFlow`). L5: review flag `NowcastMissing` for `NowcastCoverage.Missing` (`Review_Should_FlagPlannedMinutesWithoutAPublishedNowcast_When_TheCoverageMissesAny`; the integration campaign raises it for 05:59). L6: `CollidingDeskKeys` refuses a campaign whose desks share a key (`DeskKeysCollide`; theory with '/' in codes).

## 2026-10-09 ARV-104g Validation results frozen at campaign close and served as JSON (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1; passes)
- Summary: `GET api/v1/sites/{siteCode}/validation/campaigns/{id}/results` (`ValidationResultsController`, OpsArea/Validation; `Validation.View`, `[SiteScoped]`, 404 across sites, `?revision=n`) serves the ARV-104g2 results as JSON, projected per caller; `POST .../results/revisions` (`Validation.Manage`, `[RequiresRecentMfa]`, a new critical action "Recompute validation results", body at most 4 KB) adds a revision with a reason. Both carry the `validation-results` rate limiting policy (concurrency per host before authentication, 4 at once and 8 waiting for a turn, 429 with Retry-After; `Security:RateLimiting:ValidationResults`) and a request timeout (`AddRequestTimeouts`, `Validation:Results:RequestTimeout` 90 s, 503 with Retry-After; `UseRequestTimeouts` in Main after authorization). Main registers the ARV-104g2 services through `Ariva.Di/Extensions/ValidationExtensions.AddArivaValidationResults` (settings `Validation:Results`, the `SingleFlight<ValidationResultsViewModel>` singleton, `SvcValidationResults` scoped, `ISvcValidationResults`, the background freeze, the timeout policy).
- Freeze (script 0050_validation_result_revision.sql, SHA-256 250880cb3424ef538976a9cfdf3ff61f9431474f369bf6e5f00541268d1e338b in checksums.lock after the review's L1 fix; first cdd11f43ff46a9559938cc2c2e18ebd43f80910e7e833b9e8ef3ba051bc530aa, not merged): `validation_result_revision` holds per closed campaign revision 1 (frozen by the system, no reason, no person) and later revisions (a reason of 1 to 500 characters and who asked), the results document as exact bytes (`bytea`, 2 bytes to 16 MB) and `content_sha256`, which a CHECK ties to `encode(sha256(document), 'hex')`; an insert trigger accepts revisions only for a closed campaign and only as the next number; the 0047 immutability trigger refuses UPDATE and DELETE for every login and the runtime role holds no UPDATE, DELETE or TRUNCATE. `SvcValidationResults.Revisions.cs`: a closed campaign without a revision is frozen by the first computation after the close, the background `ValidationResultsFreezer` on Main (every `FreezeInterval`, 1 minute; a failed campaign retried after `FreezeRetryAfter`, 1 hour) or the first read, through one flight (`SingleFlight` keyed by campaign and purpose now: live, freeze, recompute), in a scope of its own as the system user `validation-results-freeze`, audited `ValidationResults.Frozen` against the revision; the stored row is read back so what is served is what is stored. Every read checks the hash again and that the document names the requested campaign and site (500 `Corrupt` otherwise). A recomputation computes in the `recompute` flight and inserts the next revision with the audit `ValidationResults.Recomputed` in the request's unit of work. Campaigns not closed are computed when asked and never stored.
- M3 (memory) decision, Proposed (decisions.md ARV-104g point 1): the computation stays on api-main (the reader login lives only there; api-cronz has the same 1000Mi), one computation at a time per host (`MaxConcurrentComputations` default 2 to 1, at most 4), and a campaign holds at most 400 zone-days (`ValidationCampaign.MaxZoneDays`, refused at planning with `ValidationErrors.TooManyZoneDays` and before any read with `ValidationResultsErrors.TooManyZoneDays`). Measured: a probe at ARV-104a's bounds (50 zones, 31 days, 2.23 million nowcast minutes) retained 679 MB (about 305 bytes a minute) and peaked at 1.2 GB sampled; at 400 zone-days (50 zones, 8 days) `ValidationResultsMemoryTests` measures about 196 MB of live heap at the peak (full collections while sampling), 7 s, a stored document of 187 KB. The per-minute lists left the results (`NowcastView.Minutes`, `DesksView.Minutes`; 389 bytes of JSON a minute); every summary counts its minutes. The way back to 50 x 31 is a nowcast summary that keeps error values per category instead of every compared minute (engine change, proposed as its own story).
- Serving rules (L7): `ValidationResultsViewModel.Reader.Of(roles, userId)` from the caller's stored roles (`CallerRoles`, never the token) and `ValidationResultsViewModel.For(reader)`: the desk section only for `BorderDeskAccess.Sees`, observer-level results (runs, offsets, batches, count keys, the desk-state keys with observers) every observer's for View or Manage, the caller's own for Capture only, none otherwise; the shadow's figures (zones' and overall `Shadow` and `Both`, left-out shadow minutes and their keys, the `ProofNotRead` flag) only for View. The answer carries `audience`. The results leave the service only as `ValidationResultsJson` (the projection's JSON written by the results type, camelCase, enums by name), so no controller holds the results (ShadowNowcastExposureTests unchanged and passing). `ISvcValidationResults` exposes `GetAsync` and `RecomputeAsync` only (the lead's note said GetAsync only; the recomputation was added to meet "a recomputation adds a revision"; decisions point 9).
- Data boundary row "Validation comparison results", ASVS evidence (V8.2.3, V14.1.1, V15.1.3, V15.2.2, V15.3.1), CWE rows 862, 863 and 120, wording of track completion's NotInScope ("no zone counted tracks (not tracked, or every tracker silent)", next to `ZonesWithoutTracks`) in the enum's documentation, formulas F18 "Frozen results and serving" and wiki 15.

Tests `Security/ValidationResultsLimitsTests` (Main in-process, a fake results service held open): the policy saturated (PermitLimit 1, QueueLimit 0) answers 429 with Retry-After 10 and the held request still answers 200; a computation that outlasts a 5 s RequestTimeout answers 503 with Retry-After 30 and the service sees its wait cancelled.
  - M2 (CWE-863, data boundary): asvs-l2 V8.1.2 and V13.2.2 now state the residual: the revision documents hold the shadow's zone-level aggregates (no minute) readable by the runtime role, guarded by the source scan `ValidationResultsExposureTests` and the projection, owner decision Proposed (decisions.md ARV-104g point 5), alternative SELECT on `validation_result_revision` only to `ariva_validation_reader`.

The trigger now refuses only a number beyond the latest plus one (a taken one is left to the unique key), and a refused insert returns an error without reading (`InsertAsync` true, false or null). Integration `Freeze_Should_KeepOneRevisionAndAnswerEveryCaller_When_SeveralFreezeTheSameCampaignAtOnce` (four freezes in separate scopes at once: one revision, one hash for all). Script 0050 changed (not merged): new checksum above.
  - L2: recorded as a residual in cwe-controls (CWE-863 row): the hash is unkeyed; a login that can write the table (owner or superuser, not the runtime role) could replace a document and its hash together; the audit entry records the hash for an auditor; a keyed hash or a signature outside the database would close it. Not compared at read time because the same owner could change the audit entry too.
  - L3: GET and the recomputation only read before waiting on the flight, and reads open no transaction (NHibernate releases the connection after each statement outside one), so no connection is held while waiting; noted in the code and checked in the concurrent-freeze test (`IsTransactionActive` false after a read that froze a campaign).
