The missing crossings are exactly the bin's `late_events` (A-VIS 18:00 bin: 351 crossings, 240 counted, 111 late). Cause (pipeline): Ingest splits one push into topic batches (crossings, occupancy, health) and Stream steps a zone's reference clock to each batch's receive time; a one-minute push arrives about 61 seconds after its first event, so when the occupancy or health batch of the same push (or another device of the zone) is processed first, the crossings older than the 30-second lateness allowance are late; late events only count in `late_events` and raise recomputation requests that nothing in the live path carries out. Track completion fails the same way (A-VIS 0.69, A-RES 0.88, fragmented tracks).
  - Desk-state agreement 0.10 (throughput agreement 0.84): AMAN's interval statistics become `DeskTransactionsCompleted`, which only moves `LastTransaction`; no transaction start reaches the desk engine, so an AMAN-only desk (DMO's profile has no staff or service zones) is never Serving (F10 row 3): observed Serving is Idle in the system, and an idle desk is Paused after T1 (3 minutes without a transaction, row 5).
  - Availability 0 of 61 minutes: every minute StreamLag and MissingMinute. Harness: the E2E stack gives Cronz `Availability:GraceSeconds` 30 for availability.spec.ts's planted minutes, while the live pipeline writes a minute's live part about two minutes after it ends (one-minute pushes, 30-second lateness), so the ledger decided each minute before Stream had it. Fixed in the harness: the live pipeline scopes give Cronz the production grace (120 seconds). The minutes from Cronz's first pass (05:29) to the stream's first live minute (05:32 onwards) remain unavailable: the site existed before its sensors were registered.
  - Nowcast error passes (median 0.36 minutes over 42 judged minutes); the wait error and bias had too few tracers in a 30-minute window (3 of 6).

## 2026-10-10 ARV-148 Stream on event time: watermarks from the events, not the batch's arrival (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1)
- What changed: the queue state engine runs on event time (owner decision of 2026-10-10 on ARV-104j findings F1 and F4). A zone's clock is the latest event time it has accepted, each event counting at most up to the receive time of its batch (CWE-501: a device whose clock runs ahead cannot move the watermark past Ariva's receive clock), and once the zone has had an event it never falls more than the push interval behind the receive time; the watermark is that clock less the lateness, and an event at or behind it is late as before (counted, recomputation asked). New engine setting `PushInterval` (60 s); `Lateness` default 90 s (was 30). Events of one instant are applied in a fixed order of their content (interval readings first, then crossings, readings and track samples; then line or zone, direction, track and counts) rather than arrival order. `ZoneProcessor`: devices observed once per new receive time before its records; open outages marked at each new receive time through the minute the push interval before it (was every 5 minutes through the receive time); an outage ends at the start of the minute the receive time of the device's first push after it less the push interval falls in (F4); a bin is final only once that minute has passed its end (`BinAccumulator.Accept(step, finalThroughUtc)`; implied by the watermark whenever the lateness is at least the push interval). Snapshot version 9 carries the engine clock (`QueueEngineState.ClockUtc`; older snapshots restore with the latest of the watermark and the held events; a clock ahead of the zone's receive clock or behind a held event is refused). `StreamSettings`: `LatenessSeconds` (90), `PushIntervalSeconds` (60) and `Sites:<code>` overrides (at most 500, keys are site codes, lateness at least the push interval or the host does not start); the worker and the replay runner (and `--replay`) build each site's zone settings from them. `ZoneReplay.Settle` adds the push interval.
- Golden replays regenerated (owner approval of 2026-10-10): seed 9303 2b047dfce6d587d26ac5f99e4dd9c0817627b3b63d21792caf4f0a1cc6abd05e over 3,694 outputs (was 3cce63edfb17660f53a3fb7eead6c8ae92b20c491135b406a7df8cb032199a6f over 3,202), without health rows 185bb1314cf53d6853fdcbbcadc0da691b865654e2cb9670e20518a1c4970963 over 3,042 (was 8d13749079c0c0ca347dcd8c9e41f6708a7360b7145ad61af32c4d49cf0e0a2c over 2,716); the overflow case 0a38ac8833154f3810780e1360f77996b417fc8c3b04ce422e8bed7dd1ff12a8 over 1,966 (was 8df69d9f46b3eb15d871b5e0e37c82f0c52ac62d2f1db6e022b0df0d7db569ba over 1,693). Reason: event time, and S-17's outage now 18:20 to 18:30 with only the Visitors 18:15 bin Degraded (18:30 judged, Good); the line minutes and overflow rows are unchanged, the extra rows are provisional revisions (the watermark moves with each batch of a push), and the zone's first watermark adds an empty 16:45 bin and a 16:58 live minute. The AUH-TA replay (no locked value) changes likewise (Q-RES-04 18:25 to 18:35, was 18:36); confirmed by the owner 2026-10-10, as are the 90-second default lateness and the regenerated overflow replay (decisions.md).
- Tests: `QueueEngineWatermarkTests` (table of the watermark rule, the boundary at exactly the lateness, a 61-second-old push with another batch first, the far-ahead device at engine and zone level, the content order of an instant, the overflow evening split into single-event batches and shuffled across topics giving the same final bins, minutes and line counts, F4 on the evening, the silence observed before the records of a receive time, the finality rule under a lateness below the push interval, a late event counted and recomputed, a restore mid-push, hostile and pre-9 snapshot clocks), `StreamTimingSettingsTests` (defaults, per-site overrides, refusals), the push interval in `EngineSettingsBoundaryTests`; `QueueStreamEventTimeTests` (integration, a broker of its own): two zones fed the same minute-old pushes, one per push and one split into single-crossing records produced newest first, under the site override (`Stream:Sites:DMO` 90/60 beside a global 30/30): no late event, every crossing on its line, the same bins and line minutes, S-72's outage 17:20 to 17:30 and the 17:30 bin judged (with the override removed, 321 late events). Existing tests adapted: engine, band, line, health and bin tests that are about other things run on the receive clock (`PushInterval` 0, the rule before ARV-148); the liveness, golden, AUH-TA and alert evaluation (R-003 for S-17 now clears at 18:30) tests take the new outage end.
- Checked by hand that the tests have teeth: removing the finality rule fails the two cases of `Zone_Should_KeepABinProvisional_When_AnOutageMinuteInItIsNotKnownYet`; counting ahead events at their own time fails three engine tests; ordering an instant by arrival fails the content-order test and the split-step test.
- Gotchas: (1) the worker merges each partition in its offset order, so an integration test that produces records with receive times overlapping another test's on a shared broker holds one topic's records of a zone behind them and not the other's, and the zone's clock jumps (a real Ingest appends in receive order): the event-time test has its own class and broker. (2) With a lateness of at least the push interval the watermark never passes a bin's end before the outage minutes in it are known, so the finality rule only binds below that (only engine settings can go there). (3) A T1 device's interval reading was spread over nothing when its own occupancy reading of the same instant was applied first (the stream's topic order did that); interval readings now come first in an instant. (4) xUnit v3 `TheoryData<T>` collection expressions need `new T(...)`, and `System.Random` is refused by CA5394 (Bogus' Randomizer). (5) A full unit run started while Docker was down sat idle for 24 minutes; with Docker up it ran in 3 minutes. (6) My slip: one recursive grep for the old fingerprints was started from the repository root and so walked the closed client folder; it was stopped and printed nothing from it; later searches name the project folders only. (7) Wiki 16 line 99 described the golden replay's S-17 outage as 18:20 to 18:31 with 18:15 and 18:30 marked; corrected in the review follow-up (that line only).

Security review not yet run (the lead runs it); mutation runs at the next checkpoint. The ARV-104j rehearsal was not run.

Low 1 (CWE-501): a snapshot could carry an unset engine clock beside a moved watermark; `SnapshotChecks` and `QueueStateEngine.Restore` now refuse it (the case added to `Restore_Should_RefuseTheClock_When_ItIsAheadOfTheZoneOrBehindTheEventsItHolds`, at zone and engine level). Decisions points (1) and (8), the Open decisions row, the overflow replay's comment and this entry now read confirmed by the owner 2026-10-10.

The engine learns the lane context per desk from a new signal, `DeskLaneContext` (s = the lane cycle time per person of ARV-117d for the queue zones the desk serves, Q = the people in those zones from their latest live minute), which the desk feed builds once per whole minute with the desk term's queue to desk match (`DeskRhythm.Contexts`, `DeskFeed.LaneContextsAsync`). Without a context the rows reduce to rows 5 and 7 exactly (P = T1, no rhythm), so every existing desk test is unchanged. ## 2026-10-10 ARV-150 Availability ledger from profile publication (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1)
- Plan (work notes, kept as the story runs): owner decision 2026-10-10 on F3 of ARV-104j. A site's ledger starts at the first whole minute at or after the first publication of its zone profile (the earliest `published_on` of its published or retired versions, immutable by the 0012 trigger); earlier minutes are never written, a later version never moves the start, and a site never published has no ledger. Core (`AvailabilityRule`, pure, mutated): `FirstMinute`, `EarliestLive` (moved from the ledger) and `Start` (the stored cursor or, for a site seen the first time, its first minute while that is still live or ahead, else the latest decidable minute as in ARV-118; a first publication more than 60 s ahead of the job's clock is not trusted, CWE-501). Infra (`AvailabilityLedger.SiteAsync`): reads the first publication, writes nothing for a site never published, and stores the cursor at the start on first sight even when no minute is decidable yet, so a missed run cannot move the start. Rule version 2 from this release (wiki 10 4.8k: a rule change is a new rule version, past rows never rewritten). No script: the start is derived from `zone_profile`, nothing new is stored. CWEs: 501 (the stored publication time checked against the job's clock), 863 (one site per pass, site isolation unchanged).
- Progress: Core, Infra, unit and integration tests and the docs (formulas F18, decisions, CWE-501 row) written. First story gate launched 14:20 UTC (detached, after the other gates on the machine ended): build 0 errors; unit tests 4,477, 4,476 passed and 1 failed, the failure ARV-149's `DeskRhythmScenarioTests.Engine_Should_AgreeWithTheObservers_When_DesksWithoutZonesFollowTheTransactionRhythm` (0.887 against 0.95; ARV-149's work in progress, not this story's); every `AvailabilityRuleTests` case passed (50, the 13 new ones included). The gate goes on to the full integration and E2E suites (the tree is wide through ARV-148's files).
- Progress (14:40 UTC): the first gate's integration step ran the full suite (the tree is wide): 444 tests, 443 passed, 1 skipped (the existing skip), 0 failed; 4 more than ARV-148's last run, the 4 new `AvailabilityTests`. E2E (full) running. `docs/product/pilot-to-confirm.md` TC-83 now notes the amendment.

Build 0 errors; reference golden PASS; unit 4,477 tests, 4,476 passed, 1 failed (ARV-149's `DeskRhythmScenarioTests`, see above); scanner self-test (42 rules) and scan PASS (0 errors, the 6 pending allowlist warnings); base images, chart security, NuGet and npm audits PASS; docs text rules PASS; integration 444 tests, 443 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed; E2E 637, 636 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed, none flaky (`availability.spec.ts` 9 of 9, the Cronz ledger test included). Then `node scripts/security/scan.mjs --changed` (54 files, 0 errors, 0 warnings) and `node scripts/verify.mjs quick` PASS (changed-files scan, docs text rules).
- Not done here (first hand-back): the security-reviewer verdict (CWE-501; the lead runs it); the wiki 15 section 8 (Availability) and wiki 10 section 4.8k sentences were handed to the lead, who put them in the tree (they carry other stories' uncommitted changes). `backlog/prd-phase1.json` untouched (passes stays false).
- Gotchas: the ledger's new `SELECT min(published_on)` runs before the time zone check, so a never-published site is a no-op whatever its airport. A site seen while its first minute is ahead of the latest decidable minute now stores its cursor at once (before ARV-150 nothing was stored until a minute was decidable); the existing tests that plant a profile a month back are unchanged because such a site still starts at the latest decidable minute. Integration tests in `AvailabilityTests` share one database: a test that calls `RunAsync` decides every planted site at its own clock, so new tests use `SiteAsync` only.
- Security review (2026-10-10, security-reviewer, through the lead): FAIL on two Medium CWE-501 findings, the core control holding. (1) A site first seen while its airport's time zone was unknown, or any first-sight pass that failed and rolled back for longer than the live window, lost its start: once the zone resolved, `Start` gave the latest decidable minute and every minute from the publication to the fix left the denominator. (2) The trust anchor (the 0012 trigger on `zone_profile`) had no test. Recommended: a warning when a publication too far ahead is ignored; no gap when such a publication becomes trusted; the two exceptions in wiki 15 and TC-83; "immutable" corrected (the trigger is BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE: an insert can add an earlier version); `AvailabilityRule.cs` in Stryker's mutate list; the annex to fix each pilot site and its measurement start.
- Fixes (all, uncommitted; re-review not yet run): `AvailabilityRule.KeepsStart` (a first sight at the first minute) and the ledger store that start before the time zone is resolved and commit it with nothing else (the unresolved-zone branch commits; an existing cursor is unmoved; the minutes are decided from the next run, so no failure later in a pass can lose it); `Start` never moves a stored cursor (a ledger that went on while its publication was not trusted continues from its cursor, no minute unwritten); `IsPlausiblePublication` and a once-per-site warning naming only the site; integration tests `Ledger_Should_KeepTheStart_When_TheTimeZoneIsUnknownAtFirstSight` (published 08:02:00 at an unknown zone, skipped at 08:02:10 and 08:07:10, corrected at 08:20:10: 16 minutes from 08:02, 13 caught up as Unobserved) and `ZoneProfile_Should_RefuseTheRuntimeLogin_When_ItTriesToMoveALedgersStart` (a real runtime login: UPDATE of `published_on`, DELETE and return to Draft of a published and of a retired version each 23001; an inserted earlier version moves the first publication earlier but not the stored start), the far-ahead test with its warning, the republication test's first pass storing the start; unit tests for `KeepsStart`, `IsPlausiblePublication`, the stored cursor and the settings' bounds. ## 2026-10-10 ARV-151 Simulator truth spreads service over the open desks (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1)
- Plan (work notes, kept as the story runs): owner decision 2026-10-10 on ARV-149's light-demand finding. One rule in two implementations: the people a lane serves in a minute (D) are shared among its working desks (open and not paused, or hidden from the sensors: the desks `AmanDeskProcess` works) by speed, D x (1 / svc_k) / sum of (1 / svc), each working svc_k seconds per person, so every working desk works D / sum of (1 / svc) seconds; a desk is `serving` when it works at least 30 seconds of the minute (`ScenarioDay.ServingSeconds`) or people wait, `idle` otherwise. sim.js `day.work` and `day.servers`, the C# port `ScenarioDay.WorkSeconds` and `ServerStates` (the golden fingerprints hold them together), `ValidationTruth.Serves` for the observers' view of a desk hidden from the sensors, and `AmanDeskProcess.WorkedSeconds` (not published; the seconds each desk worked in a minute, its figures unchanged) for the person-by-person test. Held-out evening: seed 2026 of the reference day (AUH-TA has no AMAN feed, so its 9304 cannot test AMAN-only desks), ingested as the reference evening (`ReferenceReplay.IngestSeed`). ## 2026-10-11 ARV-114b Health-check alarms and degraded propagation (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1; passes true)
- Plan: the three continuous checks of ARV-114a (conservation residual, track completion, occupancy against the physical capacity, per queue zone and bin in `zone_health_bin`) become typed alert metrics judged like the others (the same `AlertEvaluator` fold live and in backtest), and track completion becomes an F11 input of the bin result, so the flag flows wherever a bin's flag already flows. CWEs: CWE-94 (typed metrics only, exact enum names, the tables' checks), CWE-862 (no new endpoint; every rule endpoint keeps its `[Permission]`), with CWE-863 for the site-scoped reads.
- Built: `AlertMetric` gains `ConservationResidual` (people, 0 to 100,000), `TrackCompletion` (percent, 0 to 100) and `OccupancyOutOfRange` (minutes, 0 to 1,440), appended to the enum and stored by name; `AlertRuleValues.MaxThreshold` and `IsHealthCheck`. The pure `HealthAlertSeries` (Ariva.Core/Alerting) gives each stored queue minute the value of the bin that ended last by the minute's end (latest revision, held for 15 minutes), once that value is settled (residual: both boundaries measured; occupancy: end boundary sampled; completion: final, or ended with no track or every track exited), and `SettledThrough` stops the live evaluation before the first minute of a bin not yet settled (a bin unsettled 180 minutes after its end no longer holds it back). `AlertInputs` reads the rows (`HealthRowsAsync`, `LatestHealthMinutesAsync`, parameterised SQL by zone key and time) and keeps health rules to queue zones; `AlertEvaluation` uses the settled latest minute for them; `SvcAlertRules` refuses an overflow zone on a health rule (`AlertRuleErrors.QueueZonesOnly`). F11: `BinSettings.TrackCompletionDegradedBelow` (0.90, 0 disables) and `HealthChecks.TrackCompletionBelow` make a final bin below it `Degraded` in `BinAccumulator.Result`; provisional bins are not judged. The validation comparison's track completion judges a final bin below its target whatever the bin's own Degraded flag (`ComparisonData.Over(..., binDegradedCounts)`), so the failing bins cannot leave the criterion; outages still set bins apart. Script 0051 (`0051_health_alarms.sql` since round 2, SHA-256 a354d39229dddf67a46fd8290daef388e3423cfc7ad40e5b5fe9fed8e39ab70e; round 1 had it as `0051_alert_health_metrics.sql` without the queue_bin column) replaces the metric-list and threshold-unit checks of `alert_rule` and the metric list of `alert`. Screen: the rule form offers the three metrics with their units (percent added), a hint per metric, and only queue zones for them; English and Arabic strings.
- Proposed points for the owner: docs/product/decisions.md, ARV-114b entry, points (1) to (9), and an Open decisions row (the F11 rule on final bins only; residual and occupancy alarm without flagging; units and values; per queue zone and bin with the latest ended bin's value; the settled-value wait; queue zones only; the comparison's track completion rule; no seeded health rules; what is left for later).
- Replays: unchanged. The golden head stays 2b047dfce6d587d26ac5f99e4dd9c0817627b3b63d21792caf4f0a1cc6abd05e over 3,694 outputs (every track of the reference evening completes) and the locked overflow case keeps its head.

- Gotchas: a health rule that judged minutes as soon as the queue minute existed would skip them without a value (the bin not yet final) and never judge them again, so live and backtest would differ; the settled-value bound is what makes them equal (the unit test proves both). Marking low-completion bins Degraded would have emptied the validation criterion of its failing bins; the comparison now judges them. A Bash command holding an attack payload with destructive SQL is refused by the hook even inside a test string; write such specs with the editor.

Round 2 FAIL: no persistence test for queue_bin.degraded_by_completion_only, and a revision changing only the flag was not written. Round 3 FAIL: the lead's flag cross-check left the zone slices without health rows, so production's sliced comparison dropped those bins' nowcast minutes silently. Round 4: each slice carries its zone's health rows, read with the bins' margins; sliced equals whole with flagged bins (unit, property and service tests); every production builder of a comparison input checked. Final scan --changed 38 files, 0 errors.

## 2026-10-11 ARV-119 Weekly summary figures per site and local week (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1; passes true)
- Plan: the weekly figures only (the owner's decision of 2026-10-08 moved templates, documents, CSV, schedules, delivery and any endpoint to ARV-146): a pure builder in Ariva.Core/Reports fed by one reader of stored rows, served by a site-scoped service returning `Result<WeeklySummary>`, shaped as plain records (no value tuples) so ARV-146 can hand it on as JSON. CWEs: CWE-863 (site scope, per-role projection, border per-desk data), CWE-120 (one week per request, every read bounded and refused beyond its bound), with CWE-89 (constant, parameterised SQL).
- Built: `WeeklySummary` and its records, `WeeklySummaryInput`, `WeeklySummaryLimits` and `WeeklySummaries` (calendar, week check, build) in Ariva.Core/Reports; `ISvcWeeklySummary` and `WeeklySummaryErrors` in Ariva.Core/Services/Reports; `WeeklySummaryReader` and `SvcWeeklySummary` in Ariva.Infra/Services/Reports; `AvailabilityLedgerReader` (the ledger's per-day query moved out of `SvcAvailability` unchanged, so the availability read and the summary count alike); `DailyReports.Uptime` made internal for the week; registrations in `AccountExtensions` (Ariva.Api.Main). No endpoint, no script, nothing stored. The local week is computed in .NET (`SiteTimeZones`, the daily report's `DayBounds`) and handed to PostgreSQL as hour and day bounds, grouped with `width_bucket`, so a Berlin autumn week has 169 hours and PostgreSQL's time zone data never decides a day; minute histograms are merged per lane, hour and bucket in the database (at most one row per lane and hour), percentiles taken in .NET by F7.
- Proposed points for the owner: docs/product/decisions.md, ARV-119 entry, points (1) to (13), and an Open decisions row (Monday weeks in the site's zone, a site with an unknown zone refused; peak hours by the daily report's rule and the site's ten worst lane-hours; time to acknowledge over acknowledged alerts only; device uptime as the daily report; data quality from the latest bin revisions with track completion from the sums; overflow minutes read from `overflow_minute` with the view's rule; desk-state lane totals for every reader with small border lanes withheld; the service's own `Report.View` check; the bounds).
- Tests: unit `Ariva.UnitTests/Reports/WeeklySummaryTests` (29: calendars for Dubai, Berlin's 167 and 169 hour weeks, Beirut's skipped midnight, Lord Howe Island's half-hour change; the week check; merged percentiles for hour, day and week; peaks with the 20-wait rule and the ten-hour list; empty days; flags; rows outside the week or of another zone ignored; overflow; data quality from sums and left out without `DataQuality.View`; availability days; alerts and time to acknowledge; device uptime in a running week; desk rows for border roles only, small border lanes withheld, airport lanes for airport roles only; the JSON round trip). Integration `Ariva.IntegrationTests/Reports/WeeklySummaryTests` (7, database `it_weekly_summary`): the reference week built from the simulator (seed 9303: the evening driven through `ZoneProcessor` as the stream does, the overflow case's band minutes, the arrival desks' states from `ScenarioDay.ServerStates`), Thursday a copy, a neighbour site DMO-X holding a copy of every row with the same checkpoint and desk codes; each lane's day equals the daily report, every hour equals the engine's own minutes merged, DMO-X never counts, an airport role gets no desk row and CRW withheld, availability equals the ledger read, refusals for other sites, roles, weeks and an unknown zone, reads refused one below each bound and whole at it, Berlin's 169-hour week on the database, a running week and a week before the data.
- Gates (detached, fresh services): `node scripts/verify.mjs backend` early: PASS, exit 0 (build 0 errors, the 2 NU1510 warnings as before; reference golden up to date; unit 4,673 of 4,673; scan 1,237 files, 0 errors, the 6 pending warnings; chart security and the audits PASS). Story gate on fresh services, `node scripts/verify.mjs story --integration "FullyQualifiedName~Ariva.IntegrationTests.Reports|FullyQualifiedName~Ariva.IntegrationTests.Availability"` (15:00 to 15:30 UTC; wide through `Ariva.Di`, so the full suites): PASS, exit 0; build PASS; reference golden up to date; unit 4,671 of 4,671 (the TRX recorded three existing `HealthChecksTests` theory cases under one unknown test id; nothing failed); scanner self-test and scan 1,237 files, 0 errors, the 6 pending warnings; base images, chart security, audits, docs PASS; integration 478 passed and 1 skipped of 479; E2E 643 passed and 1 skipped. After the gate, a hardening of my own review: `WeeklySummaryReader` refuses a site code outside the site pattern itself (the key prefix is a LIKE pattern; in SQL 'DM_/%' matches DMO's keys), with its test in `Reads_Should_BeRefusedRatherThanCutShort_When_TheyPassTheirBound`; `WeeklySummaryTests` (integration) rerun 7 of 7. Then on fresh services: `node scripts/security/scan.mjs --changed` 13 files, 0 errors, 0 warnings; `node scripts/verify.mjs quick` PASS; final `node scripts/verify.mjs backend` PASS, exit 0 (build 0 errors, no warning in a changed file; unit 4,673 of 4,673; scan 1,237 files, 0 errors, the 6 pending warnings; golden, chart security and the audits PASS). The security review is the lead's step.
- Gotchas: the replay ledger's JSON does not carry a minute's wait histogram back (value tuples have no properties for System.Text.Json), so outputs read back from `ReferenceReplay.Run` store minutes whose percentiles are null; a test that needs real percentiles must drive `ZoneProcessor` itself (as `ZoneReplay.Run` does) and keep the drained outputs. The same reason made the summary's records tuple-free. `overflow_bin_15m` is a plain view that groups before filtering, so reading it for one week makes the hypertable read every chunk of the site; the summary reads `overflow_minute` with the view's rule instead. Ariva.Di is on the wide list, so registering a service widens the story gate to the full suites.
- Round 2 (security review FAIL, two required fixes and five recommendations, 2026-10-11): (1) CWE-863 and the data boundary: the small-cell rule counted desks with any minute over the whole week and then published all seven days, so a lane of three desks with one open on a day gave a terminal duty manager that desk's own day (closed desks counted too: the desk engine writes every desk's minute), and a week shown beside some withheld days could give them by difference. Now, for a reader without `BorderDesks.View`, each border cell is decided on the desks with open time in it (idle, serving or paused above 0): the week only with at least 3, the days only when every day with open time had 3 (all days or none), the desk count only with the week; `WeeklyDeskLane` carries `WeekWithheld` and `DaysWithheld`, `WeeklyDeskDay` its open desks. Unit tests: three configured desks with one open withheld; three open over the week with a day of one shows the week and no day, nothing in the JSON gives the day; every day with three shown whole; the fixture's airport-role test checks the days. Integration: the duty manager's lanes and days checked cell by cell against the simulator's own desk states. (2) The desk key shared across checkpoint kinds: integration `DeskStates_Should_CountNoMinuteOfAKeyShared_When_ACheckpointCodeIsReusedByAnotherKind` (a soft-deleted check-in checkpoint IMM with a counter AR-23 beside a live arrival desk AR-23; its hour counted for no caller, the 22 desks unchanged); without `k.kinds = 1` it fails (the duty manager's check-in lane reads 150 minutes, not 90), restored. ## 2026-10-11 ARV-120 Overflow and data-quality views (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1; passes true)
- Plan: the screens for what ARV-114a, ARV-114b and ARV-115 store: an overflow marker per queue on the live and immigration screens, a data-quality panel per zone (conservation residual, track completion, occupancy range) for `DataQuality.View` holders only, and the health-check alarms per zone on the devices screen. No report generation (ARV-146). CWEs: CWE-79 (zone, band and rule names as text) and CWE-863 (site scope, the permissions, the alert screens' role rule, no border desk data to airport roles), with CWE-89 and CWE-120 for the new read.
- Endpoint added (no read gave the screens the bands' state): `GET api/v1/sites/{siteCode}/overflow-bands` (`OverflowBandsController` in Controllers/OpsArea/Live, `ISvcOverflowBands` with the pure `OverflowBandStates` in Ariva.Core/Services/Live, `SvcOverflowBands` in Ariva.Infra/Services/Live, registered beside the desk states in `FlightExtensions`), `LiveQueue.View` and `[SiteScoped]` as the live hub's zone snapshots: the site through `ISiteScope` first (404 as for a site that does not exist), then one constant, parameterised statement that lists the overflow bands of the site's published profile with each band's latest `overflow_minute` row of the last 6 minutes (a LATERAL read by the table's key, one row per band, at most 500 bands). A band is Occupied (anyone in its latest minute with a reading), Empty (0) or NotHeard (no reading in the window, never read as empty); a queue zone is Occupied when any band is. Zone-level occupancy only. Permission matrix row added (401 anonymous, 403 the validation observer, 404 the other roles at ZZ9).
- Screens (Ariva.Web): `operations.ts` gained `overflowBands`, `zoneHealth` (the server's zone name rule checked before sending, the zone as a query value) and a page size for `openAlerts`; `OverflowMarker` (Overflow in use, the bands with their people and minute as text and in `title`), `DataQualityPanel` with `quality.ts` (F18's Proposed targets: residual 0, completion at least 90 percent on final bins only, a provisional bin's share so far in grey, any minute outside the capacity amber; a word and a colour each), `HealthAlarms` (devices). Live screen: the marker under each queue's status, the selected zone's bins of the last 2 hours under the wait chart (only with `DataQuality.View`; the handler's screen asks nothing). Immigration: the marker beside a lane's wait, the latest bin of each lane queue of the hall shown (the terminal duty manager reads it: zone-level counts; the desk grid stays border data). Devices: the open alerts of the three health-check metrics grouped by zone, with rule, metric, value in its unit, bin, raised time, severity and state, under the alert screens' role rule. English and Arabic strings; RTL checked.
- Proposed points for the owner: docs/product/decisions.md, ARV-120 entry, points (1) to (9), and an Open decisions row (the read and its permission, handlers included; the 6-minute window and NotHeard; only an occupied band marked, not on the plan; the panel's targets; what each panel shows and the per-zone reads; the immigration panel for terminal duty managers; the devices alarms from one page of 500 open alerts; the refresh periods).
- Tests: unit `Ariva.UnitTests/Live/OverflowBandStatesTests` (30: band and zone states, out-of-range readings not believed, the window's edges, grouping and order, the first reading of a repeated band, rows without a zone or band, names with markup kept as data, the permission's holders). E2E API `tests/api/overflow-bands.spec.ts` (3: planted band minutes for D-VIS, SEC-N, CI-B and CI-C read back per band against the published profile; a neighbour key `DMO-X/SEC-N`, a band name the profile does not have and a 20-minute-old reading never counted; the supervisor, duty manager, handler and administrator read the same zones, the observer 403, anonymous and a malformed token 401, another site's supervisor, an unreachable site, ZZ9, `dmo` and `DMO-X` 404 without the data; SQL, markup, traversal, NUL and overlong path payloads 400 or 404 without an echo; query values ignored; other methods refused). Functional `tests/functional/overflow-quality.spec.ts` (7: the border supervisor's live marker and panels with each judgement and tone, a zone without bins, Arabic; immigration's marker and per-lane panel; the duty manager's panels without a desk code and only its own alarm; the handler without the panel, asking nothing, refused 403 by the server; the devices alarms with a rule and a zone named with markup shown as written; the administrator's alarms of both roles; route-fulfilled answers naming a queue zone and a band with markup on the live and immigration screens; axe WCAG 2.2 AA with the panels filled; no dialog, console error or CSP violation). `tests/visual/screens.spec.ts` plants one final health bin per DMO queue zone and an occupied A-OV minute (removed afterwards), and its two remaining status checks show a body only when the status is wrong.
- Visual baselines changed (8, through `node scripts/verify.mjs visual --update` in the pinned image on fresh services, then compared again on fresh services): live-operations and immigration, en and ar, light and dark (`tests/visual/__screenshots__/screens.spec.ts/`). The other 10 are unchanged.

Story gate `node scripts/verify.mjs story --specs tests/api/overflow-bands.spec.ts,tests/functional/overflow-quality.spec.ts` (17:58 to 18:34 UTC; wide through `Ariva.Di` and the web's `src/lib/core`, so the full suites): PASS, exit 0; build; reference golden up to date; unit 4,708 of 4,708; scanner self-test (42 rules) and scan 1,248 files, 0 errors, the 6 pending allowlist warnings; base images, NuGet and npm audits, chart security; docs; web; integration 479 passed and 1 skipped of 480 (the existing skip); E2E 654 passed and 1 skipped (the existing accessibility skip), none flaky, the story's 10 among them. The security review is the lead's step.
- Gotchas: a `<td>` given `display: flex` stops being a table cell, so wrap its content; two badges side by side in a narrow table cell widen the column and wrap the other headers, so the marker stacks under the status. A table of nowrap badges in the live screen's narrow column would scroll sideways, which axe reports as a scrollable region without focusable content, so the panel is a list that wraps. A manual resolution in `alert` needs `resolution_note` (a table check). A Playwright retry starts a new worker with a new run id, so the suite retires earlier rules by a fixed mark in their names. `pgrep -f` or `pkill -f` with the gate script's name matches the shell that runs the command and ends it (exit 144); take the process group from `ps` instead.
- Round 2 (security review FAIL on one required fix, CWE-120, and four recommendations, 2026-10-11): (1) the overflow bands read cut its answer at 500 bands and flagged it `Truncated`, against the project's rule that a read is refused beyond its bound, never cut short, and nothing tested the bound. Now the pure `OverflowBandStates.Within` returns the reader's rows whole or null beyond `ISvcOverflowBands.MaxBands` (the statement still asks for 501), the service answers `OverflowBandErrors.TooMany` (a fixed text that echoes nothing), and the controller maps it to 409 Conflict (declared with `ProducesResponseType`), anything else to 404 without a detail; `Truncated` is gone from `OverflowBandsViewModel`, `operations.ts`, the screens, the specs and wiki 12. When the read is refused the live and immigration screens mark no queue and show a neutral note (`liveOperations.overflow.refused`, English and Arabic). Tests: unit `OverflowBandStatesTests.Within_*` (0, 1, 499 and 500 bands kept whole and built, 501, 502 and 10,000 refused, a bound falling inside a zone never builds that zone from part of its bands, a null list throws) and the in-process `OverflowBandsEndpointTests` (409 with the fixed text and no zones, 404 without a detail for any other error, an answer without a truncated field); functional `overflow-quality.spec.ts` "a refused overflow read marks no queue and says so on the live and immigration screens" (the answer rewritten to 409: the note, no marker, the 409 the only console error). A profile holds at most 500 zones, so no E2E site can reach the bound; the API spec says so. (2) Recommendations taken: the CWE-120, CWE-89 and CWE-862 rows of docs/security/cwe-controls.md name the read, its bound and its tests; ASVS V8.4.1 (Partly, unchanged) and ARV-095's notes in backlog/prd-asvs-gaps.json record that the overflow bands read is a second path, after the live hub, that gives a handler station manager every queue zone of its sites, border lane queues included, and nothing new, so handler isolation must cover both; wiki 12 describes the refusal; decisions point (1) records it. (3) Found in this round's visual compare: the data-quality panels' bin times change width with their digits and move the text after them, so a baseline made in one hour failed in another (16,027 pixels, the English immigration screens); the visual spec now sets them to one fixed text, as it does for the zones table's publication times (wiki 16), and the 8 baselines were regenerated. Passes stays false pending the re-check.

## 2026-10-11 ARV-122 split into ARV-122a and ARV-122b (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1)
- Why: the engineer's survey before any code found the story too big for one session (two endpoints, the bundle builder and checker, seven importers reusing the existing checks, the floor plan sanitiser again, the site lock, a new permission, about 11 E2E cases, concurrency tests) and three points no rule settled. ARV-122a carries the bundle format, export, the manifest check, the permission, and the import of the topology, desk code mappings, the draft profile and floor plans; ARV-122b adds devices, alert rules and displays. ARV-137 now depends on ARV-122b.
- Owner decisions (2026-10-11): (1) the geometry hash F22 v1 names levels and desks by their database ids, so no import can reproduce it; v1 stays unchanged (campaigns, frozen results, devices and evidence packs quote it) and a portable geometry hash, computed on request and never stored, names them by their codes; the round trip compares the portable hash. (2) Imported alert rules and displays are checked against the imported draft profile's zones with the same rules; they act only once it is published. (3) An alert rule's escalation contact (free text that may name a person) is never exported; the import answer lists the rules that need one.
- Lead's calls (Proposed, for decisions.md): a display code already taken in the target database answers 409; the bundle names its airport by IATA code, which must already exist on the target and is never created; device calibrations are never exported.

## 2026-10-11 ARV-122a Site configuration export and import: bundle, topology, mappings, zone profile and floor plans (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1)
- Built on 67f4198 plus the lead's split edits; passes true after the round 2 security review.
- What changed: `GET` and `POST api/v1/admin/sites/{siteCode}/configuration` (`SiteConfigurationController` in AdminArea/Sites: `[Permission(TransferSiteConfiguration)]`, `[RequiresRecentMfa]`, `[SiteScoped]`, `[RequestSizeLimit(20 MB)]` on the import, the `upload` concurrency policy, `no-store`; import answers 201). New permission `SiteConfiguration.Transfer` (a new `PermissionAction.Transfer`), held by system administrators only through the every-permission rule (grants are role codes, so no seed or script), in `criticalPermissions` with both routes in `security/critical-actions.json` and two rows in `security/permission-matrix.json`; the web role screen lists no permissions, so no i18n change. Core: the bundle format and its checks (`SiteConfigurationBundles`: manifest, canonical bundle hash over the envelope and the manifest, `Open` refusing missing, extra and unknown parts, entries without parts, gaps in the file parts, other versions and mismatched hashes, base64 length checked before decoding), strict typed parts (`SiteConfigurationParts`, depth 32, no unknown or duplicate member), request rules (`SiteConfigurationRules`, Fx.Specification), the pure export and import plan (`SiteConfigurationTransfer`: codes and names in, new entities with new ids out, rebased to the target site), and the portable geometry hash (`ZoneProfile.ComputePortableGeometryHash`, owner decision O1; F22 v1 untouched). Infra: `SvcSiteConfiguration` (repeatable-read export; import with the site row locked, emptiness over every kind, the ARV-018 checks on every file, one transaction, refusals and failures audited in their own transaction). `SvcFloorPlans.SafeFileName` became internal for reuse. No script.
- Decisions: owner O1 to O3, the lead's L1 to L3 and points (1) to (12) Proposed in docs/product/decisions.md (ARV-122a entry and open-decisions row); formulas F22 gains the portable hash with its reference values (recomputed in Python by a script that also reproduces v1's); cwe-controls rows CWE-501, 120, 863, 306, 79, 862 and 89; ASVS V1.5.2, V2.2.1, V2.3.4, V5.2.1, V5.2.2, V8.2.1, V8.2.2, V10.3.4 and V11.4.3 (evidence only, statuses unchanged); data-boundary row for bundles; wiki 11 section 2d and a note in wiki 04 section 10; security/README.md.
- Gotchas: (1) the route follows the AdminArea prefix (`api/v1/admin/sites/...`), not the brief's `api/v1/sites/...`. (2) The export is the first critical GET: `step-up.spec.ts` sent `data: {}` on every route, and fetch refuses a body on a GET, so it now sends none on GETs. (3) A site has no airport of its own and terminal codes are unique per airport, so a copy within one database needs `?airportCode=` (the suites use a second airport). (4) `Enum.TryParse` reads `Immigration, CheckIn` as flags; bundle kinds are read by exact name. (5) `floor_plan.original_file_name` is varchar(200) in code points while the upload cuts by text elements; bundle file names are bounded at 200 code points. (6) A plan mutates the airport it is given (AddTerminal adds to its collection), harmless in the service (a refusal never flushes) but tests need a fresh airport per plan. (7) A scope disposed without an explicit end commits what was promised, so the import's write phase promises not to commit on any unexpected exception before rethrowing. (8) The audit table's columns are `before_summary` and `after_summary`. (9) API JSON does not escape `<`: names are data in `application/json` with `nosniff`. (10) Docker was down; started detached as the earlier gate scripts do. (11) E2E sites are new each run (`CF?` plus a random tail); sites and published versions stay by design, everything else is removed in `afterAll`.
- Gates (2026-10-11, on 15ac60a with the ARV-122a changes uncommitted; fresh services; detached): `node scripts/verify.mjs backend` PASS, exit 0 (build 0 errors, the existing test-project warnings only; unit 4,842 of 4,842; scan 1,263 files, 0 errors, the 6 pending warnings; scanner self-test, base images, chart security, NuGet and npm audits). Before the gates: the new spec, step-up and the permission matrix 241 of 242 on fresh services, the one failure an over-strict assertion of mine (API JSON does not escape `<`), then the new spec 7 of 7. `node scripts/security/scan.mjs --changed` and `node scripts/verify.mjs docs` as in the hand-off report.

## 2026-10-11 ARV-122b Site configuration export and import: devices, alert rules and displays (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1)
- What changed: the bundle (format version 1) gains three required parts, `devices`, `alertRules` and `displays`, always written (an empty list when there is nothing); a bundle without them is refused like any missing part, so a bundle exported before ARV-122b must be exported again (the part bound is now 207). Export writes devices (not retired) without any credential, calibration, state or access setting (a vendor footprint travels, an assumed one is computed again), alert rules with their codes and a flag for a withheld escalation contact (never the contact), and displays without pairing state; it refuses (409) a device on a removed level and a device, rule or display naming a zone the published profile does not offer it. Import checks the new rows with the request limits (`SiteConfigurationRules`), builds rules and displays through their entities and checks their zones against the imported draft (queue zones and overflow bands for rules, queue zones only for health checks and displays), applies the rule service's role check, checks the floor plan files, refuses a display code a live display holds (409), then, after the records and files are written and flushed, registers each device through `ISvcDevices.RegisterAsync` in the same transaction: Commissioning, a new credential answered once in `deviceCredentials`; `rulesNeedingEscalationContact` lists rule codes only. Counts and the audit gain devices, alertRules and displays (counts only).
- Tests: unit (transfer: export without credentials, contacts or access settings over every part, references outside the profile, devices on removed levels, bounds 2,000/500/200 at and beyond, remapping to the new level, kept codes and the contact list, unpaired displays, the draft-zone check, entity refusals as fixed texts; rules: 47 new refusal cases, bounds, canonical rule codes; parts: 15 never-exported members refused, non-list parts); integration (round trip with devices, rules and a display: new credential matches, the source's does not, no access settings or calibrations; a taken display code 409 with nothing written; rules and displays on zones the draft lacks refused; a device the registration path refuses after records, files and a device were written leaves nothing; the commit-rollback test now with a device, exactly one ImportFailed); E2E `site-configuration.spec.ts` (8 tests: every decoded part free of `ard[kp]_`, credential fragments and the contact; nine more tampered bundles; the 409 for a taken display code; the round trip with the new credential's format asserted inside an expect, authenticating at Ingest as the copy, the source's still the source's; rules, displays and hostile names).
- Gotchas: display codes are unique deployment-wide, so a copy within one deployment needs the source's displays deleted first (the E2E and integration tests do that); the display code check sits after the file checks but before the registration path's own device rules, which run only as each device is registered after the writes, so a bundle with a taken display code and a device the registration refuses answers 409 (the exact order is in decisions point (8) and wiki 11 section 2d); rule codes cannot collide with a deleted rule's because an import needs a site without any zone profile and a rule needs a published one; the registration path writes its own `Device.Registered` audit entry (prefix, model, zone) per device, rolled back with the import.

## 2026-10-12 ARV-123 split into ARV-123a and ARV-123b (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1)
- Why: the engineer's survey before any code found the backend alone about ARV-122a's size (two CSV kinds with streamed readers, limits and per-row errors, a preview token, a commit that re-checks everything, four endpoints, the matrix and critical-action rows, unit, integration and E2E tests), and the criterion that credentials are shown once on the devices screen adds two import dialogs with i18n and functional tests. ARV-123a is the API; ARV-123b the screens. ARV-137 now depends on ARV-123b.
- Lead's calls (Proposed where the owner decides): CsvHelper 33.1.0 (pinned since ARV-041, unreferenced until now, no known vulnerabilities, MS-PL or Apache-2.0) referenced from Ariva.Infra, its low-level `CsvParser` only, no record mapping or dynamic types; the preview token lives 15 minutes; neither preview needs step-up (it writes only its audit entry); the mapping commit needs no step-up, as the single mapping endpoint (Proposed: the owner may make bulk mapping a critical action); a file at most 1 MB, a cell at most 200 characters, at most 500 device rows and 10,000 mapping rows per file, the whole file in one transaction.

## 2026-10-12 ARV-123a Bulk import of devices and desk code mappings from CSV: API (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1)
- What changed: two CSV imports, each a preview then a commit, in Ariva.Api.Main: `POST api/v1/admin/sites/{siteCode}/device-imports/preview` and `/commit` (`CreateDevice`, `[RequiresRecentMfa]` on both, `critical-actions.json`) and `.../desk-code-mapping-imports/preview` and `/commit` (`CreateDeskCodeMapping`, no second factor), all `[SiteScoped]` with the services' own site check, the `upload` policy, multipart field `file` (and `previewToken`). CsvHelper 33.1.0 referenced from Ariva.Infra (the lead's approval; central pin unchanged), its low-level `CsvParser` only (`CsvImportReader`: RFC 4180, UTF-8 with an optional byte order mark, `MaxFieldSize` 800, `ExceptionMessagesContainRawData` false, bad quoting a row refusal). Cells: 200 characters, no control character, no formula prefix in text, numbers as digits with an optional leading minus within the request's ranges. A preview is a dry run of `ISvcDevices.RegisterAsync` or `ISvcDeskCodeMappings.CreateAsync` (unchanged) under the site lock, rolled back; a file without a refused row gets a Data Protection token (a purpose per kind) binding the file's SHA-256, the site and the user for 15 minutes. A commit hashes the file, checks the token before parsing, checks every row again and applies the whole file in one transaction; devices arrive in Commissioning with each credential answered once (`no-store`). Audited (`DeviceImport.*`, `DeskCodeMappingImport.*`) with the file hash, counts and outcome only. Measured: 500 devices committed in about 4 s, 10,000 mappings in about 14 s.
- Tests: unit `CsvImportReaderTests` (quoting, CRLF, LF, BOM, UTF-16 and invalid UTF-8, headers, bounds at the bound and one beyond for rows, cells, the parser's field bound and the 1 MB file, 34 formula and number cases, 150 refusals listed as 100 with no cell echoed) and `ImportRowsTests` (codes, kinds, repeats, the token's binding to file, site, user, kind and 15 minutes, the refusal texts); integration `DeviceImportTests` and `DeskCodeMappingImportTests` (preview then commit, per-row refusals by the services' rules, a commit refused at a later row keeps nothing, a commit rolled back after its writes keeps nothing and is audited as failed once, the token refused for another user, site, file, kind or after 15 minutes, out-of-reach sites, the bounds measured); E2E `csv-imports.spec.ts` (permissions, step-up, cross-site, malformed and oversized files, formula-prefixed cells by row and column, token replays 409, credentials once by format, mappings) and `step-up.spec.ts` (sends a form to the multipart routes, marked `"body": "multipart"` in `critical-actions.json`).
- Gotchas: `CreateDevice` is a critical permission, and `CriticalActionsTests` requires every endpoint it guards to carry `[RequiresRecentMfa]`, so the device preview needs a second factor too, unlike the proposal (recorded as decisions point 2). An endpoint that takes a file answers any other content type with 415 at routing, before authorization, so the step-up spec sends such routes an empty form. Refusals come from the reader, the row checks and the dry run at different times: the answer lists them sorted by row and column.

## 2026-10-12 ARV-123b Bulk import of devices and desk code mappings from CSV: screens (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1)
- What changed: Ariva.Web on the ARV-123a API. The Devices screen has Import devices beside Register a device (holders of `Device.Create` only, as Register), the Topology screen's desk code panel has Import mappings (holders of `DeskCodeMapping.Create` only; the panel now takes the desk's site). Both open one native modal dialog like the step-up dialog (`src/lib/components/pages/imports/CsvImportDialog.svelte`, with `ImportRefusals`, `ImportPreviewRows` and `ImportedCredentials`), calling the API only through `src/lib/core/csvImports.ts` and Api.ts (site and token shapes checked, the same File object sent by the preview and the commit; the file checked in the browser: a `.csv` name with a CSV-like or empty type, not empty, at most 1 MB). The device preview's `401 mfa_required` goes through Api.ts's existing step-up, unchanged: the layout's StepUpDialog opens over the import dialog and the same form is sent again. The dialog shows the counts and the file's SHA-256, each refusal by row, column (number and spreadsheet letter, or Whole row) and reason (the server's fixed texts translated by exact match, anything else as written, never as a message key), "The first 100 of N", the rows that pass (at most 500 listed), and enables the commit only with a token and nothing refused. Device credentials are held in the dialog's own state only (`$state.raw`), listed with Copy and Copy all as CSV under a once-only warning; closing needs "I have copied every credential" (Escape and the close button show a reminder, the browser's enforced close on a repeated Escape reopens the dialog, no close while the commit is on its way), and closing, `pagehide` and unmount drop them; the devices list refreshes. A translated button opens the hidden file input (the native control speaks the browser's language). Every string in English and Arabic (`csvImport`), logical properties, codes and credentials as LTR islands. Docs: wiki 11 section 2e (and the section 6 row), wiki 07 section 2, wiki 12 Devices and Topology, cwe-controls CWE-79 and CWE-287 (CWE-532) rows, decisions.md ARV-123b points (1) to (10), Proposed. E2E: a new account `e2e.webcsvimport` (all sites, TOTP) in `tests/support/accounts.ts`, since a TOTP code counts once per account and every other TOTP account belongs to another suite.
- Tests: `tests/functional/csv-import-screens.spec.ts`, 8 tests, serial, on a site of its own per run (`CIW` and a random tail, with a published profile holding a second queue zone named with markup; devices, mappings, topology and airport removed at the end, the site and profile kept, as csv-imports.spec.ts): the device import with a real step-up (the sign-in's token aged 20 minutes), preview, commit, each credential's format inside an expect, Copy all checked against the clipboard as a boolean, Escape twice and the close button refused until confirmed, then no credential on the page, in web storage, the address or the console, the preview token nowhere, the list refreshed and the registry holding no credential; 121 refusals (first 100 listed by row and column, no cell echoed, commit disabled), a header refused as a whole, axe on the dialog; a model and a zone named with markup shown as text (no element, no dialog, no CSP violation); oversized, empty and non-CSV files refused with no request sent, exactly 1 MB taken; the mapping import (a refused row, then three mappings committed without a second factor, the panel reloaded); Arabic (translated title, file button, refusal and summary, the close and commit buttons mirrored, axe); the import hidden for a terminal duty manager's desk code panel and on an `/api/auth/me` answer without `Device.Create` (no role reads the devices screen without it), with the server's 403 for both previews. Traces are off for the file: the dialog shows live credentials.
- Gotchas: Playwright refuses `test.use({ trace })` inside a describe (it forces a new worker), so the whole file is trace-off. A full page load takes a fresh token from the refresh cookie (dropping the aged one) and the browser's language, so the step-up and Arabic tests stay on their page. A TOTP sign-in answers 401 before the code, which Chromium logs (`allowStatuses`). Axe's scrollable-region-focusable flags a scrolling table that takes no focus: the dialog's three tables scroll in a focusable named region, as `ResultsTables`. svelte-sonner toasts show above a modal's backdrop, but the copy feedback stays in the dialog anyway. The svelte MCP is unreachable over HTTP from the session (proxy 403); its stdio package `@sveltejs/mcp` 0.1.26 ran `svelte-autofixer` locally (scratchpad, with the proxy for its fetches): no issues on any changed component, suggestions only (a function called in an effect, `bind:this` where an attachment could do).
- Gates (2026-10-12, 05:38 to 06:39 UTC, on 2ec41d9 with the ARV-123b changes uncommitted; detached; a checkpoint's mutation run going on beside them in its own worktree): `node scripts/verify.mjs web` PASS, exit 0 (svelte-check 0 errors and 0 warnings, lint, build). ## 2026-10-12 ARV-153 Floor plan file names bounded in code points before storage (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1)
- What changed: the floor plan upload refuses a file name longer than 200 code points after trimming, with 400 and a fixed text (`TopologyErrors.FileNameTooLong`), before anything is read or stored, instead of cutting it at 200 text elements (which let one letter with 300 combining marks through to fail the varchar(200) insert at the commit, a 500). One rule for the upload, the entity and the site configuration import: `FloorPlan.FileNameFits` (code points as PostgreSQL counts them, the UTF-16 length checked first), used by `SvcFloorPlans.CheckFileName`, by `FloorPlan`'s constructor (the last line before the column) and by `SiteConfigurationRules.IsFileName`. The existing checks (paths, control and format characters, right-to-left overrides, lone surrogates, a blank name stored as "floor-plan") run first, unchanged. No schema change.
- Tests: unit `FloorPlanFileNameTests` (200 and 201 code points in letters, combining marks and emoji, 200 text elements of 201 code points, right-to-left overrides, lone surrogates built outside the theory data, which serialisation would turn into replacement characters, the entity, the import's bound); integration `FloorPlanTests` (a name of 200 code points stored, 305 refused with nothing stored); E2E `floor-plans.spec.ts` (one letter and 300 combining marks: 201 or 400, never 500, nothing stored on a refusal, the name not echoed).
- Gotchas: a long ordinary name, cut and stored before, is now refused (decisions point 5).

Then `node scripts/verify.mjs backend` PASS, exit 0 (unit 5,030 of 5,030; scan 1,287 files, 0 errors, the 6 pending warnings). Story gate `node scripts/verify.mjs story --specs tests/api/floor-plans.spec.ts --integration "FullyQualifiedName~FloorPlan|FullyQualifiedName~SiteConfiguration"` (13 changed files, wide: none): first run FAIL, exit 1, before any E2E test: port 5081 held by the mail emulator of the stopped run (unit 5,031 of 5,031 and integration 16 of 16 had passed); second run on fresh services, after stopping that leftover, PASS, exit 0: unit 5,031 of 5,031, integration 16 of 16, E2E 314 passed, log scan clean; it notes a checkpoint is due before the next story. `node scripts/security/scan.mjs --changed` PASS, exit 0 (8 files, 0 errors, 0 warnings) and `node scripts/verify.mjs docs` PASS, exit 0, after each run. Before the gates: the floor plan and site configuration specs 13 of 13 on fresh services. Passes stayed false until the security review.
- Round 2 (security review of 2026-10-12: PASS, with one recommendation folded in before the commit). The unsafe-name check walked the name by UTF-16 unit, and a surrogate half reports Surrogate, not Format, so invisible format characters outside the basic plane passed (tag characters U+E0001 and U+E0020 to U+E007F, U+1BCA0 to U+1BCA3, U+1D173 to U+1D17A); a pre-existing gap. `SvcFloorPlans.CheckFileName` now refuses a lone surrogate first, then any control or format character by `Rune` (the site configuration import's names go through the same check). The CWE-79 row says the format-character refusal now holds in every plane. Not changed here, reported for a follow-up: `DisplayText.IsClean` (Ariva.Core/Domain/Components/DisplayText.cs) skips every surrogate pair, so the same characters pass every display text it guards (alert rule names, zones and escalation contacts, display names, labels and messages, device models and queue zone names, integration client, outbound endpoint and report schedule names, user display names, site calendar reasons, validation texts, alert notes, canonical event and replay zone names, the email sender name); the CSV import reader (ARV-123a) refuses only control characters itself and leaves format characters to those services; `ZoneHealthCriteria` checks controls only (a query filter, nothing stored).

Passes stays false, pending the re-check.
- Security review (security-reviewer; CWE-120, CWE-79, CWE-22): PASS (2026-10-12), then PASS on round 2, which folded in its recommendation (control and format characters judged per code point, so the tag characters U+E0001 and U+E0020 to U+E007F, U+1BCA0 to U+1BCA3 and U+1D173 to U+1D17A are refused in floor plan names, the site configuration import included). The count is PostgreSQL's char_length (no normalisation on the way in); no path reaches the insert with a long name. `scan.mjs --changed` 9 files, 0 errors; no raw invisible characters in the changed files. ## 2026-10-12 ARV-158 Display texts judged per code point (DisplayText.IsClean) (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1)
- What changed: `DisplayText.IsClean` (Ariva.Core/Domain/Components/DisplayText.cs) walks the text once with `Rune.DecodeFromUtf16`: a lone surrogate is refused first (a high surrogate at the end included), then any control or format character per code point (`Rune.IsControl`, category Format), so the tag characters (U+E0001, U+E0020 to U+E007F), the shorthand format controls (U+1BCA0 to U+1BCA3) and musical formatting (U+1D173 to U+1D17A) are refused behind every `Require` and `Optional` like a right-to-left override; `allowLineBreaks` unchanged; in the basic plane a rune and a char are judged alike, so every basic plane text keeps its result. No schema change; the CHECK classes of scripts 0020 and 0022 stay basic plane only (immutable; parity left to a new script, ARV-157 or later).
- Tests: unit `DisplayTextTests` (U+E0041 inside a text, U+E0001, U+1D173 and U+1BCA0 refused; an emoji, Arabic and plain text accepted; the basic plane cases as before; lone surrogates built in a Fact; line breaks with and without `allowLineBreaks`; U+E0041 refused through the alert rule, display, device (model and queue zone name), user display name (`SvcUsers.IsUsableDisplayName`), integration client (constructor and `Rename`) and report schedule entry points). Against the previous `IsClean` the 11 cases with a character outside the basic plane fail and the 10 others pass. Integration `DeviceImportTests.Preview_Should_RefuseTheRowThroughTheRegistration_When_AModelCellHoldsATagCharacter` (site XCII: the reader passes the cell, the registration refuses the row: row 2, no column, `RegistrationRefused`, no token, nothing kept). Every invisible character is an escape; the changed files hold no raw Cf, Mn or Cs character.
- Per-character checks outside `DisplayText` (grep for `char.GetUnicodeCategory`, `CharUnicodeInfo.GetUnicodeCategory`, `char.IsControl`, `char.IsSurrogate`), none changed: Ariva.Infra/Border/AmanPull.cs:185 and Ariva.Infra/Services/Flights/SvcAcrisPull.cs:68 and 69 (control characters stripped from `outbound_endpoint.last_status` and `last_modified`, texts Ariva builds; every control character is in the basic plane, so a check per UTF-16 unit is complete); Ariva.Infra/Notifications/Email.cs:168 and 169 (`Email.Clean` drops every surrogate, so nothing outside the basic plane passes); Ariva.Infra/Imports/CsvImportReader.cs:224 and 236 (control characters only; format characters left to the services, now proved by the integration test); Ariva.Core/Domain/Criteria/ZoneHealthCriteria.cs:37 (a query filter, nothing stored); Platform/Simulation/Ariva.Simulation.Api/Emulators/Validation/ValidationEmulatorSettings.cs:61 and 63 (the emulator's observer settings); already per code point: Ariva.Infra/Services/Topology/SvcFloorPlans.cs:196 (ARV-153) and Ariva.Core/Security/UserNames.cs:25 to 29.
- Gotchas: the subdivision flag emoji (England, Scotland, Wales) are tag sequences and are now refused in every display text (decisions point 2). ## 2026-10-12 ARV-161 Geometry.SegmentsIntersect independent of point order (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1)
- What changed: `Geometry.SegmentsIntersect` (Ariva.Core/Domain/Components/Geometry.cs) puts each segment's ends in a fixed order (by x, then y; negative zero made zero) and then the two segments before any arithmetic, so all eight orders and directions of its arguments compute with the same numbers and give one answer, rounding included. A touch is an end closer than `Tolerance` (1 cm) to the other segment (`DistanceToSegment`, metres against metres); otherwise only a proper crossing counts, with each side 0 when the cross product is below Tolerance times the segment's length (the point's distance to the line, never a cross product against a length). `OnSegment` and the exact-zero test are gone; the 1e-12 `Cross` stays for the neighbour fold-back check of `IsSimplePolygon`, already order-independent. The touch answer is true in every order: a zone vertex on, or within 1 cm of, an edge that is not its own is refused; a footprint touching its zone, or within 1 cm, reaches it (decisions, 2026-10-12 entry, Proposed).
- Tests: unit `GeometryTests` (each segment case in all eight orders: the seed's points, a shared end at the origin 3.5e-14 m apart; 9 mm and 11 mm from the other segment and collinear; ends on the other segment in decimals binary cannot hold, also a kilometre out; an end 9 mm from the other's line beyond its end with the segments crossing; segments of no length; `IsSimplePolygon` and `Overlaps` rows in every rotation and direction of each ring), `FormulaPropertyTests` (the order property at `Math.Max(10_000, Iter)` cases, so 10,000 in every build, checked with a counting probe, over all eight orders; seed `cCcbgdLcNjA6` as a fixed case beside it, which runs exactly that input; an end placed on the other segment is a touch; a segment moved sideways touches within 1 cm and not beyond at any length; on a 10 cm grid the answer equals exact integer arithmetic; `IsSimplePolygon` and `Overlaps` the same in every rotation and direction of grid rings). Against the previous `Geometry.cs`, 9 of the new tests fail (the seed case and its row among them); with the fix all pass, and five runs at `ARIVA_FUZZ_ITERATIONS=20000` (100,000 cases per property) pass.
- Docs: formulas F4 (the track-crossing code uses F4's own side-of-line rule, not this predicate; the predicate described with its tolerance and canonical order), F22 (touching within 1 cm in the simple polygon rule), the property tests section; wiki 07 (polygon touches refused, footprint reach includes a touch); decisions 2026-10-12 entry and open decisions row.
- Gotchas: publication and `CreateDraft` do not re-check simplicity, so a stored zone with a vertex 1 to 9 mm beside another edge stays publishable until moved, and its export gives a bundle the import refuses (decisions point 7). Geometry.cs is not in Stryker's mutate list.
- Gates (2026-10-12, 15:17 to 15:32 UTC, on b4fb51f with the ARV-161 changes uncommitted; fresh services; detached; nothing else running): `node scripts/verify.mjs backend` PASS, exit 0 (unit 5,150 of 5,150; reference scenario golden up to date, unchanged; scan 1,295 files, 0 errors, the 6 pending warnings). Story gate `node scripts/verify.mjs story --specs tests/api/zone-profiles.spec.ts,tests/api/devices.spec.ts --integration "FullyQualifiedName~ZoneProfile|FullyQualifiedName~Device"` (7 changed files, wide: none) PASS, exit 0: unit 5,150 of 5,150, E2E 7 spec files with the smoke set, 316 passed, log scan clean (5 host logs, no credentials); it skipped the integration step ("no persistence change": it runs the filter only when Ariva.Infra or Ariva.IntegrationTests change), so the filter was run directly with the site configuration import and the seeds added (`dotnet test` Ariva.IntegrationTests `--filter "FullyQualifiedName~ZoneProfile|FullyQualifiedName~Device|FullyQualifiedName~SiteConfiguration|FullyQualifiedName~Seed"`): 56 of 56 passed, exit 0. `node scripts/security/scan.mjs --changed` PASS, exit 0 (3 files, 0 errors, 0 warnings) and `node scripts/verify.mjs docs` PASS, exit 0 (rerun after this bullet). The story gate notes 3 stories since the last checkpoint.
- Passes true after the security review.
- Security review (security-reviewer; correctness, CWE-20 geometry input, CWE-400 cost): PASS (2026-10-12). Order independence holds by construction (canonical ends and segments, NaN a deterministic false and non-finite input refused upstream); the 1 cm touch loses no real crossing and gives no false touch within the site's coordinate range (rounding about 1e-13 m); IsSimplePolygon lets no self-intersecting ring pass; cost bounded by 200 vertices per zone and 32 per footprint. `scan.mjs --changed` 4 files, 0 errors. Follow-ups: publication-time simplicity and a possible minimum-width rule against slivers (a 10 m by 0.1 mm triangle passes the 1e-4 m2 area minimum) are in ARV-162.
