## 2026-10-12 ARV-156 Tests robust under load and on a busy broker (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1)
- Taken over after a container restart ended the first attempt before it reported; its uncommitted work was reviewed part by part. Kept: the start gate in `ArivaHosts.cs` (diagnosis verified against the sources; the build moved inside the `try`, so a failed build also opens the gate), `IsNamespaceName` and its table of names, the evidence files. Changed: the namespace rule (the first attempt's non-backtracking pattern plus an `EndsWith('-')` check became the original RFC 1123 pattern on `RegexOptions.NonBacktracking` without a timeout) and its test. Dropped: `GeneratedRegexMatchers.cs`, which read the generated matcher's IL for calls to `RegexRunner.CheckTimeout` (it depends on how the generator emits its code and counts 0, a pass, should a later generator check the clock another way); the env-gated phase recorder in `BrokerOutageTests.cs`; `Arv156TemporaryReproTests.cs` (an env-gated repro, used for the load runs below and then removed).
- Cause 1, `ValidationReaderLoginTests` (Cronz), "Cannot access a disposed object. Object name: 'IServiceProvider'": a race in `WebApplicationFactory` with a host whose start fails, checked against the .NET 10 sources (aspnetcore v10.0.0 `DeferredHostBuilder.cs` and `WebApplicationFactory.cs`; runtime v10.0.0 `HostFactoryResolver.cs`, `Host.cs`, `HostingAbstractionsHostExtensions.cs`). The entry point runs on a thread of its own; `HostFactoryResolver` hands the built host to the factory at `HostBuilt` (`stopApplication: false`) and the entry point goes straight on into `app.RunAsync()`, whose `finally` disposes the host when `StartAsync` throws (here `ValidationReaderGuard`). The factory's thread meanwhile calls `DeferredHost.StartAsync`, which first resolves `IHostApplicationLifetime` from `_host.Services` and only then waits for the start's outcome. When the entry point's thread gets there first, the resolve meets the disposed provider: `ObjectDisposedException`, a subclass of `InvalidOperationException`, so only the test's message check failed. Any test whose host fails in its start rather than its build (a hosted service or a start-up validator refusing the configuration) had the same exposure.
- Fix 1 (test plumbing only, `Ariva.UnitTests/Setup/ArivaHosts.cs`): `GatedHostLifetime` wraps the host's `IHostLifetime` and holds `WaitForStartAsync` (the first step of `Host.StartAsync`, before the start-up validators and the hosted services) until a gate opens; `ArivaWebApplicationFactory.CreateHost` builds the host, calls the deferred host's `StartAsync` (which resolves the lifetime and subscribes before its first wait, so it returns at that wait), then opens the gate. A failed start is now always its own exception. No production host code changed. `HostStartGateTests` pins it deterministically: the start stays pending and no hosted service starts until the gate opens, and a booted host carries the gated lifetime.
- Proof 1 (16 `yes` CPU hogs on the 4 CPUs, started and stopped by PID): the first attempt's repro (four lanes of 500 refused starts each, Ingest, Stream, Cronz and Integration in k8s-dev with the reader login) before the fix, 20:17 to 20:21 UTC: 221 of 2,000 starts ended with `ObjectDisposedException` at `DeferredHost.StartAsync`, 1,779 with the guard's exception; after the fix, 20:47 to 20:51 UTC, with the whole integration suite also running: 2,000 of 2,000 the guard's exception. The two classes alone 50 times in a row before the fix (20:22 to 20:46 UTC): 50 passed (194 tests each), so the loop alone does not reproduce the race (200 refused starts, one at a time); after the fix (20:51 to 21:14 UTC, the hogs and the integration suite beside it, load average 20 to 29): 50 passed (217 tests each).
- Cause 2, `ValidationEmulatorTests.MainUrl_Should_BeTheAddressOfTheSimulatorsOwnDeployment_When_Checked`, `RegexMatchTimeoutException`: the simulator's namespace rule `^[a-z0-9]([-a-z0-9]{0,61}[a-z0-9])?\z` had a 100 ms timeout on a backtracking matcher. A timeout is wall-clock time from the start of a match (`RegexRunner.InitializeTimeout`), compared only where the matcher calls `CheckTimeout`: the generated code does so where it backtracks (and the interpreter at every match), and this pattern backtracks on every name of two characters or more (the loop takes the last character, then gives it back). A thread descheduled or suspended for a garbage collection for 100 ms between the two fails a 9-character name: the input was not too long, the pattern backtracks. Measured under the 16 hogs plus 16 spinning threads and a large-object allocator, 30 s each against `"ariva-dev"`: the old matcher 14 timeouts in 1,733,996 matches (the slowest timed-out call 652 ms); the same pattern on `RegexOptions.NonBacktracking` without a timeout 0 in 1,443,620 (its slowest call 2,664 ms, which any timeout would have failed).
- Fix 2 (`Ariva.Simulation.Api/Emulators/FeedSettings.cs`): the same RFC 1123 pattern with `RegexOptions.NonBacktracking` and `Timeout.Infinite`. CWE-1333: the non-backtracking engine is linear in the input's length, which is the bound against ReDoS; without a timeout no match can throw `RegexMatchTimeoutException`. The rule and so `IsOwnDeploymentMain` (CWE-918) accept exactly the same names. Test: `NamespaceLabel_Should_BeLinearWithoutATimeout_When_Matched` (the options and the timeout, read from the rule itself) beside the table of names (`IsNamespaceName_Should_TakeAnRfc1123LabelOnly_When_Checked`, 21 cases, and the 63 and 64 character and 1,000,000 character cases). A test that the pattern has no nested quantifiers would not prove it: `^[a-z0-9-]{0,62}[a-z0-9]\z` has none and still backtracks.
- Proof 2: the loops of Proof 1 run `ValidationEmulatorTests` too (the MainUrl theory, 25 cases, 11 of them with an own namespace such as `ariva-dev`): 50 of 50 passed before the fix and 50 of 50 after, so the loops did not reproduce this one either; the matcher measurement above does (14 timeouts before, 0 after), and with no timeout on the rule there is nothing left to time out.
- Cause 3, `BrokerOutageTests.Outbox_Should_HoldEventsWhileTheBrokerIsDownAndRelayThemInOrder_When_ItIsBack`, timed out at 300 s. The cause found in round 1 is the test's own bound, not the broker, the outbox or a rebalance; the three timed out runs themselves were not reproduced (below). Revised in round 2: run 12's diagnosis (further down) gives a likelier cause, a cut during the consumer's offset commit. The test waited 90 s for readiness `Unhealthy` during the outage, but MassTransit reports the bus `Degraded` while its Kafka endpoint is faulted ("Degraded Endpoints: ariva-it.it-faults", every check from the cut to the restore in the host logs) and never `Unhealthy`, so that wait always ran its whole bound (90.2, 90.4, 90.4 and 90.4 s in the four instrumented runs) and then passed its `NotBe(Healthy)` assertion. With the direct publish's message timeout (30 to 38 s) about 125 s of the fact's 300 s were fixed cost; the rest had to cover the host's start (held by `KafkaTopicsReady` until the topics exist, up to 300 s by default, and not bounded by the test at all), the first three, the relay after the restore and the final check, whose own bounds (240, 60, 300 and 120 s) added up to 870 s, so a slow step could never fail with its name: the fact's timeout fired first, three times, with no trace of which step was slow. The other suspects, with evidence: no broker container restarts (the test disables the Toxiproxy proxy; the broker keeps running); the outbox relay holds every row while the bus is not healthy (`IOutboxTransport.IsReady`), so the rows written during the outage had 0 attempts and no backoff (one run had one attempt, a pass already producing when the cut came, with a 2 s backoff); after the restore the Kafka endpoint reconnects at MassTransit's next receive transport retry (exponential, 3 to 30 s, 1,000 tries, for every transport exception but a cancellation, each turned into a `KafkaConnectionException`: `InMemoryHostConfiguration`, `ReceiveTransport` and `TopicKafkaReceiveEndpointContext`, decompiled from MassTransit 8.5.11), rejoins its group and the relay sends at its next 200 ms poll: 20.6, 36.6, 27.2 and 27.9 s from the restore to all eight received.
- Measured before the fix (the first attempt's two runs and two of this session's): quiet, the class alone 158 s; the whole integration suite 178 s; 16 hogs, the class alone 179 s (start 11.1 s, first three 9.6 s, direct publish 37.7 s, the `Unhealthy` wait 90.4 s, relay 27.2 s); the whole integration suite beside the 16 hogs and the unit loop (load average about 30) 187 s (start 26.3, first three 8.5, publish 30.3, wait 90.4, relay 27.9 s). None of these reproduced the 300 s timeout: the three failing runs needed over 110 s more in the steps that vary with load (the start, the first three, the relay) than the slowest of them; they were story gates' integration runs with nothing else running, the test starting 17 to 38 s into the run while the other collections start their containers, and their logs hold no step times (no host log, no output). With the fix the fixed cost drops by 90 s and such a run would fail naming its slow step.
- Fix 3 (`Ariva.IntegrationTests/Faults/BrokerOutageTests.cs`, test only): the outage wait is for what is asserted (any status but `Healthy`); every wait polls its condition within one budget of 270 s shared by the whole test, inside the fact's 300 s, and a step that runs out fails with its name and the time each earlier step took; each step is also written to the test's output as it happens (kept with the result, also when the fact times out), and the host's start is a step of its own. The assertions are unchanged; the summary now says what the readiness checks report (`Degraded`).
- Proof 3 (21:15 to 21:39 UTC, 16 `yes` CPU hogs of its own on the 4 CPUs, load average 18 to 21; the class alone, `-showLiveOutput` to read the steps): 10 runs after the fix, 10 passed; the test took 65.7 to 90.1 s (179 s before the fix under the same hogs): start 6.7 to 9.2 s, ready 8.1 to 11.4 s, first three 8.8 to 10.0 s, direct publish 30.4 to 38.9 s, readiness reporting the outage at once (0.0 s instead of 90 s), restore to all eight received 12.3 to 32.6 s, healthy again 3.0 s later. The argument from the fix: the 90 s that every run lost is gone (about 110 s back with the shorter outage), every remaining wait ends as soon as its condition holds, and should a step still run out, the 270 s budget names it with the earlier steps' times before the fact's 300 s.
- Other regular expressions with a timeout that can throw under load the same way (an IL scan of all 65 `[GeneratedRegex]` rules in the production assemblies, counting each generated matcher's calls to `RegexRunner.CheckTimeout`, rerun on the fixed build; and a search for `new Regex(` and static `Regex` calls): 12 generated rules backtrack under a timeout, all in production assemblies: `Site.CodePattern` (Ariva.Core/Domain/Entities/Site.cs:51), `TopologyCodes.Code` (Topology.cs:62), `FlightRules.Carrier` and `Codeshare` (Flights/FlightData.cs:297, 312), `OutboundRules.PathAndQuery` and `Scope` (Integration/OutboundRules.cs:253, 259), `EmailAddresses.Rule` (Ariva.Infra/Notifications/Email.cs:110), `EmailTemplates.Placeholder` (Email.cs:179, unanchored, so its scan loop checks the clock at every position), `CsvImportReader.Number` (Imports/CsvImportReader.cs:245), 100 ms each; `JobsDashboardGuard.InlineScript`, `ConfigScript` and `StaticFile` (Ariva.Api.Cronz/Jobs/JobsDashboard.cs:313, 317, 320), 1 s each. Three interpreted calls check the clock at every match whatever the pattern: the email attachment's file name (Email.cs:199) and the Cronz dashboard settings `KeySha256` and `BasePath` (JobsDashboard.cs:30, 32), 1 s each. None was changed: the simulator's namespace rule was the only one in the simulator (its other rules have anchored, bounded patterns and no timeout), and the rest are request, feed, template and settings rules of Ariva.Core, Ariva.Infra and the Cronz host, where a timed out match would refuse a valid request or start (500 or a failed start) only on a loaded node; proposed as a follow-up story (the same change where the pattern allows it: `NonBacktracking` has no lookarounds, which `TopologyCodes.Code` uses). The test code holds 22 more regular expressions with 1 s timeouts (source scans in architecture, seed and persistence tests), also unchanged.

- Round 2 loop, run 12 (23:37 to 23:43 UTC) failed with the fact's 300 s timeout and no budget message. Its step log stops at "the direct publish refused at 55.6 s" (readiness never reported the outage). Its console log: the probe consumer logged one `Local_AllBrokersDown` at the cut and then nothing (the passing runs log five consumer errors and four "Retrying ... ReceiveTransport faulted"), no `masstransit-bus` Degraded line, no "checkpoint failed" line, and the outbox relay, which sends only while `bus.CheckHealth()` is Healthy, tried row 4 six times (producers 5 to 10, each failing after the 30 s message timeout). The cut came 0.0 s after the third record reached the consumer.
- Diagnosis of run 12 (a temporary diagnostic test against the faults fixture, removed afterwards; quiet machine, 23:46 to 23:55 UTC): (A) cutting the proxy from inside the consumer while it handles the third record, so before that record's checkpoint: the bus stayed Healthy for 46 s and turned Degraded only when MassTransit logged "checkpoint failed with offset: 3 ... Local: Waiting for coordinator" 45.1 s after the cut; until then `KafkaMessageConsumer`'s consume loop is held by the checkpoint (`HandleKafkaError` cancels it on `Local_AllBrokersDown`, but the endpoint faults only once the loop and the checkpoint end). (B) cutting 2 s after the third record (checkpoint done): Degraded 1 s after the cut. (C) as (A), then disposing the host 10 s after the cut with the broker still cut: the dispose took 35.2 s, ending only when the checkpoint failed. So the cause of run 12: the cut landed while the consumer's offset commit for the third record was in flight; MassTransit kept the endpoint Ready and the bus Healthy for as long as that commit stayed pending (45 s in (A); in run 12 it never returned within four minutes, perhaps because the commit request had already been sent); readiness never reported the outage, the budget's TimeoutException came at 270 s, and the host's dispose, still with the broker cut, waited for the same commit past the fact's 300 s, which hid the message. The same chain explains the three original 300 s timeouts better than the 90 s wait did: in the old test the `NotBe(Healthy)` assertion fails at about 130 s in this race and the dispose then hangs until 300 s, which is exactly "timed out after 300000 milliseconds" with no message. Not proven for those runs (they left no logs).
- Round 3 (the coordinator's go-ahead, 2026-10-13), test only: `BrokerOutageTests` cuts the broker only once the probe consumer's committed offset equals the topic's latest offset, both read past the proxy (`CommittedAllAsync`, a condition within the 270 s budget, never a sleep; the comment at it names ARV-165), so the cut cannot land in an offset commit; and the body runs in a `try` whose `finally` restores the proxy before the admin client and the host are disposed, so a failing step reports its own message instead of hanging the dispose. One quiet run: the first three committed at once (5.3 s), the session expired 45.0 s after the cut, all eight received 10.3 s after the restore, the test 63.6 s.
- Evidence for ARV-165 (production finding, not changed here; from the round 2 diagnostic test on the faults fixture, quiet machine, 2026-10-12 23:46 to 23:55 UTC): (1) the proxy cut from inside the consumer while it handled the third record, so before that record's offset commit: the bus stayed Healthy for 46 s and turned Degraded only when MassTransit logged "checkpoint failed with offset: 3 ... Local: Waiting for coordinator", 45.1 s after the cut; (2) the cut 2 s after the third record, its commit done: Degraded 1 s after the cut; (3) as (1), then the host disposed 10 s after the cut with the broker still cut: the dispose took 35.2 s and ended only when the commit failed. In loop run 12 the commit never returned within four minutes (no "checkpoint failed" line), the bus stayed Healthy throughout, and the outbox relay, which sends while the bus is Healthy, produced row 4 six times (each failing after the 30 s message timeout, backing off 2 s to 32 s) instead of waiting. So: the bus check is blind while an offset commit is in flight, the host's stop waits on that commit, and the relay produces and backs off instead of waiting, which can delay its resumption after the outage by up to its backoff (300 s at most).

- Diagnosis of run 34: a race in Testcontainers.Kafka 4.15.0's container start, not in the test or in Ariva. `KafkaBuilder` (decompiled; unchanged on the library's `develop` and `main` branches) starts the container with entrypoint `/bin/sh -c` and the command `while [ ! -f /testcontainers.sh ]; do sleep 0.1; done; /testcontainers.sh`, and its start callback copies the script in with `CopyAsync(..., "/testcontainers.sh", mode 755)` (a Docker archive upload). The loop sees the file as soon as it exists and executes it; when the copy still has it open for writing, `execve` fails with ETXTBSY ("Text file busy"), the shell exits 126, and the container stops before Kafka starts. A loaded machine widens that window. Every Kafka fixture of the integration suite starts its container the same way, so any of them can fail like this in a gate. Proposed, not applied (stop and report): give the fixtures' Kafka containers a command that never executes a file that may still be written, `while [ ! -s /testcontainers.sh ]; do sleep 0.1; done; /bin/sh /testcontainers.sh` (the shell reads the script instead of executing it, and waits for it to be non-empty; the script is a few hundred bytes written in one piece), in one shared builder used by `FaultsFixture` and the other Kafka fixtures, and report the race to Testcontainers; then the gates and the 50-run loop again.
- Round 5 (the coordinator's go-ahead, 2026-10-13), test infrastructure only: `Ariva.IntegrationTests/Setup/KafkaContainers.Builder()` builds every Kafka container of the suite with the suite's image (`confluentinc/cp-kafka:7.6.1`) and the start command `while [ ! -s /testcontainers.sh ]; do sleep 0.1; done; /bin/sh /testcontainers.sh` (the shell waits for a non-empty script and reads it instead of executing the file), with a comment naming the race and ARV-156. In Testcontainers 4.15.0 `WithCommand(params string[])` appends to the module's command, so the builder replaces it with `OverwriteEnumerable`. Every place the suite builds a Kafka container (a search for `KafkaBuilder`, `KafkaContainer`, `ContainerBuilder(`, `cp-kafka` and `apache/kafka` in Platform): `Messaging/KafkaPipelineTests.cs` (`KafkaFixture`, used by `KafkaPipelineTests`, `TopicProvisioningTests`, `AmanKafkaTests`, `QueueStreamTests`, `QueueStreamScaleOutTests` and `QueueStreamEventTimeTests`) and `Faults/FaultsFixture.cs`; both now use the builder. Not Testcontainers.Kafka, so not affected: `DatabaseChartBootstrapTests` (a database image), the Aspire AppHost (`AddKafka`), the CI and ZAP service containers and `docker-compose.dev.yml` (`apache/kafka:4.1.0`), and the E2E services script. Checked: `docker inspect` of both fixtures' containers during a run shows entrypoint `["/bin/sh","-c"]` and the new command, and `BrokerOutageTests` with `TopicProvisioningTests` passed (2 of 2).
- Ready-to-paste issue for Testcontainers (the owner's call to file; not filed). Title: "Kafka module: container exits 126 'Text file busy' when the start script is executed while it is still being copied". Version: Testcontainers.Kafka 4.15.0 (.NET; the same command on the `develop` and `main` branches), Docker on Linux, image `confluentinc/cp-kafka:7.6.1`. The start command: `KafkaBuilder.Init()` sets the entrypoint `/bin/sh -c` and the command `while [ ! -f /testcontainers.sh ]; do sleep 0.1; done; /testcontainers.sh`, and the start callback copies the script in with `CopyAsync(..., "/testcontainers.sh", fileMode: Unix.FileMode755)`. The race: the loop sees the file as soon as it exists and executes it; if the archive copy still holds it open for writing, `execve` fails with ETXTBSY, the container log shows "/bin/sh: /testcontainers.sh: Text file busy", the shell exits 126 and `StartAsync` throws `ContainerNotRunningException` ("exited with code 126"). Reproduction hint: start many Kafka containers in a loop on a loaded host (we saw it once in about 34 fixture starts beside 16 CPU-bound processes on 4 CPUs). Workaround: replace the command, `.WithCommand(new OverwriteEnumerable<string>(["while [ ! -s /testcontainers.sh ]; do sleep 0.1; done; /bin/sh /testcontainers.sh"]))`, so the shell reads the script instead of executing a file that may still be open for writing; a fix in the module could copy the script under another name and rename it into place, or use the same `/bin/sh` form.

Story gate `node scripts/verify.mjs story --integration "FullyQualifiedName~BrokerOutage|FullyQualifiedName~ValidationReaderLogin|FullyQualifiedName~ValidationEmulator"` (wide: FeedSettings.cs) PASS, exit 0: unit 5,228 of 5,228, integration 508 passed and 1 skipped of 509 (12 m 33 s; every Kafka fixture through the new builder), E2E 687 passed and 1 skipped (16.8 m), log scan 5 host logs, 96,197 lines, no credentials; every step PASS. `node scripts/security/scan.mjs --changed` PASS, exit 0 (9 files, 0 errors, 0 warnings). `node scripts/verify.mjs docs` PASS, exit 0.

With Proof 1 and 2 (50 of 50 for `ValidationReaderLoginTests` and `ValidationEmulatorTests` under the same load), each of the story's three tests has passed 50 times in a row on a loaded machine. Nothing left running: no hog, loop, diagnostic process or test container.

- Security review (security-reviewer; CWE-1333, CWE-918, availability): FAIL in round 1 on two documentation fixes (R1 the CWE-1333 row overstated the control; R2 four places said readiness fails on a broker outage), then PASS on rounds 2 to 5 (2026-10-13): the regex row lists every untimed rule with its reason (RedactionEnricher must stay untimed, since Serilog emits the event when an enricher throws); the readiness wording corrected with an open decision; the cut conditions bounded inside the budget and hiding nothing the test is for, the two races moved to ARV-165 and ARV-166 with deterministic reproductions required; the Kafka builder keeps everything the module sets and its command is a constant. `scan.mjs --changed` 9 files, 0 errors. The Testcontainers issue text above is for the owner to file or not.

## 2026-10-13 ARV-166 A produce queued behind an in-flight message on a dropped connection never completes (Phase 1, branch story/mvp-phase1)
- Started on 5bf7106 (tree clean). Passes true after the security review. Findings are written here as they come (the container has restarted twice recently).
- Cause (found 2026-10-13, 07:40 to 08:08 UTC; a temporary diagnostic test against the faults fixture, kept out of the tree, with the ARV-156 sequence: warm-up, `FaultsFixture.StallAsync`, produce A not awaited, `CutAsync` 2 s later, produce B 0.5 s after that, `RestoreAsync`, produce C): not librdkafka, not idempotence, not `message.timeout.ms`: MassTransit 8.5.11's rider destroys the producer that B is waiting on. (1) A plain Confluent.Kafka 2.15.1 producer with Ariva's own settings (`KafkaClientConfig.Producer`: idempotence, `Acks.All`, `MessageTimeoutMs` 30,000, `LingerMs` 0), never disposed: A ended "Local: Message timed out" after 38.8 s and B after 36.3 s, reported together (librdkafka debug: "timed out 2+0 message(s) (MsgId 2..3): message.timeout.ms exceeded", then the idempotent epoch-bump drain); C succeeded after the restore. So librdkafka reports a message queued behind one in flight on a dropped connection. (2) The same producer disposed 5 s after B (as below, no flush): A and B never completed, still pending 150 s after the dispose and 60 s after the restore. (3) Through `ISvcMessageBus` (MassTransit), twice: A ended after 30.9 s and 31.8 s; B was pending 90 s after the cut and 45 s after the restore; C succeeded in 0.5 s; the producer that had logged "Error (Local_AllBrokersDown) ... on producer: ariva-diag166#producer-4" every 2 to 10 s logged nothing after A's S-FAULT, and a cut after C was reported by `#producer-11`: a new librdkafka instance. The chain, read in the decompiled 8.5.11 assemblies: `KafkaTopicSendTransportContext.Send` runs the produce inside `PipeContextSupervisor<ProducerContext>.Send`, which on any exception from the pipe (a delivery failure, a local error, or a cancellation) calls `activeContext.Faulted`; `ActivePipeContext.Faulted` disposes the shared, cached producer context ("recreated whenever a fault is propagated to the usage"); `KafkaProducerContext.Dispose` flushes with a token linked to a source that its `OnError` handler cancelled at the first local error (here `Local_AllBrokersDown` at the cut), so the flush ends at once, and then disposes the Confluent producer; `Producer.Dispose` stops its poll thread and destroys the handle, and librdkafka silently discards outstanding messages on destroy (rdkafka.h: "you should call rd_kafka_flush() before you destroy the producer. Otherwise, any outstanding messages will be silently discarded"). B's delivery handler is never invoked, so its `TaskCompletionSource` never completes, and its `GCHandle` (with the key and value) is never freed. One producer per topic (the rider keeps one per registered topic), so any failed or cancelled produce on a topic strands every other produce outstanding on that topic at that moment, broker outage or not: a device push whose request is aborted cancels its produce and strands the other pushes on the same topic. In run 16 the relay's row 4 and the direct publish shared `ariva.sla.breach-detected.v1`. The librdkafka 2.15.1 and 2.16.0 changelogs and the Confluent.Kafka 2.16.0 changelog list nothing about undelivered reports; 2.16.0 reports `ALL_BROKERS_DOWN` once per outage instead of every re-bootstrap, which does not change the chain (one local error is enough).
- Why the ARV-156 timing reproduces only sometimes (found 2026-10-13 08:31 UTC): a message that has been sent is timed out by the broker's own thread, which scans its queue only when it wakes, and while the broker is unreachable it wakes at each reconnect attempt (librdkafka's `reconnect.backoff.max.ms`, 10 s). When the scan that times out the first message also catches the one queued behind it (both due within the same wake-up), both reports arrive before the rider disposes the producer and nothing is stranded: the new test with the ARV-156 timing (B 0.5 s after the cut) passed once on the unfixed code, the row, A and B all reported together 9 s after the row was due. In the two diagnostic runs through MassTransit the wake-ups (every 10 s, visible in the producer's "Local_AllBrokersDown" lines at 13.8, 23.8 and 33.8 s) fell between A's and B's deadlines, so B was stranded. Queuing B more than 10 s after the first message makes the strand certain: B is due after the first message's latest possible report. The test does that (B 12 s after the cut).
- Mechanism chosen (and why): the cause sits in MassTransit's rider, which Ariva cannot change, and the cause-level fix (one Ariva-owned Confluent producer per host for produces, the rider kept for consumers; never disposed on a failed send) would replace the implementation behind `ISvcMessageBus` and so contradict CLAUDE.md's messaging rule and ADR-0018's decision 1; it is recorded as an open decision for the owner (`docs/product/decisions.md`, ADR-0018 note of 2026-10-13). Within the rider: (1) `KafkaProduce` bounds every produce Ariva awaits (`SendTimeout` 45 s: librdkafka's 30 s message timeout, its reporting delay of up to the 10 s reconnect backoff, measured 30.6 to 39.5 s in ARV-156 and 36.3 to 39.1 s here, plus 5 s): the produce gets a token of its own that cancels at the bound (Confluent completes its await as cancelled, the rider releases it), and the wait itself ends at the bound (`WaitAsync`) even if a client ignores the token; a produce that runs out fails with `KafkaProduceTimeoutException` (a `TimeoutException`, so Ingest answers 503 and the relay ends its pass). (2) The caller's token never reaches the rider: before, a device dropping its request cancelled its produce, which made the rider dispose the topic's producer and strand every other push on that topic; now the caller stops waiting at once and its produce runs on to its report or its bound, observed in a continuation. (3) The relay's pass produces for at most `Kafka:Outbox:PassSeconds` (60 s): each send is awaited for what is left of it (a token and `WaitAsync`), and one that runs out is a broker failure (row failed with its backoff, key held back, pass committed). (4) The dead-letter retry loops (`DeadLetterFilter`, `UnreadableFilter`, through `DeadLetterRetry`) end on `MessagingShutdown`, signalled by `ApplicationStopping` and by the bus's `PreStop` (an `IBusObserver`, which also covers a host disposed without a stop, the ARV-156 case); the stream worker's dead-letter loop and `PartitionedConsumer` now take the worker's token instead of `CancellationToken.None`. (5) `dotnet.producer.delivery.report.fields` none: a stranded delivery handler stays pinned by its GCHandle for good, and without the key, value and headers it costs a few hundred bytes instead of up to 1 MB. No librdkafka setting changes the behaviour (the producer is destroyed; no setting stops the rider from doing that), so none was changed beyond (5).
- Cause verified by a second reading (2026-10-13, after the restart; ilspycmd on the net10.0 assemblies of MassTransit 8.5.11, MassTransit.Kafka 8.5.11 and Confluent.Kafka 2.15.1 in the NuGet cache). The caller's token reaches librdkafka's await: `TopicProducer<TKey,TValue>.Produce(key, value, pipe, cancellationToken)` passes it to `KafkaTopicSendTransportContext.Send(IPipe<ProducerContext>, cancellationToken)` and into `SendPipe`, whose `CreateContext` builds `KafkaMessageSendContext` with it, and `KafkaTopicSendTransportContext.Send(ProducerContext, KafkaSendContext)` calls `producerContext.Produce(topic, message, sendContext.CancellationToken)`, which is Confluent's `Producer.ProduceAsync`; there `cancellationToken.Register(() => handler.TrySetCanceled())` completes the await as cancelled and leaves the message queued in librdkafka. The cancellation then faults the shared producer: `PipeContextSupervisor<TContext>.Send` has `catch (Exception exception) { await activeContext.Faulted(exception); throw; }` with no filter, so an `OperationCanceledException` is a fault like any other; `ProducerContextFactory.CreateActiveContext` gives `SupervisorExtensions.AddActiveContext(supervisor, contextHandle, ...)`, an `ActivePipeContext<T>` over the cached handle, and `ActivePipeContext<T>.Faulted` is `await _contextHandle.DisposeAsync()`; the handle is the `AsyncPipeContextAgent<ProducerContext>` from `GetContext`, whose `DisposeAsync` is `PipeContextAgent<T>.DisposeAsync` (first call only: `_inactive.TrySetResult`), which disposes the `KafkaProducerContext`, and `GetContext` creates a new producer on the next send because the handle `IsDisposed`. `KafkaProducerContext.Dispose` flushes with a 30 s token linked to `_cancellationTokenSource`, which `OnError` cancels at the first local error (`error.IsLocalError`: a broker down, but also an idle disconnect reported as `Local_Transport`), so on such a producer the flush ends at once; then `Producer.Dispose` cancels the poll task (`callbackCts.Cancel(); callbackTask.Wait()`) and disposes the handle: no delivery report is served after that, so the `TypedTaskDeliveryHandlerShim` of every message still outstanding never completes (its `GCHandle` stays). Finding (2) holds: a caller's cancellation disposes the shared producer. Two details the first reading did not record: on a producer that has never seen a local error the dispose flushes for up to 30 s synchronously on the faulting send's thread (outstanding messages then get their reports, nothing is stranded); and a second fault on an already disposed handle is a no-op (`TrySetResult` false), so a stranded produce cancelled at Ariva's 45 s bound does not dispose the producer that replaced it. Ariva's own bound does cancel through the same path: when the 45 s token fires on a producer that is still live (librdkafka not having reported by then, which the measurements make rare: 30.6 to 39.5 s), that producer is disposed too; accepted, because every send stranded by it is bounded the same way. The token is what releases a stranded produce's async chain (rider and Confluent state machines, which hold the serialised message); `dotnet.producer.delivery.report.fields` none then leaves the pinned handler without the key and value, so (1) and (5) belong together.
- The uncommitted DeadLetterShutdownTests failed before the restart (scratchpad 166/after, 08:48 to 08:59: both cases, "the poison message delivered again and dead-lettered after the restart" not within 270 s). Cause, from its host log and the decompiled pipes: the loop's `OperationCanceledException` carried the linked token, not the message's, so MassTransit's `RetryFilter<TContext>` (it rethrows only when `ex.CancellationToken` is the context's) treated it as a failure and retried; with `RetryCount` 0 `ExponentialRetryPolicy.GetRetryInterval` threw `IndexOutOfRangeException` (R-RETRY then R-FAULT in the log), which the receive pipe's `RescueFilter` (configured by `ReceivePipeConfiguration.CreatePipe` with `Ignore<OperationCanceledException>()` only) sent to the error pipe, so the message completed and its offset was committed: lost, neither dead-lettered nor redelivered. An `OperationCanceledException` that escapes the pipe instead is not rescued: `ReceivePipeDispatcher.Dispatch` calls `receiveLock.Faulted`, and `KafkaReceiveLockContext` marks the offset's `PendingConfirmation` faulted, so it is not committed by itself. Fix: the loop ends with an `OperationCanceledException` that carries the message's own token.
- Proven with a temporary revert (the loop ending with its linked token again; `ConsumePipelineTests.Pipe_Should_LeaveTheMessageUnacknowledgedWithoutRetryOrFault_When_TheEndpointStopsWhileItsDeadLetterIsRefused` and the three `BoundedProduceTests` that check the token, 5 of 5 failed, then the fix restored): with `RetryCount` 0 the receive ended in `IndexOutOfRangeException` and a published `Fault<T>` (rescued: acknowledged, the message lost); with `RetryCount` 2 the receive did end as a cancellation (not acknowledged) but the retry filter's exhausted path (`RetryFaulted`, `NotifyPendingFaults`) published a `Fault<T>`. So the production default (3) kept the message but published a spurious fault, and a host configured without retries lost it. Residual, not fixable inside the rider: MassTransit's `BatchCheckpointer.ReadBatch` awaits each `PendingConfirmation` in offset order and, when one is faulted, checkpoints the ones before it and carries on, so a later record of the same partition (another key; `ConcurrentMessageLimit` 8) that completes and is checkpointed (every `CheckpointInterval`, 5 s) before `KafkaMessageConsumer.ActiveAndActualAgentsCompleted` cancels the checkpoint token commits past the record left unacknowledged at the stop. It needs the broker to take commits while refusing the dead letter (a partial outage, not a full one: then the commit fails too); the stop warning now names topic, partition and offset so that the record can be replayed. Before ARV-166 the same message kept the pod from stopping until it was killed, which kept it.
- Changes by the second engineer (2026-10-13, after the restart): (a) `DeadLetterRetry.SendUntilAcceptedAsync` takes the message's token and the stop token apart and always ends with an `OperationCanceledException` carrying the message's token; its two log lines name the partition (also the stream worker's), for a replay. (b) `OutboxRelay.SendWithinAsync` waits on the same linked token it gives the transport (`WaitAsync(linked.Token)`) instead of a second timer: with two timers due at the same moment the row's error was sometimes "The operation has timed out." (seen in a StuckProduceTests host log) instead of naming the pass bound. (c) StuckProduceTests: the host runs with `Kafka:Outbox:PassSeconds` 20, so the relay's pass is ended by its own bound against the real stuck produce (measured 20.2 s), checked from the committed row without a lock (the bus stayed Healthy through the cut, so the relay ran passes back to back, 09:26:00 to :20 and :20 to :40 in the host log, and a later pass relocked the row), and the leader lock is polled every 25 ms to catch the 200 ms gap between passes; new fact `Produce_Should_NotStrandTheOtherSends_When_ACallerGivesUp` (stall, a send outstanding, cut, wait for the producer's `Local_AllBrokersDown`, another caller gives up, restore): first run, the outstanding send completed after 11.2 s and both messages were consumed once, in order. (d) ConsumePipelineTests and BoundedProduceTests as above, plus a test that an abandoned produce's later failure raises no unobserved task exception (with a control that does). (e) BrokerOutageTests: the comments now say the race is bounded, not removed (ARV-167). (f) Docs: ADR-0018 note rewritten before commit and the owner's decision added (ARV-167); decisions.md records the owner's decision (the open row removed); overview, cwe-controls (CWE-400), asvs-l2 and wiki 10 corrected (the dead-letter stop, the checkpoint residual, which ARV-167 does not change, and what operators see).
- Found 2026-10-13 09:40 (DeadLetterShutdownTests with MassTransit at Debug, scratchpad 166/diag3): with the loop ending as the message's cancellation the record is no longer acknowledged (T-FAULT instead of R-FAULT, and offset 6 did come back), but the test still failed: a consumer of the first host, created after that host's bus had stopped and its provider was disposed, took the partition at the next rebalance and faulted the record on every assignment ("ObjectDisposedException: IServiceProvider" from `RiderRegistrationContext.GetService`, then the stop warning, since that host's `MessagingShutdown` was cancelled), so the restarted host never got it. Mechanism, read in MassTransit 8.5.11: at the cut `KafkaMessageConsumer.HandleKafkaError` cancels the consume loop on any local error ("Consume Loop Exited" at 09:40:38.765); the consumer completes only when its dispatch ends; `MessagingShutdown` (ApplicationStopping, or the bus's PreStop) ended the dispatch before the bus stop chose its endpoints, so the endpoint reported Completed ("Endpoint Completed" at 09:40:41.98) and `ReceiveEndpointCollection.StopEndpoints` skips it (it stops only endpoints whose `IsStarted()`: Started, Ready or Faulted), its `ReceiveTransportAgent` is never stopped, and its `Run` loop starts the transport again after its one second delay: a new Confluent consumer at 09:40:42.998, after "Bus stopped" at 09:40:42.17. Fix: the loops end on their own endpoint's `IReceiveEndpointObserver.Stopping`, which `ReceiveEndpoint.Stop` raises after the endpoint was chosen and before `TransportHandle.Stop`, so the transport agent is stopping before the dispatch can end and nothing restarts. `MessagingShutdown` (host lifetime and bus observer) is replaced by a per-endpoint signal connected in `ConsumePipeline.Configure`. Independent of ARV-166 and left as found: a Kafka endpoint whose consumer ended on a broker error and is between restarts when the bus stops is skipped the same way and restarts into a stopped host; in production the process exits right after the stop, so it lasts until then (the group then waits out the member's session).
- Verified on the real broker (2026-10-13 10:08, scratchpad 166/neg): `StuckProduceTests.Produce_Should_NotStrandTheOtherSends_When_ACallerGivesUp` with the caller's token linked into the produce again (a temporary build, source restored): the send outstanding when another caller gave up ended only at the bound, "after 45.0 s (KafkaProduceTimeoutException)", although the broker was back 2 s after the caller gave up; with the fix it completed after 11.0 s and both messages were consumed. This is finding (2) observed end to end: a caller's cancellation disposes the producer the other sends are on. DeadLetterShutdownTests (both cases) passed after the endpoint signal and a fix to the test's dead-letter reader (it stopped at its first empty 500 ms poll, which a fresh consumer's first fetch can exceed): the poison message was delivered again and dead-lettered after the restart at 51.5 s and 47.1 s (the restarted host waits out the old member's session, about 36 s, since the old host closed its consumer while the broker was still cut).
- Found while answering whether (2) keeps at-least-once (2026-10-13, read in `ZoneProcessor` and `QueueStateEngine.Interval`): nothing is lost (an abandoned produce runs on to its report or bound, and the device resends a push whose request it dropped, with the same batch id), but the duplicate is not dropped everywhere. The sensing archive drops it (`sensing_batch`), consumers behind the inbox drop theirs by event id, and the queue engine counts a repeated track entry or exit as a duplicate; it has no batch-id check, though, so a repeated interval-count batch is applied again (`Interval` tallies `In` and `Out` again). Pre-existing for any resend whose first produce reached the broker (docs/architecture/sensor-adapters.md says consumers drop a resent body, which holds for the archive, not for the engine), and more frequent with (2), since an abandoned produce now completes. Not fixed here: the coordinator made it ARV-168 (priority 37); the overview (Producing) and the ADR-0018 note point to it.
- Gates (2026-10-13, on fresh services, the tree as handed back): `node scripts/verify.mjs backend` exit 0 (PASS dotnet build, golden, unit tests, scanner self-test, security scan, base images, chart security, NuGet and npm audits); `node scripts/verify.mjs story --specs tests/api/device-push.spec.ts,tests/api/device-health.spec.ts,tests/api/aman-feed.spec.ts --integration "FullyQualifiedName~BrokerOutage|...|FullyQualifiedName~QueueStream"` exit 0, widened to the full suites (wide: Ariva.Infra Messaging and Streaming): unit 5,242 passed, integration 513 passed and 1 skipped, E2E 687 passed and 1 skipped; `node scripts/security/scan.mjs --changed` exit 0 (16 files, 0 errors, 0 warnings); `node scripts/verify.mjs docs` exit 0. The coordinator recorded the owner's decision in decisions.md (2026-10-13, point 1) and made the repeated interval-count batch ARV-168; this entry's decisions bullet refers to it.
- Tallies after the gates (2026-10-13 10:51 to 11:32, one process per run, 16 `yes` hogs, stopped afterwards): StuckProduceTests 10 of 10 passed (both facts each run, 121 to 128 s a run); BrokerOutageTests 10 of 10 passed (110 to 134 s). Passes stays false, until the security review.
- Security review (security-reviewer; CWE-400, with CWE-532 and at-least-once): PASS (2026-10-13) on code, tests and docs: every awaited produce goes through BoundedAsync; the relay cannot hold its transaction, lock or rows past PassSeconds; Ingest answers 503 with Retry-After within the bound; the dead-letter loops end at a stop without acknowledging; nothing of a message is logged. `scan.mjs --changed` 16 files, 0 errors. Conditions met by the lead: C1, residual (a) is new reachability made by this change (before it the stop hung and nothing was lost), so the owner decided on 2026-10-13 to accept it for ARV-166 and close it before the pilot in ARV-171 (a refused dead letter written durably first); C2, ADR-0018's note now says so. Also: residual (b), the stray consumer, folded into ARV-165; the producer queue bound for ARV-167; the review's test recommendations in ARV-171; the 'Decided' cells in decisions.md punctuated.
